# GLM review 2026-11-02 (glm-5.3): 396 findings, 206 high severity, 12 shard(s)

Revision 30d4503a729c. Shards: agentbox#2, agentbox#3, dreamlab-ai-website, knowledgegraph, nostr-rust-forum, ontomark, sidestr-rs, solid-pod-rs, visionclaw, visionclaw#2, visionclaw#3, visiongraph. Unverified hypotheses: reproduce each with a failing test or check (build-with-quality) before acting.

## agentbox#2 / critical

### F-01 — The root operator signing key is ambient and readable in every agent process in the shipped default
- Topics: AB-16.1, AB-16.5, AB-16.7, AB-16.13
- Evidence: AB-16.1 N4: role_isolation OFF is "the shipped default — a same-uid boundary. Every supervised program, MCP server, skill and agent runs as devuser, so each can read every other's 0600 key file and the mirror key, and uid 1000 is admitted to the session socket by design." AB-16.13: with the flag off "every other ROLE variable stays in PID 1 and reaches every child" (entrypoint-unified.sh:432, :476); AB-16.5: operatorKeyHex returns "first non-empty of AGENTBOX_PRIVKEY_HEX, AGENTBOX_BRIDGE_SK, OPERATOR_NOSTR_PRIVKEY" (operator-key.cjs:58) and the gateway's default identity signs AS the operator with the root key (gateway.cjs:155). AB-16.7: with the flag off a co-resident process reads the key file (SUCCEEDS), reads the bridge key (SUCCEEDS) and is ADMITTED to the session socket (session-plane-acl.json:8-11).
- Failure: any compromised or misbehaving agent, skill or MCP server exfiltrates the root operator Nostr key from env or disk and can then sign as the operator — issue commands to the nostr-gateway (only the commander pubkey is honoured, gateway.cjs:151,157), publish governance events, and drive every tmux session through the socket.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE, AB-16.1 N4; AB-16.7 note)

### F-02 — The break-glass bearer is the same value as BRIDGE_TOKEN while its lifecycle controls are proposed-only
- Topics: AB-16.6, AB-16.9, AB-12.3, AB-12.7
- Evidence: AB-16.9 C3: the proxy break-glass bearer is "still the same value as BRIDGE_TOKEN until Q4" (proxy.mjs:53, role-accounts.json:46-53). AB-12.7: the Unmute voice backend authenticates to tab0-bridge with "Bearer BRIDGE_TOKEN aka KYUTAI_LLM_API_KEY" (voice/README.md:93), and AB-12.3 shows that token guarding every bridge surface. AB-16.6: unset expiry "permits unbounded lifetime", unset scope "is unrestricted", the sentinel pubkey makes "every downstream attribution for this request a placeholder", and "ADR-2027 record status: decision proposed, implementation none, activation inactive"; the OPEN note adds verified_paths is empty, so the custody claims are "attested by reading, not by a gate".
- Failure: anyone who legitimately holds the voice-loop LLM credential (any container on the shared network, any leaked compose env) also holds a full NIP-98 identity bypass on LAN port 9096 with no expiry bound and placeholder attribution; leaking one config value compromises both surfaces at once.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE and OPEN in AB-16.6; note in AB-16.9 C3)

### F-03 — Only one of nine agent side-effect surfaces is governed by the action pipeline
- Topics: AB-14.1, AB-14.14
- Evidence: AB-14.1 DIVERGENCE1: GOVERNANCE-capabilities.md:339-342 names "no single policy decision point"; "every OTHER surface above (direct tool call, code-mode, ACI shell, consultant, dream, beads, alt harness) still reaches EXEC with no interceptor"; the dream engine "RECORDS each side effect … but nothing there approves or denies" (exec-record.js:63). AB-14.14: exactly one surface, POST /v1/tasks, is wired (action-plane.js:115), and task-spawn is deliberately FAST_PATH 'local' with no approval receipt.
- Failure: an agent performing a mutating, egress or spend action through any path other than POST /v1/tasks does so with no approval gate, no capability token and no journal entry — the governance and audit trail the docs describe does not exist for those paths, so an incident investigation finds nothing.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE, "TOP OPEN RISK")

### F-04 — management-api's Nostr consumer processes events whose signatures it has not verified
- Topics: AB-13.7, AB-14.6
- Evidence: "DRIFT at this declared revision: verifier exceptions accept id/sig string shape. This does not establish I01 authenticity, relay-consumer.js:743,756." The same consumer solely implements "ACSP governance 31400-31405, agent-intent 38000+, payments 38200/38201, the outbox publisher and external fanout" (AB-13.1 N1); on a 31403 it calls governanceDecisionSink.notify (server.js:1425, relay-consumer.js:657); with AGENTBOX_INTENT_COMMAND set the intent spec returns a command to execute (default-intent-spec.js:71-88).
- Failure: a forged kind-38000 intent or kind-38200 payment event from an impersonating publisher is written to the inbox, drives intent markers and payment projections, or dispatches a configured command; the authority gate re-verifies before release (AB-14.6), but inbox state and intent/payment handling are poisoned without any signature ever being checked.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DRIFT; repair deferred to AB-40.1)

### F-05 — Dream apply-repair sends un-redacted failing hunks, including key material, to the default vendor LLM
- Topics: AB-23.5, AB-23.15, AB-16.14
- Evidence: AB-23.5: "the failing-hunk excerpt goes straight from read_blob to the repair prompt without the source-stage denylist or key-material withholding (source.rs:1320-1336)"; "the execution review reproduces this with harmless canaries and a capturing model stub." AB-23.15: the night provider "defaults to zai" (agentbox.toml:2585, config.rs:184) — an external vendor. AB-16.14 records spend-capable keys living on workspace binds (agentbox.toml:1926).
- Failure: on any night where a patch fails to apply in a repository containing secrets, the failing hunk — potentially a private key or token — is posted verbatim to Z.AI's API in the repair prompt.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no formal marker; stated as an audit-verified limit in AB-23.5 prose

### F-06 — Voice/cockpit ports 8444 and 8443 are published 0.0.0.0 outside the one sanctioned, CI-gated LAN door
- Topics: AB-12.7, AB-12.3, AB-12.9
- Evidence: "DIVERGENCE — port 8444 and port 8443 are published 0.0.0.0 by docker-compose.voice.yml:40-41, while only port 9096 is the ADR-045 D2 sanctioned NIP-98-gated LAN door covered by the loopback CI gate." Only /approvals/* is proxied through the NIP-98 gate; /embed* and /api/* are reverse-proxied directly to the Unmute frontend/backend (voice/console/Caddyfile), whose authentication the material does not describe, and the Unmute backend holds the bridge bearer (voice/README.md:93).
- Failure: a LAN actor reaches the Unmute realtime loop on 8444, drives a voice turn, and through it the bridge's /v1/chat/completions, whose tool allowlist includes tmux and session-control send patterns (AB-12.7) — command injection into agent sessions from outside the audited ingress boundary with no NIP-98 in the path.
- Confidence: medium (the publish and proxy split are stated; auth on /embed and /api is not)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE in AB-12.7)

### F-07 — Two panels contradict on whether the live mirror redacts before egress, and on ADR-2026's status
- Topics: AB-16.11 vs AB-13.9, AB-13.17
- Evidence: AB-16.11 draws the mirror as "composes UNREDACTED selected text" then gift-wraps to the cloud relay, and calls ADR-2026 "proposed / partial / INACTIVE for the complete egress policy." AB-13.9, at the same verified_commit, shows redactForEgress on the path with a fail-closed skip (egress-policy.cjs:118, nostr-live-mirror.cjs:453-457), and AB-13.17 says "RESOLVED ADR-2026 — the mirror and the kind-30840 digest now share ONE policy contract: … mandatory redaction."
- Failure: if AB-16.11 describes the deployed hook, session content leaves the box unredacted (sealed only to a derived child key) to a public cloud worker relay; if AB-13.9 does, AB-16.11's entire custody analysis is false. Either way one panel's security claim is wrong and nothing in the material says which.
- Confidence: high that the contradiction exists; medium on which panel matches production
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: partially — both sides carry DIVERGENCE markers, but the inter-panel conflict is nowhere reconciled

### F-08 — Malformed zone metadata silently downgrades an encrypted-zone digest to plaintext
- Topics: AB-23.24
- Evidence: "Malformed or absent zones become empty; unknown section can select first configured zone (zone_crypto.rs:86-105)"; "No encrypted match means plaintext plan (zone_crypto.rs:222-224)"; "Malformed metadata produces plaintext selection even with the gate on"; additionally "latest means latest locally held" epoch and the post-publish read-back "checks ID only" (digest.rs:468-477).
- Failure: a typo in section/zone config causes digest text that policy required to be zone-encrypted to be signed and published plaintext to the relay, and the ID-only read-back still reports success — a silent confidentiality breach with no error anywhere.
- Confidence: medium (source path reproduced in isolation; the probe did not publish)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Privacy note in AB-23.24)

### F-09 — Irreversible sats payments never reach the authority gate and spend lives in three unreconciled ledgers
- Topics: AB-15.6, AB-14.11
- Evidence: "the authority classification table names payment_settlement zero-tolerance as an irreversible above-threshold sats spend (agentbox.toml:1405), but no production path passes that actionClass to the gate. The only call sites are tests/sovereign/authority.test.js:129 and :248, and routes/payments.js never reaches management-api/lib/authority.js:226." And: "spend is accounted in three stores that never reconcile: the flock-guarded cost_cap ledger file (ledger.rs:75), the pod-held sats balance behind GET /v1/pay/balance (payments.js:205), and the marketplace token_budget (llm-marketplace.js:85). No surface sums them."
- Failure: a bug or prompt-injected agent drains sats through /v1/pay/buy or a 402 settlement with no approval and no zero-tolerance gate, and after the fact no single number answers what was spent.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DEBT ×2 in AB-15.6; DEBT in AB-14.11)

### F-10 — The annexe retention sweep can delete active nights and hides its own errors
- Topics: AB-23.18
- Evidence: "Shell ends with true; find and deletion errors hidden (engine.rs:809)"; "The exact retention command deleted a disposable old parent directory containing a freshly written child. It checks neither active jobs nor night locks"; "a failing find command still returned success with no stderr."
- Failure: during the nightly window the sweep deletes a directory an in-flight or just-started night is using on the annexe host; the night fails with a misleading environmental error, nothing in the logs names the deletion, and the remote work is gone.
- Confidence: high (fixture-reproduced)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no formal marker; stated as an audit-verified limit in AB-23.18 prose

### F-11 — The BC20 URN mapping store is the only route back for content-addressed identities and has no recovery receipt
- Topics: AB-17.4, AB-17.5, AB-17.6
- Evidence: "Where the VisionClaw kind is content-addressed (execution, kg) the local is a FRESH sha256-12 and the original urn:agentbox identity is recovered only from the durable UrnMapping store — lose the store, lose the crossing"; the store is one JSONL file (BC20_URN_MAPPING_PATH or /var/lib/agentbox/code-harness/bc20-urn-mappings.jsonl); "roundTrips() proves the algebra against a FRESH IN-MEMORY store … not a durability or recovery proof"; a missing row makes the inbound crossing "unrecoverable".
- Failure: that single JSONL file is truncated, corrupted or omitted from backups; every historical execution and kg crossing becomes permanently one-way — VisionClaw identifiers can no longer be resolved to their agentbox provenance, silently breaking the bidirectional-provenance contract.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE, PROTOCOL-registry rows open)

### F-12 — A gate-accepted candidate is published as a branch name, not the evaluated commit
- Topics: AB-23.6, AB-23.4
- Evidence: "the PR request carries a branch name, not the verified commit hash (seams.rs:341). A successful create command returns Some even for empty stdout (seams.rs:531)"; "The tested command boundary does not validate the URL or bind the pushed head to the evaluated tree." Compounding: "an evaluator changing tracked files after the candidate tree was recorded" and formatting "not a confinement boundary" (AB-23.4).
- Failure: between evaluation and push the branch head moves (a second process, a stray evaluator write); the human merges a PR whose content was never judged by the gate while the ledger records an ACCEPT for a different tree.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Open, publication identity)

### F-13 — The dream journal's at-most-once guarantee does not survive power loss
- Topics: AB-23.7, AB-23.1
- Evidence: "The writer does not synchronise file or directory contents (manifest.rs:328)"; the reset helper is "several link/unlink operations with best-effort rollback (runstate.rs:466)"; AB-23.1: "Persisted suppresses a repeat after a process crash; it neither confirms a draft was delivered nor supplies a power-loss transaction."
- Failure: power loss after the forge push but before the journal rename reaches disk: on reboot the night looks unfinished, the accepted candidate is re-run and a second draft PR is published for the same change; conversely a lost verdict leaves the ledger claiming a publication that never happened.
- Confidence: medium (power-loss window inferred from the stated absence of synchronisation)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no formal marker; stated as an audit-verified limit in AB-23.7 prose

### F-14 — Concurrent journal appends can share a seq, and dedup returns the old envelope without comparing payloads — contradicting the stated invariant
- Topics: AB-23.23 vs AB-14.7, AB-14.8
- Evidence: AB-14.7 asserts "session_urn + seq is the journal key, contiguous from 0, assigned HERE, never by the caller" (execution-journal.js:16-18). AB-23.23, for the same lines 152-185: "Concurrent appends can share seq; restart needs hydration", and "Caller key becomes deterministic event ID — Duplicate ID returns prior envelope, without payload comparison (exec-record.js:52-54)."
- Failure: two concurrent effects in one session journal under the same (session_urn, seq); replay, hydrate and the seq-watermark projections (AB-14.8) mis-order or silently skip one. Separately, a retried exec/record whose payload differs gets back the first envelope and the caller proceeds believing the new effect was journaled — the audit trail records something that did not happen.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: the behaviours are stated in AB-23.23's audit note, but the contradiction with AB-14.7's contiguity invariant carries no marker

### F-15 — The manifest, the decision records and the security docs describe a different system from the one running, in both directions at once
- Topics: AB-15.15, AB-20.3, AB-21.4, AB-24.6, AB-20.13, AB-12.15
- Evidence: AB-15.15: SECURITY-profiles.md:96 records consult_advice as "STAGED for rebuild, local-only" while the 2026-10-30 amendment leaves local_only empty, so "once the new image boots … consult advice takes the proxy." AB-20.3 and AB-21.4: memory rerank and trajectory classify are "configured, takes effect at the next image activation." AB-24.6: LOOM_JEV reaches the agentbox proxy "live when the loom service is recreated and the new agentbox image is activated." AB-20.13: ADR-2082 moved staged→live only after a live tools/list. AB-12.15 Tension: the manifest sets [voicelink] enabled = true and record_events = true (agentbox.toml:2226,2233) while ADR-2151 records the slot as "proposed, implementation none, inactive" (ADR-2151:5-7) and the path "bypasses tab0-bridge and the Unmute loop" over ssh stdio.
- Failure: the next routine rebuild silently activates cloud-proxy defaults the security docs promised were local-only, and activates an ssh-stdio voice ingress no decision record accepts; until then, every runbook and assurance claim read from the manifest or SECURITY-profiles describes a system that does not exist yet.
- Confidence: high on the divergence; medium (inferred) on the voicelink path's live behaviour
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: partially — each instance carries a marker (Open / staged / Tension); the systemic pattern does not

## Not judgeable from this material

1. **Which source the running containers actually run.** Multiple panels say changes are "staged until a rebuild" or "the active container has not been rebuilt with this source change" (AB-16.12, AB-20.13, AB-24.13); I need image digests, build receipts and a live /v1/system plus boot-log comparison against verified_commit 6500582dfae23bf8a38a6942916d208154e43625 before any finding that turns on "deployed" versus "source".
2. **Production values of the custody-relevant configuration**: whether BREAK_GLASS is set and with what expiry/scope, whether it really equals BRIDGE_TOKEN today, the AGENTBOX_EGRESS and per-path switch states, and the mirror recipient enumeration — none of these deployment values appear, and F-02 and F-07 both turn on them.
3. **Authentication on the published voice surfaces** (/embed* and /api/* behind port 8444/8443) — required to size F-06; the material describes the Caddy routing but not the auth posture of the Unmute frontend/backend.
4. **Whether any secret backup has actually been taken and restored**: the backup tool's tests are synthetic (SelfTest), the zip path is described as unencrypted and unverified, and no custodian has accepted any register row (AB-16.8, AB-16.9, AB-16.12).
5. **Live behavioural evidence beyond fixtures**: current recall-harness receipts for the running index, a real gateway command round trip, and which relay admission binary is actually booted — the audits reproduce behaviour with fixture services and cannot attest the running fleet.

## agentbox#2 / premortem

# Post-mortem — reconstructed from the estate corpus

The incident is not recorded in this corpus; what follows is the three failure chains this material most directly supports, ranked, then fifteen latent defects found while tracing them. Every claim carries the citation the corpus gives. Where a step joins two documented facts into a narrative the material does not itself assert, confidence is marked inferred. Nothing here has been verified against running code.

---

### R-1 — Shipped same-UID custody boundary: any agent process can read every signing key and drive every session
- Topics: AB-16.1, AB-16.5, AB-16.7, AB-16.13, AB-14.14, AB-17.2, AB-13.3, AB-16.8
- Chain of events:
  1. `role_isolation` ships off. Every supervised program, MCP server, skill and agent runs as `devuser` uid 1000 (AB-16.1 N4).
  2. A compromised or prompt-injected agent turn runs as devuser. It reads the bridge key at `/run/secrets/nostr.key` (written `0400 devuser` with the flag off, `config/entrypoint-unified.sh:1111-1120`), per-agent DID key files at 0600, the Z.AI key (DEVUSER_CLASS, ambient in every agent process, `config/custody/env-classes.json:90,227-230`), the mirror child secret (`services/nostr-pod-bridge/src/identity_port/port.rs:635-637`), and — via R-3's placement — the faucet treasury key.
  3. It connects to the session-plane socket; uid 1000 is in `allowed_uids` by design (`config/custody/session-plane-acl.json:8-11`) and issues `op send` against any slug, injecting instructions into other sessions including the coordinator.
  4. Over loopback it forges `X-Agentbox-Pubkey` and stamps any `did:nostr` into journalled `agent_did` (`management-api/lib/action-plane.js:222-233`) or emits agent-events under the container identity.
  5. It signs events under the sovereign identity. There is no rotation or revocation: ADR-2027 is proposed/none/inactive (AB-16.8), and relay allowlist revocation requires a full nix rebuild — a one build-deploy cycle compromise window (AB-13.3, AB-16.8 note).
- Evidence: AB-16.7 draws the exact reads as `SUCCEEDS` for a co-resident devuser peer against key file, socket and bridge key; AB-16.1 N4 states the same-uid boundary in one sentence; AB-14.14 and AB-17.2 both carry the same-UID debt note; AB-16.5 records that the gateway's default identity holds the ROOT operator key and the port hands devuser the mirror child secret.
- Warning signs visible today: the custody register has zero confirmed custodians, cadences or incident windows (AB-16.9, every row "UNCONFIRMED"); the W3b consumer cutover is unwired so the isolation flag "must stay off until the owner's boot rehearsal" (AB-16.13, ADR-2122:244-245) — an availability pressure that keeps the weak posture the default; seccomp is allow-by-default and explicitly not the confinement boundary (AB-16.2).
- Confidence: high — every link is stated; only the exploit narrative is inferred.
- Severity: high

### R-2 — Client session content leaves custody by default: unredacted mirror to a public relay, Z.AI as the dream default, plaintext digest fallback
- Topics: AB-16.11, AB-13.9, AB-13.17, AB-13.10, AB-16.5, AB-23.15, AB-23.24, AB-24.14
- Chain of events:
  1. Client work runs in sessions on the box. The `nostr-live-mirror` hook fires on Stop by default, composes UNREDACTED selected text, gift-wraps it and publishes to the cloud worker relay `wss://dreamlab-nostr-relay...workers.dev` — the only non-LAN egress in this domain (AB-16.11, AB-13.9).
  2. The wrap is sealed to the mirror child key — whose secret is devuser-readable (AB-16.5, `port.rs:635-637`), so any co-resident process (R-1) can decrypt the thread; rotation is "bump the env tag" with no revocation of the previously derived child at any relay (AB-16.5, ADR-2027 divergence).
  3. Independently, nightly dream reports over repo content go to Z.AI because it is the default provider; the egress register row `zai-night` carries marker `no-accepted-record` — "nothing accepts the corpus leaving" (`config/egress-policy.json:346-361`). Selecting Loom does not by itself confine to LAN: the fallback policy sends to Zai whenever a key exists (AB-23.15).
  4. The kind-30840 digest path can publish plaintext: with the gate on, malformed or absent zone metadata selects a plaintext plan, and an unknown section can select the first configured zone (`services/dream-engine/src/zone_crypto.rs:86-105,221-224`).
  5. Client-confidential material surfaces at a vendor or on the relay; the contractual breach costs the client.
- Evidence: AB-16.11 states the mirror composes unredacted text and that the mirror's off switch "fail-open on any publish error"; the same diagram concludes "there is no single answer to 'is session content leaving this box, and redacted how'". AB-23.24's Privacy paragraph states the plaintext-selection behavior outright.
- Warning signs visible today: the register itself records that no accepted record covers the Z.AI path; the mirror and digest share a policy contract but not recipient semantics (AB-13.17 note); the ADR-2066/2026 shared egress contract is marked proposed/partial/inactive (AB-16.11 X).
- Confidence: high for the documented posture; the composite incident is inferred.
- Severity: high

### R-3 — Faucet treasury key on a host-visible workspace bind plus a permissive-only SpendPolicy
- Topics: AB-16.13, AB-16.9, AB-15 (spend-gating drift note)
- Chain of events:
  1. The sidechain faucet's treasury signing key is read from `/home/devuser/workspace/sidestr/agents/treasury.key` (`agentbox.toml:1926`), which is also the schema default when the key is empty — a spend-capable key on a host-visible bind, outside the `agentbox-secrets` volume the custody model inventories (AB-16.13 Tension).
  2. The 2026-10 narrowing only repoints the role table: migrate copies the legacy workspace twin once and never deletes it, and with the flag off the manifest still points the faucet at the bind (ADR-2122:232-235; `role-accounts.json:78`).
  3. Every devuser process can read it (R-1).
  4. `sidestr-wallet` consults a `SpendPolicy` before every signature but ships only a permissive implementation; the authority gate is left to the caller, "which nothing in this topic yet supplies" (AB-15 drift note, see AB-33.7).
  5. An autonomous or injected run signs spends with no cap, no approval surface and no journal; client or operator funds move.
- Evidence: the Tension paragraph in AB-16.13 names the bind, the default and the never-deleted twin; the AB-15 drift note names the permissive policy and the missing caller-side gate in the same breath as the three unreconciled spend stores.
- Warning signs visible today: the manifest default resolves to the bind when the key is empty; the custody register row for the backup artefact and every other row is unconfirmed (AB-16.9); the spend-accounting debt (three stores, no reconciliation, AB-15.6 note) means a drain would also be slow to detect.
- Confidence: high for both facts (key placement, permissive policy); the exploitation path is inferred.
- Severity: high

---

### F-01 — Relay consumer accepts events without established signature authenticity
- Topics: AB-13.7, AB-13.1, AB-14.6
- Evidence: "verifier exceptions accept id/sig string shape. This does not establish I01 authenticity" (`mcp/nostr-bridge/relay-consumer.js:743,756`), while this same consumer is the sole JS implementer of ACSP governance 31400-31405, agent-intent 38000+, payments 38200/38201 and the inbox writer path (AB-13.1 N1). The authority gate does re-verify before release (AB-14.6), which contains forged 31403s at the gate but not elsewhere.
- Failure: a forged agent-intent, payment or governance event with non-verifiable signature is persisted to `pods/*/events/inbox/` and consumed downstream as if authentic.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift)
- Severity: high

### F-02 — tab0-bridge fail-open fallback can double-submit an instruction
- Topics: AB-12.5, AB-12.15
- Evidence: on a sessionSend throw the bridge re-sends via `tmux send-keys` (`config/tab0-bridge/server.mjs:162-168`, marked FAIL-OPEN ADR-044 D3), while the lifecycle audit states "send can return failure after submission; prior submission may be unknown" (AB-12.15, `plane.rs:411,415`).
- Failure: a socket timeout after the plane already typed the text causes a second injection; an instruction to a live agent executes twice.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: no
- Severity: high

### F-03 — Annexe retention sweep can delete an active night's data
- Topics: AB-23.18
- Evidence: "The exact retention command deleted a disposable old parent directory containing a freshly written child. It checks neither active jobs nor night locks, and a failing find command still returned success with no stderr" (`services/dream-engine/src/engine.rs:806-810`).
- Failure: one repo-night's sweep removes another night's freshly created working directory mid-run; the affected night returns a harness fault or evaluates against missing input.
- Confidence: high (reproduced on fixtures per the audit)
- Marked by authors: no
- Severity: high

### F-04 — Break-glass bearer lifecycle is unbounded and shares a value with BRIDGE_TOKEN
- Topics: AB-16.6, AB-16.9
- Evidence: "unset expiry permits unbounded lifetime"; "unset scope is unrestricted"; durable per-use receipt unproven; ADR-2027 sets `verified_paths` empty and record status proposed/none/inactive (`config/nip98-proxy/proxy.mjs:53,533-567`); the register row states the bearer is "still the same value as BRIDGE_TOKEN until Q4".
- Failure: the token leaks once (it is env-carried and devuser-readable); the holder retains a full identity bypass on port 9096 and the bridge gate indefinitely, with no revocation procedure.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Open)
- Severity: high

### F-05 — nostr-gateway defaults to signing as the ROOT operator key
- Topics: AB-16.5
- Evidence: "the DEFAULT identity is 'operator', so out of the box the gateway holds and signs with the ROOT operator key. The derived-child mode exists but is opt-in via env" (`config/nostr-gateway/gateway.cjs:154-155`).
- Failure: compromise of the devuser-run gateway process (trivial under R-1) yields the root operator key, not a derived child, and with it the adminPub command channel.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Divergence)
- Severity: high

### F-06 — Digest zone fallback can publish plaintext with the encryption gate on
- Topics: AB-23.24, AB-13.10
- Evidence: "Malformed or absent zones become empty; unknown section can select first configured zone" (`zone_crypto.rs:86-105`); "Malformed metadata produces plaintext selection even with the gate on" and the no-key refusal applies "only after zone policy identifies an encrypted zone" (`zone_crypto.rs:221-224`).
- Failure: a metadata typo in one section routes a nightly digest — summary, actions, actionable questions over session content — to the relay unencrypted while operators believe zone encryption is enforced.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: no
- Severity: high

### F-07 — The email raw-access capability token is a public value
- Topics: AB-16.10
- Evidence: the invariant states the Nostr public key is "the publishable half… Passing it as a tool argument is the intended design" (`skills/email-search/SKILL.md`); the gate is `is nostr_pubkey on PRIVILEGED_NOSTR_PUBKEYS`, accepting bare 64-hex.
- Failure: the operator's npub is published (forum, mirrors); anyone holding the LAN bearer token and that public string receives verbatim mail from both Proton accounts, bypassing the sanitised tier.
- Confidence: inferred — the design is documented as intended; the weakness follows from pubkeys being public.
- Marked by authors: no
- Severity: high

### F-08 — Session-plane dirty gate fails open on Git errors
- Topics: AB-12.15
- Evidence: "the dirty gate uses the desired directory and fails open on Git errors" — "Git errors become zero changes" (`services/agentbox-sessions/src/plane.rs:325`), feeding the create/recreate planner.
- Failure: a transient git failure makes a worktree holding uncommitted client work read as clean; reconcile recreates the pane and the work is lost.
- Confidence: medium
- Marked by authors: no
- Severity: high

### F-09 — Voice compose publishes unsanctioned 0.0.0.0 ports
- Topics: AB-12.7
- Evidence: "port 8444 and port 8443 are published 0.0.0.0 by docker-compose.voice.yml:40-41, while only port 9096 is the ADR-045 D2 sanctioned NIP-98-gated LAN door covered by the loopback CI gate."
- Failure: an attack surface the CI gate was written to cover exists outside it; the cockpit and Unmute frontend are reachable from the LAN with no gate parity check.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Divergence)
- Severity: medium

### F-10 — Every agent side-effect surface except POST /v1/tasks bypasses the action pipeline
- Topics: AB-14.1, AB-14.2
- Evidence: named "TOP OPEN RISK": direct tool call, code-mode, ACI shell, consultant, dream, beads and alt-harness "still reaches EXEC with no interceptor"; the dream engine "records only, never polices" via `POST /v1/exec/record` (ADR-2071 Phase 1).
- Failure: an injected consultant sub-call or dream-night effect performs an egress or spend with no approval, no capability token and no journalled deny — the pipeline's contract tests pass while real traffic never enters it (AB-14.2 note).
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Divergence)
- Severity: high

### F-11 — System One consumer records say local-only while `local_only` is empty
- Topics: AB-15.15, AB-14.5, AB-22.2
- Evidence: SECURITY-profiles records consult advice as "STAGED for rebuild, local-only, with no measured uplift claimed… which the 2026-10-30 amendment no longer makes true because `local_only` is empty" (`agentbox.toml:909`, `system_one.rs:522-577`); the same projection applies to approval review (`agentbox.toml:908`) and trajectory classify.
- Failure: after the rebuild the pending-approval queue, memory rerank shortlists and consultant questions flow to the vendor-backed proxy while the governing doc still describes a local-only posture.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Open)
- Severity: medium

### F-12 — EXP-B8 tick takes unjournalled outward actions
- Topics: AB-14.15
- Evidence: the stopping rule fires "a signed forum post (`scripts/experiments/exp-b8-label-log.cjs:550-563`) and a pull request opened with `gh` (`:573-606`), and records neither through `POST /v1/exec/record` or the authority journal."
- Failure: an unattended experiment tick posts and opens PRs outside the governance journal ADR-2071 placed around the dream night, with no record an auditor can replay.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Open)
- Severity: medium

### F-13 — Draft PR publication is not bound to the evaluated tree
- Topics: AB-23.6
- Evidence: "the PR request carries a branch name, not the verified commit hash (`dream-core/src/seams.rs:341`). A successful create command returns Some even for empty stdout (`:531`)" and the adapter "selects the acceptance notice by Option presence."
- Failure: a draft is opened with no URL and still reported as accepted; a human merges a branch head that was never the tree the gate evaluated (compounded by the frozen-A-ships-B dispatch drift, AB-23.11).

### F-14 — Session-plane picker targets the most-active client, not the invoking one
- Topics: AB-12.15
- Evidence: "the picker chooses a client by activity rather than proving invoking-client identity" (`services/agentbox-sessions/src/pick.rs:17,24-26`), then switches that client explicitly (`:71`).

### F-15 — BC20 UrnMapping store loss is unrecoverable identity loss
- Topics: AB-17.4, AB-17.6

Verification notes for the checking engineer: F-01's containment claim depends on the gate-side re-verification drawn in AB-14.6 — confirm the waiter path cannot be satisfied by consumer-written state alone. F-02 depends on whether the session-plane send can in fact error after typing (AB-12.15's lifecycle audit asserts it; the diagram alone does not). F-03, F-06 and F-08 rest on the linked estate-review probes, not on diagrams; the corpus presents them as reproduced on fixtures. R-2's composite and F-07 are the only chains that go beyond assembled facts into narrative.

## agentbox#3 / critical

### F-01 — Every secret in the container is readable by every process today: role isolation is designed but switched off
- Topics: AB-36, AB-34.6
- Evidence: AB-36's own summary: "every secret in the container reached every process… every long-running program ran as devuser (uid 1000), so a 0600 key file protected nothing from a co-resident agent (AB-16.1, AB-16.7)"; `[security].role_isolation` "ships **false**" (`agentbox.toml:2506`), activation only on a flag-on rehearsal receipt that does not exist (the only committed receipt is STAGED with flag false, `docs/estate-closeout/x1-rehearsal-20261003T090708Z.json:4,8`). Off-mode chain keys are read straight from at-rest paths by devuser (`run-producer.sh:70-71`, `run-faucet.sh:27`). Compounding: the flag-off build bakes devuser into `wheel` with a `NOPASSWD: ALL` drop-in (`flake.nix:4045`, `config/bake-devuser-privilege.sh:44-47`), inert only because `no-new-privileges` is set (`docker-compose.yml:129`).
- Failure: any compromised or prompt-injected MCP tool, hook or skill running as devuser reads the sovereign identity key, the forum house key, the settlement signer and faucet treasury keys, and can sign or spend with them; reverting either container-level guard on a flag-off build restores devuser→root past every boundary in AB-36.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Open — flag ships off, activation gated on absent receipt; sudoers Tension marked separately)

### F-02 — Even with the isolation flag on, MANAGEMENT_API_KEY, BRIDGE_TOKEN, provider keys and the ssh-agent socket stay ambient in every agent process
- Topics: AB-36.6
- Evidence: 37 DEVUSER_CLASS names "among them provider API keys, BRIDGE_TOKEN, MANAGEMENT_API_KEY, the System One vendor keys… and the ssh-agent socket path that grants use of every key the agent holds, remain in the agent environment by exception Q8" (`config/custody/env-classes.json:90,92,195,203,211`, `ADR-2122…md:178-179`).
- Failure: any agent-side code (tool, skill, injected instruction) exfiltrates or directly uses the management API key — full control of the API surface including GPU-metered and payment-adjacent routes (AB-31 lists `routes/payments.js`, `routes/broker-bridge.js`, `routes/llm-marketplace.js`) — or drives every key in the agent's ssh-agent. Activating role isolation does not close this.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt)

### F-03 — The identity port cannot tell workloads apart: uid 1000 (all agent tooling) is granted sovereign and forum-house signing, and receipts are not crash-durable
- Topics: AB-36.4, AB-36.5
- Evidence: the ACL grants caller uid 1000 `sign_event` on core kinds 38410-38415, 38415, 30840, 30841 and `forum_event` as JunkieJarvis on kinds 1, 42, 31923 (`config/custody/identity-port-acl.json:39-40`); caller tags, content and timestamp are passed through (`port.rs:559-564`); `mirror_key` with secret true is returned to devuser (`identity-port-acl.json:42`, called "the one key export" at `ADR-2101…md:230-234`). The mesh audit quoted in AB-36.4: "UID identifies an account, ordinary event signing checks kind rather than catalogue semantics, and appended receipts are not a crash-durable protocol inbox."
- Failure: one malicious tool among many running as devuser publishes house-account forum messages or sovereign-kind events with attacker-chosen content and tags; the port has no way to attribute or refuse it, and a crash erases the appended receipts so the signing is unauditable afterwards.
- Confidence: high (the grants, pass-through and audit qualification are stated; the any-tool implication is inferred from uid semantics the audit itself states)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: partially — the audit qualification is quoted in AB-36.4 prose; the uid-1000 grant consequence itself is unmarked

### F-04 — The signature-verification repair is merged but not deployed, and two verification paths with separate rosters remain
- Topics: AB-40
- Evidence: "Both the forum-governance and local inbound paths call _verifySig before dispatch. A verifier exception now rejects the event" (`relay-consumer.js:743-749`); but "It is merged source, awaiting deployment; it does not certify a running container"; Open: "ABI-05 consumer/roster consolidation and deployment remain unverified" — the independent authority consumer (`authority-consumer.js:159`) already rejects exceptions, so two rosters/ingresses coexist.
- Failure: a production container built before the merge turns a verifier exception (e.g. a dependency outage) into acceptance, and an unverified kind-31403 dispatches the orchestrator with side effects (`relay-consumer.js:479,487`); meanwhile the two unconsolidated rosters drift, so a signer accepted on one path is rejected (or accepted) differently on the other.
- Confidence: high (the deployment gap is the topic's own statement; whether any specific running host lacks the fix is not shown)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Open, covering consolidation and deployment)

### F-05 — The governed-write door's commit boundary does not bind the push: a concurrent writer's commits publish under another decision's attestation
- Topics: AB-25 (closing Tension), cross-ref AB-25.7
- Evidence: "A pathspec-limited commit does not constrain the later branch push to that commit. A second writer after the ahead-count check was reproduced with real local Git" (estate-review 2026-10-31-corpus-consumer.md); the apply path does "pathspec-limited commit, then ordinary branch push (commit failure recorded, never thrown)" (`management-api/lib/ontology-apply.js:365,821`); AB-25.7 notes GitHubSync/elevation routinely rewrite the corpus.
- Failure: a human-approved kind-31403 decision applies a corpus edit while a sync/elevation writer commits in the window; the push publishes the foreign commits under the approved decision, or a commit failure is only recorded and state silently diverges — governance attestation says one thing, the branch contains another.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension)

### F-06 — The settlement chain's containment is not committed: ADR-2103 D3 claims on-seal binding that the genesis does not have, and the mainnet gate is a field-existence check
- Topics: AB-32.2, AB-32.3, AB-31.5
- Evidence: D3 says `parent`, `headerProfile`, `currencyPin`, `cashOut`, `pegConfirmations`, `refundBlocks` are committed as a `pin:` record (`ADR-2103…md:73-74`); the seal established upstream commits only four things and "the pin is UNBUILT" (`ADR-2103…md:208,210-211`); the actual binding is "the committed document plus its genesisHash" (`sidechain/README.md:77`). The genesis test enforces only that a mainnet parent carries a `p21Receipt` field, never that it resolves (`tests/config/sidechain-genesis.test.sh:86`); the receipt-resolution CI check, the level-1-on-mainnet refusal and the refusing node are "not built" (`ADR-2103…md:229-230`).
- Failure: the containment properties the whole programme leans on (which parent, currency pin, cashOut false) are enforceable only against the git document; a mismatched runtime configuration or a future P4 activation passes every built check while violating the containment the record claims is sealed — the "regulatory gate exists as code" invariant (AB-31.9) is, at the chain layer, a filename check.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high (it becomes a custody incident the moment value flows; today the chain is testnet)
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension + Open + Debt)

### F-07 — Two governing documents disagree on how spend keys are derived from the identity key
- Topics: AB-31.7, AB-34.4
- Evidence: PRD-024 still says "PRD-024 still derives k_spend and k_sign from k_id by derive_subkey" (`sovereign-settlement.md:171-173`); the registry row, since the 2026-10-02 ADR-2097 D3 amendment, says the binding key is "an INDEPENDENT key minted beside k_id, signed by k_id", "independent, never derived" (`PROTOCOL-registry.md:172`); the built kind-38420 row follows the registry (AB-34.4 B1).
- Failure: an implementer of P3/P4 working from the design document derives spend authority from `k_id`; any compromise of the identity key then moves funds — exactly the loss the key-separation invariant (`INGRESS-identity.md:445`, "NEVER spends") exists to prevent. The two texts will be reconciled only by whoever reads both.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium (nothing built today derives spend keys; the divergence is a loaded trap for the phases that will)
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift)

### F-08 — A session instruction can be executed twice: submission succeeds, the API reports failure, and the bridge resubmits via tmux fallback
- Topics: AB-39.3
- Evidence: tmux submits text (`plane.rs:411`) → busy-hook write fails (`plane.rs:414-415`) → API returns ok false (`api.rs:106`) → client promise rejects (`client.cjs:9,18`) → "Bridge catches send error then uses tmux fallback" (`server.mjs:157,162,168`) which "may submit again"; the audit states "the error response does not always mean input was withheld… duplicate bridge delivery is a source-supported consequence"; separately, client timeout leaves "completion unknown" (`client.cjs:4,9`).
- Failure: an agent instruction with external effects (file operations, messages, spend requests) is delivered, the caller is told it failed, and the fallback delivers it again — the agent executes it twice while every caller believes exactly one attempt happened; the timeout path invites a third human retry.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (audit "What it shows" + REQUIRED delivery-ID fix; no Tension/Debt word, but explicitly flagged)

### F-09 — BRIDGE_TOKEN is simultaneously the voice-bridge approval secret and the LLM bearer key, and the speech stack runs ungated by it
- Topics: AB-27.7, AB-36.6
- Evidence: "LLM = tab0-bridge (OpenAI-compatible /v1/chat/completions), KYUTAI_LLM_API_KEY=${BRIDGE_TOKEN}" (`agentbox.sh:2749`); "NOT gated by BRIDGE_TOKEN — brought up before the check below" for the shared speech stack (`agentbox.sh:2723`); BRIDGE_TOKEN is a DEVUSER_CLASS ambient variable in every devuser process (`env-classes.json:203`, AB-36.6).
- Failure: one token compromise grants both bridge approvals and LLM spend, so the blast radius of any voice-component leak is doubled; and anything that can reach the speech containers gets the shared ASR/TTS services without the token at all, because they start before and outside the token check.
- Confidence: medium — the reuse, ordering and ambient exposure are stated; the exploit path is inferred
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-10 — The reconciler treats a Git error as a clean worktree and kills panes holding work; closed API sessions leave worktrees with no owner
- Topics: AB-39.1, AB-39.4
- Evidence: "Inspect desired plan.dir; Git error becomes zero changes (`plane.rs:325`)" so the dirty-hold (`reconcile.rs:85`) never fires, then "kill old pane, start replacement" (`plane.rs:268-277`); the audit's counterexample: "Git refuses the already-checked-out branch before the old pane is killed"; API close "Close rebuilds shared-directory plan… kills pane" while "Current API worktree remains; legacy reaper targets old root" (`plane.rs:475-479,527-530`); a removed seed's owned survivor is still listed (`plane.rs:373,383`).
- Failure: a transient git lock/permission error makes in-progress work look clean; the reconciler kills the session and the replacement checkout then fails on the already-checked-out branch — the session is dead and the work stranded; API-created worktrees accumulate with no cleanup or archive contract.
- Confidence: high (behavior stated; the wedge scenario is the audit's own counterexample)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (audit "What it shows"/counterexample)

### F-11 — Cancelling one ComfyUI job can kill a different job: the interrupt is global despite carrying a prompt_id
- Topics: AB-38.3
- Evidence: the client POSTs interrupt with prompt_id (`comfy_client.py:325`), but the pinned upstream handler checks a running snapshot before signalling (upstream server.py#L1258); the audit reproduces "owned A finishes; B starts" → "Upstream global interrupt can reach B"; the acknowledgement "is not terminal history" (`comfy_client.py:309,366`).
- Failure: an operator or agent cancels a long generation; a second job that entered the queue in the race window is terminated instead — wrong deliverable lost, GPU spend wasted, and the cancellation is reported as acknowledged-success for the job that had already finished.
- Confidence: high (controlled reproduction recorded in the material)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (CONTROLLED/GAP-class audit note)

### F-12 — Level-2 signing is live on ephemeral Nostr kinds that relays are not expected to retain, and the corrected stored-kind profile has no allocation
- Topics: AB-35.3, AB-34.5
- Evidence: "23510 to 23514 are EPHEMERAL under NIP-01: relays are not expected to retain them" (`REVIEW-kofn-consensus-gpt6-astra.md:217`); the registry still allocates that band without recording the rejection, and the corrected stored kinds have no allocation (`PROTOCOL-registry.md:163-173` vs `ADR-2101…md:162-168`); yet "Level 2 usable: sidestr-round 0.1.0's cosign co-signs with the JS signers live" (`sovereign-settlement.md:336`, AB-34.5 N6), and the required durable journal/signer service is unbuilt (AB-35.5).
- Failure: after a relay purge or a producer restart, the votes and justifications for an in-flight signing round no longer exist on the wire or on disk; a conflicting or re-sealed block cannot be proven or disproven from retained evidence — finality claims become unreconstructable exactly when they are disputed.
- Confidence: medium — live co-signing and the ephemerality finding are stated; the lost-evidence consequence is inferred
- Severity: medium (testnet chain today; becomes high the moment level-2 signs value)
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt + Open)

### F-13 — The upstream-pin rule was violated by its own bump: consensus code baked into the image is an "active development head"
- Topics: AB-34.1
- Evidence: the rule is "bump a line only after the chain has been restarted and a block produced on that commit" (`config/sidechain/upstream-pins:5-7`); the `blaketestnode` line was bumped as an "operator-accepted active development head" (`upstream-pins:10`), and that commit is baked into the image (`lib/sidestr-upstream.nix:57-60`).
- Failure: the block-producing and block-verifying code for both sealed chains runs an unreviewed development commit that never produced a block under the pin discipline; historical blocks may validate differently, and the one mechanism that makes "the rules the chain is produced by" a repository fact (the business claim in AB-34) has been exercised against itself.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension)

### F-14 — Voicelink Stop/Notification recorder hooks are written into every profile while ADR-2151 records the feature as not implemented
- Topics: AB-28.1, AB-27.7
- Evidence: ADR-2151 is `proposed`, `implementation_status: none`, `activation_status: inactive` (`ADR-2151…md:5-7`); yet `provision-stacks` "writes the voicelink Stop and Notification recorder hooks into every profile whenever [voicelink].record_events is true, and the manifest sets it true" (`stacks.rs:311-323`, `agentbox.toml:2233`); the same pattern repeats for the isaac-sim sidecar (catalogue `live`/`gate: null` while the ADR is `proposed`, AB-27.14) and the voice console (ran 5 days while its gate said false, AB-27.7).
- Failure: every Claude Code session gets Stop/Notification hooks spooling session events through an implementation no decision record has accepted; an operator auditing against ADR-2151 correctly concludes no such path should exist, so the event data is collected with no reviewed retention, routing or access story.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension, twice)

### F-15 — A TUI save silently resets hand-edited model-router configuration — including the cost ceiling — to fixed literals
- Topics: AB-28.10, cross-ref AB-29
- Evidence: only `enabled` round-trips through the TUI state (`tui_read.rs:127`); the section renderer "emits the block verbatim — enabled from state(s), every OTHER key a FIXED literal (provider "openrouter", quality_bar 0.50, cost_ceiling_usd_per_mtok 0, …)" (`tui_sections.rs:193-200`); the DOC-DRIFT note: "a TUI save therefore RESETS provider/quality_bar/cost_ceiling/trajectory/assets_dir to these literals even if the operator hand-edited agentbox.toml… there is no read-back-and-preserve path" — unlike the ADR-2031 consultant-model protection (AB-28.3).
- Failure: an operator tightens the cost ceiling or changes provider in `agentbox.toml`; the next unrelated TUI save silently rewrites `cost_ceiling_usd_per_mtok` to 0 and the provider to openrouter, and routing/spend behaviour changes with no prompt, log or diff shown to the operator.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DOC-DRIFT)

## Not judgeable from this material

1. **What is actually deployed.** Several topics distinguish merged source from running containers (AB-40 "awaiting deployment"; multiple system_one/Jev consumers "staged until a rebuild, so not live"). I would need the per-service deployment pins — image builds, running commits — before judging which of the fixed and broken behaviours above are live on any given host.
2. **Network-level exposure of the sidecars.** code-server binds `0.0.0.0:8080` reachable across `visionclaw_network` (AB-27.6), the speech stack starts ungated (AB-27.7), the ComfyUI sidecar and isaac-sim runner sit on the same network (AB-27.1, AB-27.14). I need the compose topology and each service's own auth to say what a co-resident container can actually reach.
3. **The runtime uid census in the shipped (flag-off) image.** F-01/F-03 hinge on which processes and agent-spawned tools share uid 1000 and the `ab-identity-port` socket group; the material asserts the design, not the live process list.
4. **The blast radius of MANAGEMENT_API_KEY.** `routes/payments.js`, `broker-bridge.js` and `llm-marketplace.js` are listed as sources in AB-31 but never drawn; without the route table I cannot size what an ambient-key holder (F-02) can spend or reconfigure.
5. **Durability of WORKSPACE state.** Beads SQLite, session worktrees, the router ledger/bandit state and `blocks.dat` all live on binds; AB-37.3 shows secret-backup uses a fixed, narrow filename allowlist. I need the actual backup coverage and a crash-recovery rehearsal to judge what a host failure loses.

## agentbox#3 / premortem

# Post-mortem — custody and data-integrity failure in the agentbox estate

Scope note: this is a verification-first draft. Every claim below is drawn from the estate's own diagram corpus at the cited revisions; where I reason past the material I say "inferred". The three root-cause candidates are ranked by likelihood given the last recorded state: role isolation staged but off, secrets ambient to every agent process, the host Docker socket unresolved, and a reproduced race in the governed-write door.

---

### R-1 — Every agent-side process still holds the estate's operational secrets: role isolation ships off, and even on, 37 named secrets stay ambient by exception
- Topics: AB-36, AB-36.3, AB-36.6, AB-36.7
- Chain of events: an agent-side component (MCP server, skill script, or a third-party dependency it spawns — the estate itself runs `uvx blender-mcp`, `@claude-flow/cli`, skill-bridged binaries) executes as devuser (uid 1000) in the main container → it reads the ambient environment, which carries `MANAGEMENT_API_KEY`, `BRIDGE_TOKEN`, provider API keys and the ssh-agent socket path → it exfiltrates them → the attacker drives the management API (payment-gated and GPU-metered routes, the governed-apply surface), spends provider credit directly, and uses the agent-held ssh keys; with the flag off it can additionally read the at-rest sovereign identity key at `/run/secrets/nostr.key`, which is devuser-owned in that mode.
- Evidence: "Until this change every secret in the container reached every process… every long-running program ran as devuser (uid 1000), so a 0600 key file protected nothing from a co-resident agent" (AB-36 intro); `[security].role_isolation` ships false (`agentbox.toml:2506`); the only committed rehearsal receipt is verdict STAGED with flag false (`docs/estate-closeout/x1-rehearsal-20261003T090708Z.json:4`, `:8`); 37 DEVUSER_CLASS names — "among them provider API keys, `BRIDGE_TOKEN`, `MANAGEMENT_API_KEY`, the System One vendor keys… and the ssh-agent socket path that grants use of every key the agent holds — remain in the agent environment by exception Q8" (`config/custody/env-classes.json:90`, `:92`, `ADR-2122:178-179`, AB-36.6 Debt); with the flag off, `/run/secrets` is "chown 1000:1000, chmod 0700 as before… devuser's /run/secrets/nostr.key is written as today" (`config/entrypoint-unified.sh:396`).
- Warning signs visible today: the design's own premise (one mistaken or compromised tool could "impersonate the organisation or move value", AB-36 business text); the Debt paragraph naming all 37; the STAGED-only receipt; the measured upstream defects in the very packages the estate runs (the `@claude-flow/cli` tarball omissions and the silently failing transformers import, AB-29.1).
- Confidence: high (the exposure is stated directly; the exploitation chain is inferred).

### R-2 — The host Docker socket stays world-accessible: any devuser compromise escalates to root on the host and reads every at-rest key
- Topics: AB-36, AB-36.1, AB-36.7
- Chain of events: a compromised agent tool runs as devuser → reaches the host Docker socket bind, whose inode earlier boots widened and which remains world-accessible on the host → the daemon (root on the host) is driven to mount the host filesystem or spawn a privileged container → the attacker reads the `agentbox-secrets` volume, every at-rest chain and identity key, and host credentials; the container-side boot can only detect and record `degraded:docker-socket`, never close it, and the host rehearsal records the socket mode without gating on it.
- Evidence: "the host Docker socket inode stays world-accessible until the host chmods it. The socket is a bind of the host inode that earlier boots widened, and skipping the chmod under the flag does not narrow it" (`docker-compose.override.yml:116-117`); "The flag-on boot can only detect and record `degraded:docker-socket`" (`config/entrypoint-unified.sh:614`); "The host half records the mode for the owner without gating on it" (`scripts/activation/role-isolation-rehearsal.host.sh:18-19`) "while ADR-2122 counts `degraded:docker-socket` as a failure" (`ADR-2122:162`); uid 965 is reserved precisely because "the Docker daemon is root on the host" (`config/role-accounts.json:6`); AB-36 intro: the bypass "only closes under the flag, and only fully once the host narrows the socket's mode".
- Warning signs visible today: the Open (R2) block itself; the business text "the host machine's Docker control socket has to be tightened on the host itself"; the flag shipping off, so the socket is reachable from devuser processes today.
- Confidence: high that the socket is open as described; inferred that it is the escalation vector in this incident.

### R-3 — The governed-write door can publish unaudited concurrent commits under a signed forum decision
- Topics: AB-25.12, AB-25.7
- Chain of events: a human approves a kind-31403 decision → `applyOntologyDecision` performs a pathspec-limited commit and a branch push (`management-api/lib/ontology-apply.js:365`, `:821`) → a second writer commits to the same branch after the ahead-count check but before the push → the push carries the second writer's unvetted commits to the corpus under the governance receipt → downstream condensation, embedding and recall build on tampered data attributed to an approved decision; the same node records that commit failure is "recorded, never thrown", so the door degrades silently rather than aborting loudly.
- Evidence: "A pathspec-limited commit does not constrain the later branch push to that commit. A second writer after the ahead-count check was reproduced with real local Git" (`../../estate-review/2026-10-31-corpus-consumer.md#commit-and-attestation-boundaries`, AB-25.12 Tension); concurrent corpus writers demonstrably exist — "GitHubSync/elevation rewrote the corpus" with no natural re-trigger for the condense cache (AB-25.7 note); the commit-failure behavior is stated in the APPLY node itself (`ontology-apply.js:365`, `:821`).
- Warning signs visible today: the authors' own reproduction with real local Git on 2026-10-31; the topic's governing note that the current guards live in OM-07 while this panel retains the historical stamp.
- Confidence: high that the race exists (reproduced in the material); its role in this incident is inferred.

---

### F-01 — The sudo route follows the build-time flag, not the boot-time one, and a single compose guard keeps devuser from root
- Topics: AB-36.2, AB-36.7
- Evidence: the design made the flag boot-class (ADR-2122 §2 via AB-36.2), but the catalogue records it rebuild-class because the devuser sudo route is fixed at image build (`management-api/lib/system-manifest.js:97-98`); built with the flag off, the image keeps devuser in `wheel` (`flake.nix:4045`) and bakes a `NOPASSWD: ALL` drop-in (`config/bake-devuser-privilege.sh:44-47`, `flake.nix:4059`); "the grant is inert only because `no-new-privileges` is set (`docker-compose.yml:129`) and no setuid sudo ships"; booted on without a rebuild it only records `degraded:devuser-sudo` (`config/entrypoint-unified.sh:651`).
- Failure: the operator flips `[security].role_isolation = true` without rebuilding, or an override drops `no-new-privileges` — devuser reaches root and every role boundary in AB-36 is void.
- Confidence: high (facts stated); escalation scenario inferred.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension)

### F-02 — The setup seed claims all learning gates are off while shipping two of them on
- Topics: AB-30.3
- Evidence: the seed comment and file header describe the shipped default as "all learning/hygiene gates OFF" (`setup/server/src/main.rs:70-76`, `setup/agentbox.default.toml:5-6`), but `[memory_learning] enabled = true` (`agentbox.default.toml:451`) and `[seo_watch] enabled = true` (`agentbox.default.toml:845`).
- Failure: a fresh client install is provisioned from the seed; the operator relies on the stated default; client conversations are ingested into memory learning and client domains placed under SEO watch — undisclosed retention and an egress surface the client never agreed to.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift)

### F-03 — The signature-verification repair is merged but undeployed, and the two verification rosters are unconsolidated
- Topics: AB-40, AB-40.1
- Evidence: "It is merged source, awaiting deployment; it does not certify a running container or complete the wider governance migration"; "ABI-05 consumer/roster consolidation and deployment remain unverified"; the forum and local inbound paths reject on verifier exception only post-repair (`mcp/nostr-bridge/relay-consumer.js:748-749`), while the independent consumer already rejected at `management-api/lib/authority-consumer.js:159`.
- Failure: a deployed container predating the merge — or the unconsolidated ingress path — processes a kind-31403 whose signature check throws (verifier dependency unavailable) and proceeds to roster check and orchestrator dispatch (`relay-consumer.js:479`, `:487`): an unverifiable decision gains effects.
- Confidence: medium (defect class stated; a stale container or unconsolidated path at incident time is inferred)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Open)

### F-04 — Identity-port grants are keyed to the shared uid 1000, so every agent tool holds the full devuser grant set
- Topics: AB-36.5, AB-37.2, AB-36.4
- Evidence: the ACL grants uid 1000 `sign_event` on colloquy kinds 38410-38415, 30840, 30841 and `forum_event` on JunkieJarvis kinds 1, 42, 31923 (`config/custody/identity-port-acl.json:34-43`, `:39-40`); devuser is in the socket group `ab-identity-port` (`config/role-accounts.json:130-133`); the mesh audit states "UID identifies an account, ordinary event signing checks kind rather than catalogue semantics" (AB-36.4 note, AB-37.2).
- Failure: one compromised tool connects to the socket and posts kind-1 notes and calendar events as the house account, signs colloquy units, and obtains NIP-98 auth to the pod base URL — impersonation with valid receipts and no per-caller distinction.
- Confidence: high on the ACL shape; the incident path is inferred
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-05 — `mirror_key` exports a private key secret to any devuser process
- Topics: AB-36.5
- Evidence: the devuser grant is "mirror_key with secret true (`identity-port-acl.json:42`)"; the design specified a `mirror_wrap` operation instead, and ADR-2101 "records it as the one key export, with a `mirror_wrap` operation as the retiring condition" (`ADR-2101:230-234`, AB-36.5 text: "That tension is recorded in AB-16").
- Failure: any devuser process exports the live-mirror child key; the export lands in an agent transcript or log and lives outside the port's audit boundary indefinitely, since the retiring condition is unbuilt.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (recorded as a tension, cross-referenced to AB-16)

### F-06 — A TUI save silently resets the model router's cost ceiling, quality bar and provider to literals
- Topics: AB-28.10, AB-29.2
- Evidence: `tui_sections.rs:193-200` emits fixed literals (`provider "openrouter"`, `quality_bar 0.50`, `cost_ceiling_usd_per_mtok 0`, …) on every TUI-driven write; the DOC-DRIFT note states "a TUI save therefore RESETS provider/quality_bar/cost_ceiling/trajectory/assets_dir… there is no read-back-and-preserve path"; only `enabled` round-trips through the flat state (`tui_read.rs:127-128`).
- Failure: the operator tightens the cost ceiling in `agentbox.toml`, later saves any unrelated TUI change — the ceiling reverts to 0 and the quality bar to 0.50, and the routing flow drawn in AB-29.4 shows no ceiling enforcement at decision time, so routed spend runs unbounded.
- Confidence: high for the reset; the "no enforcement at execution" reading is inferred
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DOC-DRIFT)

### F-07 — code-server and Jupyter bind 0.0.0.0 on the shared sidecar bridge, reachable from every neighbour container
- Topics: AB-27.5, AB-27.6
- Evidence: code-server binds `0.0.0.0:8080` and the note says it is "still not 127.0.0.1 — the docker-compose loopback publish only constrains host->container, not bridge->container on visionclaw_network" (`flake.nix:2858` area, AB-27.6 DOC-DRIFT note); Jupyter runs `--ip=0.0.0.0 --port=8888` (`flake.nix:2469`); the password is "mint/adopt" — adoption can reuse a weak existing value (`config/entrypoint-unified.sh:1013-1044`); sidecars share `visionclaw_network` (AB-27.1, AB-27.14).
- Failure: a compromised sidecar reaches code-server's terminal over the bridge with an adopted weak password → a shell as devuser in the main container → the entire ambient-secret set of R-1.
- Confidence: high on the binds and adopt behaviour; the exploitation chain is inferred
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DOC-DRIFT in the AB-27.6 note)

### F-08 — A failed post-submission hook turns a delivered instruction into an error, and the bridge resubmits
- Topics: AB-39.3
- Evidence: send submits text via tmux (`plane.rs:411`) and only then writes the busy hook (`plane.rs:414-415`); hook-write failure returns `ok false` (`api.rs:106`) — "The error response does not always mean input was withheld"; the client rejects on error reply or timeout (`client.cjs:9`, `:18`); the bridge "catches send error then uses tmux fallback" (`server.mjs:157`, `:162`, `:168`) and the diagram marks it "may submit again"; the audit: "duplicate bridge delivery is a source-supported consequence… A delivery ID and staged receipt are needed before retry can be made reliable."
- Failure: a destructive or billable instruction is delivered, the hook write fails, the bridge resubmits — the instruction executes twice against the client's environment.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-09 — A Git inspection failure is treated as a clean worktree, so reconcile restarts over work it never checked
- Topics: AB-39.1, AB-39.4
- Evidence: "Inspect desired plan.dir — Git error becomes zero changes (`plane.rs:325`)" precedes the restart path; the audit counterexample shows Git refusing an already-checked-out branch before the old pane is killed, and states "Both probes replace a process whose old worktree contains work"; AB-39.4 adds that surviving sessions can be left with "orphaned work" and only a "legacy reaper targets old root" (`plane.rs:527-530`).
- Failure: reconcile misreads a failing git call as zero changes, kills the old pane, and the replacement's worktree setup then fails — the session is down and in-progress client work is stranded in a worktree with no visible owner.
- Confidence: high for the code path; the combined end state is inferred
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-10 — Send applies weaker blocker checks than status, so text can be submitted into a login/blocked tool session
- Topics: AB-39.2
- Evidence: status reconstructs tool launch rules and detects tool blockers (`plane.rs:374`, `:378`; `status.rs:57-58`), but send "looks up manifest seed only" and applies common blockers, refusing only classified dead or blocked (`plane.rs:405-408`, `:342`, `:344`); "An API tool's login pattern can appear in its blocked status row yet be absent from its send check"; the native probe observed the dummy process consuming the input.
- Failure: task text — potentially containing credentials or destructive shell lines — is submitted into a session whose tool is sitting at an interactive login prompt instead of the intended consumer.
- Confidence: high (stated); the concrete harm is inferred
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-11 — Cancellation carries a job id but the server op is not atomic, so the interrupt can land on the wrong job
- Topics: AB-38.3
- Evidence: the client POSTs an interrupt with `prompt_id` (`comfy_client.py:325`) but the pinned upstream handler "checks a snapshot before signalling"; the audit reproduces "owned A finishes; B starts → upstream global interrupt can reach B"; "Request acknowledgement is not terminal history" (`comfy_client.py:309`, `:366`); the fix is marked REQUIRED: "use atomic per-job cancellation route and verify terminal history".
- Failure: an operator cancels job A; A completes and an expensive job B starts; the global interrupt destroys B — hours of GPU render lost, or the caller believes A was canceled when it actually completed.
- Confidence: high (reproduced with controlled effects per the audit)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-12 — Secret-backup uses a fixed filename allowlist that silently omits any key provisioned under another name
- Topics: AB-37.3
- Evidence: backup scans a configured root (`services/secret-backup/src/main.rs:154`, `:156`) against a fixed allowlist (`:58`, `:66`, `:178`) and skips other basenames (`:179`); the audit: "Synthetic federation-key filenames are omitted even inside the scan root… The proposed key needs explicit inclusion, recovery-state inventory and an off-host restore rehearsal."
- Failure: a joint-custody or node key is provisioned under a name absent from the allowlist, never backed up, and a host disk loss makes it permanently unrecoverable — locked-out spaces or funds.
- Confidence: high on the omission; that such a key exists today is proposed-stage (inferred)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-13 — Identity-port receipts are appended, not crash-durable, so a signed action can exist without its audit record
- Topics: AB-36.4, AB-37.2
- Evidence: the port writes one JSONL line before returning (`identity_port/port.rs:320`, `:326`, `:328`; `identity_port/receipts.rs:132`, `:136`) and refuses if the receipt cannot be written; the mesh audit qualifies: "appended receipts are not a crash-durable protocol inbox."
- Failure: the port signs, then crashes or loses power before the append reaches disk — the action happened with no durable receipt, and a later dispute about what the port signed cannot be settled from the journal; a torn line also breaks replay.
- Confidence: medium (non-durability stated; the loss window is inferred)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-14 — An upstream pin was bumped against its own rule and the unproven consensus code is baked into the image
- Topics: AB-34.1
- Evidence: the rule is to bump a pin "only after the chain has been restarted and a block produced on that commit" (`config/sidechain/upstream-pins:5-7`); the `blaketestnode` line was bumped 2026-10-01 as an "operator-accepted active development head" — "a reason that is acceptance rather than a produced block" (`upstream-pins:10`, commit `47e187934`); "The bake now carries that same commit, so the acceptance is baked into the image as well" (`lib/sidestr-upstream.nix:57-60`).
- Failure: unverified consensus code baked into the image mints a block the mirrors or federation reject — a chain split or invalid state announced to public relays under kind 33333.
- Confidence: high on the facts; the consequence is inferred
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension)

### F-15 — The faucet treasury key's legacy workspace twin is never deleted and stays devuser-readable in both modes
- Topics: AB-34.3, AB-34.6
- Evidence: the producer convention is keys as files under `/var/lib/agentbox/secrets` (`run-producer.sh:7`); the role table names "the workspace file… only as its legacy twin" (`config/role-accounts.json:78`); with the flag off — how it ships — the faucet still defaults to the workspace path (`run-faucet.sh:27`, `agentbox.toml:1926`); "the migrate step copies that legacy file once and never deletes it, so a spend-capable key stays on the host-visible bind in both modes until a manual purge" (`ADR-2122:232-235`).
- Failure: any devuser process, or anyone with the host bind, reads the treasury key and spends directly from it — bypassing the agent's rate limits entirely; severity rises the moment the chain carries non-test value.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension)

## dreamlab-ai-website / critical

### F-01 — A plaintext admin private key sits in git history, unrotated
- Topics: DW-07.2
- Evidence: `CLOSEOUT-SECURITY-AUDIT.md:300` records that the deleted root seeders embedded a plaintext `ADMIN_PRIVKEY_HEX`; `CLOSEOUT-SECURITY-AUDIT.md:116` states the scripts were deleted (commit `3baf2fa4`, 2026-10-07) but "key not rotated, remains in history from `c0bcece`" and that whether it is live "cannot be decided from this repo".
- Failure: anyone with read access to the repository history (clone, fork, former CI log) obtains a private key that, if it corresponds to any entry in `[admin].static_pubkeys` or the auth-worker `ADMIN_PUBKEYS` secret, grants full forum admin: moderation, whitelist promotion, zone access.
- Confidence: high that the key is in history and was never rotated; whether it is live is not decidable from this material
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt, DW-07.2)

### F-02 — One key is both primary forum admin and governance publisher; the split is staged, never executed
- Topics: DW-03.3, DW-03.4, DW-06.7
- Evidence: `dreamlab.toml:39-72` comment and DW-03.4's state diagram: `visionclaw-server` (`11ed6422...663c`) is simultaneously primary admin and `[governance].agent_pubkeys` publisher; the runbook split is "Staged", not "Split". The auth-worker `ADMIN_PUBKEYS` Cloudflare secret "cannot be rotated from this repo or CI" (`dreamlab.toml:39-72` comment).
- Failure: compromise of the VisionClaw server (an external system, DW-01.8) yields total forum administration plus governance publication in one key; there is no independent operator/admin key to revoke it with.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift D7; "Open, staged" in DW-03.4)

### F-03 — Decrypted/plaintext user data is routed to a third-party cloud endpoint by default
- Topics: DW-03.8, DW-04.5, DW-07.4, DW-09.5
- Evidence: the overlay opts in to `JEV_TRIAGE`, `JEV_SEARCH_RERANK` and `JEV_RATIONALE_CHECK` (`forum-config/deploy/SYSTEM-ONE.md:3-5`), with a 5-minute relay cron for triage (`relay-worker.wrangler.toml:99-100`); "all three reach TypeSafe jev-latest directly", the auth worker's rationale check is "cloud by default and private only when `JEV_RATIONALE_CHECK_LOCAL_ONLY=1`" (DW-03.8), search rerank is "not local-only, because the kit's per-consumer local-only switch is off by default" (`SYSTEM-ONE.md:13-16`); when enquiry tagging is enabled, "plaintext enquiry is request state" to the "vendor endpoint with API key" (`scripts/lib/jev.mjs:59`, DW-09.5).
- Failure: user search queries, relay events swept by the 5-minute triage cron, and (when `DREAMLAB_JEV=1`) decrypted contact-enquiry text leave the trust boundary to a vendor API by default, with no local-only default and no redaction step shown.
- Confidence: high on the configuration facts; the incident impact is inferred — what the triage/rationale payloads contain is not stated
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: partially — the opt-in is labelled OPEN in DW-03.8; the default-cloud direction and its privacy consequence carry no marker

### F-04 — An encrypted zone distributes its key to the agent cohort under a decision record that does not exist
- Topics: DW-03.1, DW-03.3, DW-06.6 (I7), DW-06.9 (D15)
- Evidence: `dreamlab.toml:149` sets zone4 `agent_keys = true` and `dreamlab.toml:123,137,148` set `zone2/3/4` `encrypted = true`; the `[encryption]` master gate cites 'ADR-2016' (`dreamlab.toml:34`) but "no ADR-2016-*.md exists in docs/adr/ at HEAD — the record was never written" (I7). DW-03.3 states the consequence of agent cohort membership directly: `website-info` gets `cohorts = []` "because the `agent` cohort would receive the zone4 key" (`dreamlab.toml:407-412`).
- Failure: E2EE confidentiality of zone2–zone4 was widened (from "only zone3") to include machine agents holding the zone4 key, with no surviving decision record naming which agents, why, or how the key is revoked; the governing doc still asserts the opposite (`IDENTITY-zones.md:180-181`).
- Confidence: high on all stated facts
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift D15 / invariant I7 breach); the agent-cohort-receives-the-key consequence itself is stated without a marker

### F-05 — Automatic poker payouts are not bound to the verified game replay
- Topics: DW-10.4
- Evidence: the verifier checks "commitment, seed, actions, cards, result and deterministic house play" (`nostr-bbs-poker/src/verify.rs:151`) but its Claim "has no settlement amount, destination or supplied root"; the client's "payment uses supplied settlement, root and destination without deriving all from replay" (`poker/live.rs:968`) and "Verified status allows automatic payment" (`poker/live.rs:1036`).
- Failure: a compromised, buggy, or MITM'd house reply passes game verification while directing an automatic on-chain transfer of a chosen amount to a chosen destination; the user never sees a confirmation prompt because the hand is "verified".
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt, DW-10.4 D)

### F-06 — Money effects are emitted before persistence; restarts destroy active hands; persist failure does not stop dealing
- Topics: DW-10.3
- Evidence: "Finished hand emits Done and optional Pay before its Persist effect" (`table.rs:846`); "restart reconstructs Citizen from Ledger and starts with empty active-hand maps" (`table.rs:189`) — "an idle active hand survives a simulated month of ticks but disappears after reconstruction"; "failed file write is logged without stopping subsequent dealing" (`run.rs:276`); effects run in queue order and house debt is cleared on producer acceptance (`run.rs:460`).
- Failure: a crash between the Pay effect's acceptance and the ledger Persist re-presents the settlement as unpaid after restart — duplicate payment or lost settlement record; any service restart mid-hand silently deletes every member's in-progress game and staked position; a failing disk lets the game continue while the persisted book diverges from what was paid.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt, DW-10.3 C and F)

### F-07 — House credit admission trusts an unverifiable payment claim and reserves nothing
- Topics: DW-10.2
