- Evidence: "a non-hex 64-byte transaction ID can renew the payment claim timestamp" (`table.rs:399`); dealing checks balances "without reserving active buy-ins" (`table.rs:535`) so "two simultaneous buy-ins can use the same available house balance and cap" (`table.rs:225`); native fixtures "admit two 200-unit hands against 200 units of house balance and a 200-unit cap" on the treasury-funded asset tables (DREAM, BLAKES7).
- Failure: a member submits a fabricated transaction ID, keeps the claim alive past grace, and plays on unsecured credit; concurrent buy-ins double-count the same house balance and daily cap, exposing the treasury beyond the configured payout ceiling.
- Confidence: high (fixture-demonstrated); whether the payout cap is ultimately breached is explicitly not demonstrated
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt, DW-10.2 C and F)

### F-08 — The poker house service and the browser client run different kit commits
- Topics: DW-10.1, DW-01.5
- Evidence: "service pin dd454474 differs from website client pin 9c36b603" (`project/agentbox/lib/poker-citizen.nix:32` vs `.github/workflows/deploy.yml:130`); the runner "executes the baked binary" (`config/poker/run-citizen.sh:23`); the 42 poker files were byte-identical across the two pins but "equal poker source does not prove all dependencies equal"; ADR-2009's "exact-commit lockstep" wording is stronger than the deployed reality.
- Failure: the two halves of a money protocol (house state machine, client verifier/settlement) drift independently — a future repin on one side only ships a client whose replay assumptions no longer match the house's dealing, or vice versa, with no gate comparing the two pins.
- Confidence: high on the pin divergence; medium on the failure scenario (inferred)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift, DW-10.1 E)

### F-09 — Visitor enquiry capture is best-effort end-to-end: a failed monitor copy silently disappears the message
- Topics: DW-04.3, DW-09.3, DW-07.8
- Evidence: each submission's monitor copy is "Best-effort separate monitor wrap — missing/equal key is a no-op" and "Copy failure does not change visitor-facing primary result" (`src/lib/nostr.ts:352,363`, DW-09.3); the watcher exists precisely because "the relay rewrites every kind-1059 read to the viewer's own #p, so the admin inbox is unreadable to anyone else" (`scripts/inbox-watch/run.mjs:4-11`) — the admin's own copy is the only other place the message exists.
- Failure: the relay accepts the primary wrap (visitor sees success), the monitor copy fails or the key is missing/equal, and the enquiry lands only in an inbox no automated process reads — a customer contact, signup or chat message is lost with no error anywhere.
- Confidence: high on the mechanism; the end-to-end loss requires a copy failure, which is plausible but unquantified
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no — described as design ("best effort") in DW-09.3 without a Debt/Drift marker

### F-10 — The nightly digest accounting permanently drops messages it fetched but did not show
- Topics: DW-07.8, DW-09.4
- Evidence: "all fetched IDs become persisted seen — undisplayed items do not roll into next digest" (`run.mjs:389`); the digest "itemises first 25" only (`digest.mjs:18`); "any content containing probe phrase is omitted as a synthetic probe" (`run.mjs:200`) — a sender-controlled string; "next cursor is oldest second minus one — unseen ties at the boundary can be skipped" (`digest.mjs:74`); wrap IDs are "marked seen before verification and classification" (`run.mjs:328`). The 30-message fixture "displays 25, marks all 30 seen and reports zero new on its next run".
- Failure: any night with more than 25 messages, a message containing the probe phrase, a boundary tie, or a crash after marking-seen permanently removes customer enquiries from the operator's view while the run reports healthy.
- Confidence: high (fixture-demonstrated)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt, DW-09.4 and DW-07.8)

### F-11 — The only backup path has no restore procedure, lands plaintext on a shared mount, and depends on one cron host
- Topics: DW-07.5
- Evidence: "This is the only backup/restore path for the forum found in this repo — there is no corresponding restore script, only the raw D1/R2/KV export" (DW-07.5); kind-40/42/0 events are "signed plaintext, so 'the NAS copy is readable content — keep it on the trusted segment only'" (`forum-backup.sh:16-18`) with default destination `/mnt/dell/shared/backups/dreamlab-forum`; the schedule is a single supercronic entry on the agentbox host (`crontab:6`).
- Failure: a D1/R2 loss event arrives and the operator has exports in an untested format, no restore runbook, and a readable copy of all forum content on a shared NAS segment; if the agentbox host is down, backups silently stop (single point of failure, inferred).
- Confidence: high on no-restore-script and plaintext-NAS facts; the single-host dependency is inferred
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no — stated plainly in DW-07.5 prose, no marker

### F-12 — Content generators fail soft: a missing directory or YAML typo ships an empty site with a green build
- Topics: DW-06.1, DW-06.2
- Evidence: "a missing `public/data/workshops/` writes an empty `workshop-list.json` instead of failing the build" (`generate-workshop-list.mjs:121-124`) and "a YAML parse error writes an empty `testimonials.json`" (`generate-testimonials.mjs:22-25`); both run in `predev`/`prebuild` and nothing downstream flags the empty artefacts.
- Failure: a renamed content directory or a malformed `site-content.yaml` in a dependency-bump commit deploys a site whose entire workshops section and testimonials are silently empty; every gate (build, discovery check, deploy) passes.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no — the fail-soft behaviour is described without any marker

### F-13 — The production API surface is raw `*.workers.dev` hosts; the documented branded end-state is a known-broken configuration, and DNS state is unverified for months
- Topics: DW-01.3, DW-06.7 (D4)
- Evidence: the branded worker domains "are **not provisioned in DNS** — the client instead talks to raw `*.workers.dev` hosts baked into the build env (`deploy.yml:49-61`; verified 2026-06-09 `ERR_NAME_NOT_RESOLVED`, not re-checked as of 2026-08-31)" and "shipping the branded domains baked in previously severed every client API call" (`BASELINE-architecture.md:91-97`); the branded domains remain "the documented end-state".
- Failure: anyone executing the documented end-state (baking branded domains into the env) without first provisioning DNS repeats the total client-API outage; meanwhile production depends on `workers.dev` hostname availability, whose current DNS resolution nobody has checked since June 2026.
- Confidence: high on all facts
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift D4)

### F-14 — Admin key rotation is a four-to-five-location atomic manual change with one unverifiable site and one mirror never checked
- Topics: DW-03.4, DW-03.5, DW-03.6
- Evidence: rotation must move together `[admin].static_pubkeys`, relay and search `ADMIN_PUBKEYS` `[vars]`, the auth-worker `ADMIN_PUBKEYS` Cloudflare secret — which "cannot be rotated from this repo or CI" — plus the client `VITE_ADMIN_PUBKEY` mirror once the split runbook runs (`deploy.yml:62-73`); before 2026-09-05 CI compared only one of the mirror groups, so "a rotation that updated the TOML and missed a mirror shipped stale keys or zones silently" (`config-mirrors.mjs:1-18`); `[nip05]` is "still not compared at all" (`config-mirrors.mjs:360-361`).
- Failure: the next admin-key rotation (e.g., forced by F-01) misses the unverifiable CF secret or the unchecked nip05 mirror; stale admin keys remain live in one worker, or nip05 identity assertions diverge — inconsistent privilege enforcement across workers with no CI signal.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: partially — nip05 is marked Debt (DW-03.6); the rotation atomicity itself is presented as accepted design (ADR-2005 deferral), not as a defect

### F-15 — The automated SEO proposal publisher commits before its own verification step, so the normal path always rejects after committing
- Topics: DW-08.5
- Evidence: "inspect staged names AFTER commit — normal empty index fails expected-file check" (`propose.mjs:298,301`); the commit is created "with hooks disabled" (`propose.mjs:288`) and the push/label/draft-PR code is "not reached in local success-to-commit fixture" (DW-08.5) — the fixture demonstrates the publication defect without contacting GitHub.
- Failure: every successful proposal run creates a local commit on a branch and then fails its own expected-files check (the index is empty post-commit); no PR is ever published and orphaned committed branches accumulate — the ADR-2010 "tested, human-reviewed proposals" path cannot complete in the normal case.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift, DW-08.5)

## Not judgeable from this material

1. Whether the history-embedded `ADMIN_PRIVKEY_HEX` (from `c0bcece`) corresponds to a live key in the static admin set or the Cloudflare secret, and who can read the repository history (F-01 hinges on this).
2. What payload content the 5-minute `JEV_TRIAGE` relay cron and the auth-worker's cloud-default rationale check actually transmit to TypeSafe — event bodies, member data, or only public metadata (F-03's impact).
3. Per-agent cohort membership: whether `visionclaw-server` or any other governance/external agent actually holds the `agent` cohort and therefore the zone4 key (F-04's blast radius).
4. Current DNS resolution for the branded worker domains and any production dependency on `*.workers.dev` availability, given the last check was 2026-06-09 (F-13).
5. Backup restorability in practice — the export formats, whether a D1/R2 restore has ever been rehearsed, and whether the agentbox cron host is redundant — plus the build provenance of the baked poker binary at agentbox pin `dd454474` (F-11, F-08).

## dreamlab-ai-website / premortem

# Post-mortem — dreamlab-ai-website estate

Scope: a serious data, custody or security failure twelve months out. The three root causes below are ranked hypotheses; every claim is drawn from the distilled material at the cited paths, and each is checkable against the code. Where a diagram did not assert order or concurrency, that is stated rather than assumed.

---

### R-1 — Poker house treasury paid out against unsecured, unreserved credit
- Topics: DW-10.2, DW-10.3, DW-10.6, DW-10.1
- Chain of events:
  1. A member of an asset table (DREAM or BLAKES7, configured at `dreamlab.toml:320`) submits a payment claim whose transaction ID is a non-hex 64-byte string; the table renews the claim's timestamp instead of rejecting it, so the "claim older than the grace period" refusal never fires.
  2. With debt claimed but unpaid, the member requests a buy-in; the table checks member balance, house balance less *settled* unpaid obligations, and the remaining daily payout cap — then deals **without reserving the active buy-in**.
  3. A second simultaneous buy-in (same member or a coordinated one) sees the same unreserved balance and cap; both are admitted. The native fixtures demonstrate exactly this: two 200-unit hands admitted against 200 units of house balance and a 200-unit cap.
  4. Finished hands emit Pay; the runner clears house debt on producer acceptance. Settled payouts leave the house while the buy-in transfers never arrive, and the claim-renewal keeps the debt inside its grace period indefinitely.
  5. Detection stays green: the live-hand probe prints "played and settled" on the next Offer even when the member still owes the transfer, and the profile provisioner exits 0 even on a rejected publication.
- Evidence: `nostr-bbs-poker-citizen/src/table.rs:399` (DEBT, non-hex txid renews claim timestamp), `table.rs:260` (grace-period refusal), `table.rs:521`, `table.rs:535` (checks then deal without reserving), `table.rs:225` (DEBT, two simultaneous buy-ins share balance and cap), `nostr-bbs-poker-citizen/src/run.rs:460` (debt cleared on producer acceptance), `scripts/seed/test-poker-citizen.mjs:84`, `scripts/seed/provision-poker-citizen.mjs:66`; fixtures stated in DW-10.2 ("admit two 200-unit hands against 200 units of house balance and a 200-unit cap… exposure without reservation, not a demonstrated breach of the payout cap").
- Warning signs visible today: both DW-10.2 DEBT nodes are tagged in the distillation; the double-admission fixture already exists; DW-10.6's probes are already tagged as certifying too much.
- Confidence: high for the defect (fixtures demonstrate admission against unreserved balance); inferred for deliberate multi-member exploitation — no live attack was executed in the recorded review.
- Severity: high

### R-2 — Member wallets auto-pay settlement fields the verifier never derives from the replayed game
- Topics: DW-10.4, DW-10.1
- Chain of events:
  1. A finished hand produces a Done reply carrying commitment, record, root, settlement amount and opponent.
  2. The browser matches its remembered nonce and replays the record; the engine verifier checks commitment, seed, actions, cards, result and deterministic house play.
  3. The verifier's Claim contains no settlement amount, destination or root; the payment path uses the **supplied** settlement, root and destination without deriving them from the replay.
  4. Verified status permits **automatic** payment; the wallet's only additional guard is that it has not already paid this hand. A Done whose replayed game is genuine but whose settlement/destination fields are wrong — from a buggy house build, a divergent dependency, or a compromised house seat key — is paid with no human confirmation.
  5. The house-side risk is elevated by the live pin skew: the agentbox house service runs kit pin `dd454474` while the browser client consumes `9c36b603`; the 42 poker files are byte-identical but "equal poker source does not prove all dependencies equal", and the house keys are held outside this repo's custody.
- Evidence: `nostr-bbs-forum-client/src/poker/live.rs:840`, `live.rs:911`, `live.rs:968` (DEBT: payment uses supplied settlement/root/destination), `live.rs:1036` (verified → automatic payment), `live.rs:554`, `nostr-bbs-poker/src/verify.rs:151` and DW-10.4's "What it shows" ("its Claim has no settlement amount, destination or supplied root"); pin skew `project/agentbox/lib/poker-citizen.nix:32` vs `.github/workflows/deploy.yml:130` (DRIFT-tagged).
- Warning signs visible today: the DEBT at `live.rs:968` and the DRIFT at `poker-citizen.nix:32` are both tagged in the current material; the recorded review explicitly did not execute a malicious browser payout, so this path is unexercised.
- Confidence: high that the binding between replay and payment fields is missing (source-stated); inferred that a wrong or malicious Done occurs in production.
- Severity: high

### R-3 — Forum-wide admin compromise from key material that is unrotated in git history or held by an automated server
- Topics: DW-03.4, DW-03.3, DW-07.2, DW-04.6
- Chain of events:
  1. Three seed scripts carrying a plaintext `ADMIN_PRIVKEY_HEX` were deleted on 2026-10-07, but the key remains in git history from `c0bcece` and was never rotated; whether it is still live "cannot be decided from this repo".
  2. Independently, the `visionclaw-server` key is simultaneously the governance publisher and the **primary admin** pubkey; the split is staged, not applied, and the auth-worker `ADMIN_PUBKEYS` Cloudflare secret cannot be rotated from this repo or CI.
  3. Anyone obtaining either key joins admin = static ∪ D1: whitelist promotion via `/api/whitelist/set-admin` (only last-admin demotion is blocked) and privileged event deletion that requires no author match — a direct mass-data-loss path.
  4. Recovery is slow by construction: rotation is a four- or five-location atomic change across `dreamlab.toml`, two wrangler `[vars]` copies, a Cloudflare secret pushable only by the `set-worker-secrets.yml` workflow, and the client mirror `VITE_ADMIN_PUBKEY`.
- Evidence: `CLOSEOUT-SECURITY-AUDIT.md:116` and `:300` (key in history, no rotation), `dreamlab.toml:39-72` (unsplit key, staged runbook), `docs/security/SECURITY_OVERVIEW.md:259` (static ∪ D1, first-user bootstrap), `:270` (set-admin, last-admin guard), `nostr-rust-forum/.../nip_handlers.rs:2468` (author-or-privileged deletion, no recipient-only path), DW-03.5's mirror set.
- Warning signs visible today: drift-register entry D7 ("Admin/governance key unsplit, staged not applied") and the DW-07.2 Debt on the unrotated history key are both open in the current material.
- Confidence: medium — the key material's presence and the unsplit design are certain; whether the history key is still privileged and is found and used within twelve months is inferred.
- Severity: high

---

### F-01 — Inbox pagination and the 25-item cap mark undisplayed messages as seen; they never resurface
- Topics: DW-09.4, DW-07.8
- Evidence: digest itemises the first 25 (`scripts/lib/digest.mjs:18`); wrap IDs are marked seen before verification and classification (`scripts/inbox-watch/run.mjs:328`); after relay acknowledgement **all** fetched IDs persist as seen (`run.mjs:373`, `run.mjs:389`, DEBT-tagged); separately the next cursor is "oldest second minus one" so unseen ties at the boundary can be skipped (`digest.mjs:74`, DEBT-tagged). The 30-message fixture "displays 25, marks all 30 seen and reports zero new on its next run".
- Failure: any day with more than 25 monitor copies — a mail burst after an outage, a campaign, a spam flood — permanently drops the tail of genuine client enquiries from the operator's view.
- Confidence: high (demonstrated by the recorded fixture).
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt)
- Severity: high

### F-02 — A sender-controlled probe phrase suppresses message classification
- Topics: DW-09.4, DW-07.8
- Evidence: "any content containing probe phrase is omitted as a synthetic probe" (`scripts/inbox-watch/run.mjs:200`, DEBT-tagged); DW-07.8 states "a sender-controlled probe phrase can also suppress classification".
- Failure: an enquiry (crafted, since the phrase lives in repo scripts, or coincidental) containing the probe phrase is excluded from the digest entirely — targeted silencing of the contact channel.
- Confidence: high for the behaviour; inferred for deliberate use by an outside sender.
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt)
- Severity: high

### F-03 — Confidential enquiry plaintext leaves to a vendor endpoint unless an opt-in env is set
- Topics: DW-09.5, DW-07.8, DW-03.8
- Evidence: topic annotation is on when `DREAMLAB_JEV=1` and local-only **only** when `DREAMLAB_JEV_TOPIC_LOCAL_ONLY=1` (`scripts/lib/enquiry-topic.mjs:27-32`, `:86`); without a local restriction the path is "vendor endpoint with API key — plaintext enquiry is request state" (`scripts/lib/jev.mjs:59`); DW-09.5 records that "its executable default is broader than the module's old local-only header comment". Dry-run additionally prints composed digests including enquiry excerpts (`run.mjs:365`).
- Failure: decrypted client PII (contact, signup, chat copies held by the monitor key) is transmitted to a third-party model endpoint, breaching any confidentiality expectation the E2EE ingress implies.
- Confidence: high for the code path; medium that the switch is on in production (Workers-side JEV flags are opted in per DW-03.8, but the inbox-watch switch is documented as off by default).
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift)
- Severity: high

### F-04 — The whole agent cohort holds the zone4 encryption key, and disabling encryption revokes nothing
- Topics: DW-03.1, DW-03.3, DW-04.9
- Evidence: `zone4` sets `agent_keys = true` (`forum-config/dreamlab.toml:149`); the `[[agents]]` roster has eleven entries; the roster comment for `website-info` states the exclusion exists precisely because "the agent cohort would receive the zone4 key" (`dreamlab.toml:407-412`); the master gate `[encryption].enabled` "false suppresses every zone's flag (keys already held still decrypt)" (`dreamlab.toml:33-37`).
- Failure: compromise of any single agent service (moderation-bot, poker-citizen, junkiejarvis, …) decrypts the encrypted DreamLab zone; flipping the master gate off after an incident does not revoke keys already held, including by retired agents.
- Confidence: high for the configuration facts; inferred for the compromise.
- Marked by authors: no (the website-info exclusion shows awareness; the sprawl and non-revocation are stated but untagged)
- Severity: high

### F-05 — The only backup path has no restore script and has never been exercised end-to-end
- Topics: DW-07.5, DW-03.11
- Evidence: "there is no corresponding restore script, only the raw D1/R2/KV export" (DW-07.5); the relay Durable Object manages its own SQLite schema at runtime (DW-03.10/DW-03.11) and does not appear in the backup enumeration; the NAS copy contains readable plaintext content by design.
- Failure: the incident that forces the first real restore discovers the export is unusable, partial (DO state absent), or stale — turning a contained failure into permanent forum data loss.
- Confidence: high for the absence of a restore path; inferred for DO-storage exclusion (derived from what the backup enumerates).
- Marked by authors: no
- Severity: high

### F-06 — First-user-is-admin bootstrap reopens after any reset or partial restore
- Topics: DW-04.6, DW-07.5
- Evidence: `GET /api/setup-status` reports `needsSetup` when no `is_admin=1` row exists and the first user becomes admin (`docs/security/SECURITY_OVERVIEW.md:259`); `reset-db` exists as an operator action (`scripts/seed/whitelist-admin-recipient.mjs:19-21`); there is no tested restore path (DW-07.5).
- Failure: a DB reset or a restore that drops the members/whitelist tables reopens the bootstrap race; whoever hits the fresh endpoint first owns the forum.
- Confidence: medium — each component is stated; the chain (bad restore → empty admin table → attacker wins the race) is inferred.
- Marked by authors: no
- Severity: high

### F-07 — Poker recovery loses the active game and can lose the settled book around pays
- Topics: DW-10.3
- Evidence: restart "reconstructs Citizen from Ledger and starts with empty active-hand maps" (`table.rs:189`, DEBT-tagged; the fixture shows an idle active hand surviving a month of ticks but disappearing after reconstruction); a finished hand "emits Done and optional Pay **before** its Persist effect" (`table.rs:846`); a failed file write "is logged without stopping subsequent dealing" (`run.rs:276`, DEBT-tagged).
- Failure: (a) a service restart mid-hand erases members' in-progress games while the ledger keeps obligations; (b) a crash or failed persist in the window after a Pay is emitted executes a transfer the reconstructed ledger has no record of — duplicate settlement attempts or an unreconcilable treasury book.
- Confidence: high for the code-shape; the recorded review induced no production crash or duplicate payment.
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt)
- Severity: high

### F-08 — House service and browser client run different kit commits
- Topics: DW-10.1
- Evidence: "service pin dd454474 differs from website client pin 9c36b603" (`project/agentbox/lib/poker-citizen.nix:32`, DRIFT-tagged) against `deploy.yml:130`; "the source comparison found no changes in the 42 poker files across the two pins; it did not certify the complete dependency graph or running binary provenance".
- Failure: a divergent dependency (serialisation, commitment scheme, settlement semantics) between the two pins produces records the client rejects — or accepts with different settlement meaning — breaking R-1/R-2 surfaces invisibly.
- Confidence: medium (the skew is certain; divergent behaviour is unproven).
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift)
- Severity: medium

### F-09 — Poker operational probes report success regardless of what actually happened
- Topics: DW-10.6
- Evidence: the profile provisioner resolves on the first OK without matching the kind-0 event ID (`scripts/seed/provision-poker-citizen.mjs:62`) and "rejected publication still resolves instead of returning a failed exit status" (`:66`, both DEBT-tagged); the live-hand probe does not compare the Done commitment against the remembered one and performs no engine replay or settlement confirmation (`scripts/seed/test-poker-citizen.mjs:86`, `:105`), and "next Offer prints played and settled even when the member still owes the transfer" (`:84`, DEBT-tagged).
- Failure: every custody defect in R-1/F-07 progresses while monitoring reports green; the first human signal is the missing money.
- Confidence: high (mocked executions reproduce each false green).
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt)
- Severity: medium

### F-10 — SEO-watch ledger and stderr persist unsanitised provider errors, including key echoes
- Topics: DW-08.6, DW-08.4
- Evidence: the report is scrubbed before writing (`scripts/seo-watch/run.mjs:241`) but "ledger serialises raw advice error — error log is also unsanitised" (`run.mjs:253`, DRIFT-tagged); "a synthetic provider-key echo reaches the ledger and stderr but not the report".
- Failure: a provider error echoing the API key leaks that credential into the persisted ledger and stderr on the operator host.
- Confidence: high (demonstrated with a synthetic echo).
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift)
- Severity: medium

### F-11 — The search-worker is blind to D1-promoted admins
- Topics: DW-04.6, DW-03.10
- Evidence: "the search-worker honours only its own `ADMIN_PUBKEYS` `[vars]` value — D1-promoted admins are not visible to it" (`docs/security/SECURITY_OVERVIEW.md:272`, stated as a known gap); its copy lives at `forum-config/deploy/search-worker.wrangler.toml:46`.
- Failure: an admin promoted through the documented D1 path cannot exercise admin rights on search, and the split resolution model gives different admins different powers per worker — privilege decisions made against one worker's view are wrong for another's.
- Confidence: high.
- Marked by authors: yes (documented as a known gap)
- Severity: medium

### F-12 — Talk-to-AI identity claims are explicitly unverified
- Topics: DW-04.3
- Evidence: `identityHint: pubkey` is "EXPLICITLY UNVERIFIED — no proof of possession" (`src/components/AIChatFab.tsx:431-432` comment, reproduced in the diagram); the DM rides an ephemeral session key.
- Failure: a visitor attributes their chat message to any victim's pubkey; the agent and the operator reading monitor copies act on a forged identity — free impersonation inside the advice channel.
- Confidence: high for the missing verification; inferred for downstream harm.
- Marked by authors: no (flagged in a code comment, not in the Drift/Debt register)
- Severity: medium

### F-13 — Chat replies have no request/reply correlation
- Topics: DW-04.3
- Evidence: "the DM transport carries no protocol-level correlation" (`AIChatFab.tsx:9-19`, `ChatMessage.lateForQuestion`); the estate closeout note leaves "adding request/reply correlation" open (`docs/IDENTITY-zones.md:208`).
- Failure: a late answer to a timed-out question is presented as the answer to whatever was asked most recently — an operator or client acts on advice given to a different question.
- Confidence: high for the mechanism; medium for business impact.
- Marked by authors: yes (Open)
- Severity: medium

### F-14 — The zone-cohort dual-accept invariant has a lockout regression precedent
- Topics: DW-03.1, DW-06.6
- Evidence: `required_cohorts` must stay dual-accept (zone id + legacy slug) or locked-zone members lose access; "dropping either arm collapsed legacy members to welcome-only in a 2026-07-20 regression" (`dreamlab.toml:116-119` comment; invariant I6, `IDENTITY-zones.md:178-179`).
- Failure: a future cohort cleanup or migration that drops the legacy arm again locks every legacy member out of the encrypted Minimoonoir/Family/DreamLab zones — the paying-client population of this forum.
- Confidence: medium (guard is documented, which lowers likelihood; impact is certain if breached).
- Marked by authors: yes (held as invariant I6 with recorded regression history)
- Severity: medium

### F-15 — The `[nip05]` section is excluded from every mirror check
- Topics: DW-03.6, DW-03.7
- Evidence: "`[nip05]` is still not compared at all: its `dreamlab.toml` section is 'still elided from source views' in the checker's own header" (`scripts/lib/config-mirrors.mjs:360-361`, Debt-tagged).
- Failure: a key rotation that updates the admin/agent mirrors leaves the NIP-05 handle assertion pointing at a stale pubkey; any client or agent that trusts the handle verification resolves the operator or an agent to the wrong (possibly retired, possibly compromised-history) key.
- Confidence: medium — the unchecked mirror is certain; where nip05 is consumed for trust is not shown in this material.
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt)
- Severity: medium

## knowledgegraph / critical

Findings from the six distillation files, ranked as instructed. Author markers are DRIFT, DEBT, OPEN, TENSION; anything else (NOTE, LIMIT, INVARIANT) is treated as unmarked.

### F-01 — The credential scan cannot see the bytes the site actually serves
- Topics: KG-05.3
- Evidence: The final gate is described as a credential scan (publish.yml:492-520), but the scanner "skips binary files" (check-secrets.sh:150) and gzip of selected files happens earlier in the same sequence (publish.yml:347-373). The material records the demonstrated consequence: "Actual gzip step hides a synthetic canary from this scan."
- Failure: A token or key in any file the pipeline gzips (contexts, machine artefacts, notes-derived output) passes the final gate and is published to narrativegoldmine.com; detection depends entirely on someone outside the pipeline.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DEBT, KG-05.3 note)

### F-02 — Privacy obligation "exclude private identifiers from public outputs" has no successor control on the live publish path
- Topics: KG-07.1, KG-07.2, KG-05.3
- Evidence: Baseline obligation 9 requires private identifiers be excluded from public outputs (BASELINE-narrativegoldmine.md:202-208, per KG-07.1). The successor path's gates are enumerated as required files, minimum counts, domain contracts (publish.yml:264-324), copy hashes, compression, browser smoke, credential scan and layout (publish.yml:341-373, 492-520) — none is stated to screen identifiers. The authors concede the general gap: "Those promises need acceptance evidence for the replacement publication path," and instruct not to treat "live status fields as fresh runtime evidence" (KG-07 intro, business note).
- Failure: A corpus or working page containing a person- or client-identifying string ships publicly; every gate passes because the content is well-formed; discovery is manual and after the fact.
- Confidence: medium, inferred — this is absence of a stated control; the material does not show whether screening exists outside the listed gates.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no (the general acceptance gap is admitted; this specific obligation is not flagged)

### F-03 — Generation markers attest bytes that later stages replace
- Topics: KG-05.3, KG-01.1
- Evidence: The compiler emits generation markers (mod.rs:990-1016, publish.yml:155-165), then the workflow overlays static contexts "AFTER marker creation" (publish.yml:257-262); the DEBT note states "overlay can change a manifest-listed context; Compiler receipt does not inventory the final website." Additionally, hash verification runs before gzip ("verify copied hashes, then gzip selected files," publish.yml:347-373), so the compressed bytes actually served are never re-verified — and byte receipts cannot certify semantics anyway, since "UUID/time and blank-node labels prevent blanket byte equality" (KG-07.3, manifest.py:149-150).
- Failure: A deployed context differs from its certificate (overlay swap or compressed-byte corruption); during an incident no one can reconstruct or prove what was actually served, and a tampered gzipped artefact passes every check.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: partly — yes (DEBT) for the overlay; the hash-before-gzip ordering and byte-vs-semantic limit are stated but unflagged.

### F-04 — Final gates compare counts, not identities: a wrong page can replace the right one and pass
- Topics: KG-05.3, KG-07.2
- Evidence: The retired gate "compares exact class identities" (release_gate.py:127-148); the successor's final notes gate "compares counts, not identities" and "Equal-count replacement passes the scoped probe" (check-urls.sh:115-126); presence checks likewise "do not verify every output identity" (check-urls.sh:90-95). KG-07.2 spells it out: "A notes-page count can agree even when the intended page is absent."
- Failure: A build or overlay mistake drops the intended page and keeps or emits a stale/duplicate one; counts match, gates are green, and the site silently serves the wrong page until a human notices.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DEBT, KG-05.3 and KG-07.2)

### F-05 — Nothing verifies that any deployment happened or what it contains
- Topics: KG-01.1, KG-04.6, KG-05.3
- Evidence: Three panels carry the same OPEN: "source configuration establishes the route; A deployed generation needs its own receipt" (KG-01.1); "workflow configuration does not prove a live deployment" (KG-04.6, publish.yml:528-533); "OPEN: verify each deployment" (KG-05.3).
- Failure: A failed deploy, partial push to gh-pages, or manual rollback leaves the live site on an unknown generation; during an incident no one can answer "which generation is narrativegoldmine.com serving," and stale content persists undetected.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (OPEN, three panels)

### F-06 — Build inputs the workflow consumes are invisible to its own trigger
- Topics: KG-05.5
- Evidence: The push trigger covers selected path filters from which "working/ and root assets/ absent from push list" (publish.yml:20-31, DEBT note at publish.yml:24-30), while the build "uses knowledge plus public working content" (publish.yml:155-183) and links "root assets linked into Quartz staging" (publish.yml:195).
- Failure: An urgent correction — or the removal of content that should never have been public — made only under working/ or root assets/ never triggers publication; the site silently serves the previous version indefinitely until an unrelated path changes or someone remembers the manual run.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DEBT)

### F-07 — The maintained viewer and the shipped viewer are different trees; fixes to the former never reach the site
- Topics: KG-04.6, KG-04.1
- Evidence: "Configured workflow builds visionGraph copy" (publish.yml:421-440), deploying to knowledgeGraph gh-pages / narrativegoldmine.com (publish.yml:528-533). The retained tree pins vowl-wasm v0.1.1 (explorer/modern/package.json:20) with six domains (explorer/modern/src/types/scope.ts:73-80); the shipped copy pins vowl-wasm v0.2.2 plus ontomark-graph v0.2.0 (WasmVOWL/modern/package.json:24-25) with nine domains (WasmVOWL/modern/src/types/scope.ts:76-86). Nothing states the two are synchronised.
- Failure: An engineer fixes a production graph bug in knowledgeGraph/explorer/modern, merges, and the public site is unchanged because the publisher builds visionGraph's tree; conversely the "domain contracts" gate (publish.yml:264-324) validates against a list that one of the two trees disagrees with.
- Confidence: medium, inferred — the divergence and separate build are stated; the "fixes don't ship" consequence follows directly from them.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no (adjacent INVARIANT/OPEN notes exist, but the divergence itself is not flagged as a defect)

### F-08 — The only scope guard checks counts, not shape: malformed scopes are publishable and can crash the renderer
- Topics: KG-04.3
- Evidence: assertScope enforces only upper bounds (1500 nodes / 4000 edges / focus 300) with "no finite-integer or non-negative check; no array-length or endpoint validation" (explorer/modern/src/types/scope.ts:144-157); the DEBT note states "Inconsistent overview arrays can be published," and the panel says stronger guarantees "must come from another boundary and are not supplied by this assertion." Truncation also occurs before the guard runs (scopeStore.ts:303, :172), and "Unknown domain decodes to overview" silently (scope.ts:206-218).
- Failure: A build emits a scope with NaN/negative counts or arrays shorter than the declared counts; the guard passes, the published graph route indexes missing entries and renders blank or throws for every visitor; a mistyped domain URL shows the overview with no error.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DEBT) for the numeric/array gaps; the silent unknown-domain fallback is unmarked.

### F-09 — Position buffer pool leaks on every transfer: the retained viewer's simulation dies after two frames
- Topics: KG-04.2, KG-04.6
- Evidence: "release requires original object identity … A real transferred return is a new object, two sends exhaust pool" (positionTransport.ts:76-82, with physics.worker.ts:166-183, 287-289 and useSimulation.ts:159-160); the business note confirms "The retained viewer can stop receiving positions." The deployed visionGraph pool "reclaims four successive transfers" (WasmVOWL/modern/src/workers/positionTransport.ts:49-94) — verified for four transfers only.
- Failure: Any deployment serving the retained knowledgeGraph viewer shows a graph that stops updating after two position transfers. The shipped visionGraph copy's behaviour beyond four transfers is unproven, so the same failure mode may exist there at a longer interval.
- Confidence: high for the mechanism; blast radius limited by which tree actually serves traffic (see F-07).
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DEBT) for the retained tree; the deployed-copy uncertainty is unmarked.

### F-10 — Page retrieval reports missing concepts when the index fails, and caches successes forever
- Topics: KG-04.4
- Evidence: On "no match or unavailable index" the service returns a "concept-exists stub, no page API attempt" (pageService.ts:267-274) — an index outage is presented as an unfinished concept; "malformed search document can throw during lookup" (pageService.ts:184-216); and "successful caches have no generation expiry," persisting "for the module lifetime" (pageService.ts:58-59, 127, 158-173, 233-243).
- Failure: A search-index outage, or one page missing from the index, makes the site tell readers that real published pages are un-written "unfinished concepts"; after a redeploy, existing sessions keep serving the previous generation's page content until a full app reload.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DEBT, both notes)

### F-11 — "Corpus page identity immutable" versus a whole-tree move, and permalinks that drop the node id
- Topics: KG-07.3, KG-04.1
- Evidence: ADR-2004 "forbids relocating corpus pages" (ADR-2004:34-39) while "The whole source tree was later archived" (logseq-era-2026-09-22/README.md:1-4) — flagged TENSION. Separately, the legacy redirect "preserves query string; path nodeId dropped" (router.tsx:48-50, 87-88), so the URL-level form of node identity is silently discarded.
- Failure: External bookmarks and third-party links to a specific node land on the generic graph without that node; any consumer relying on ADR-2004's identity/forever-in-place guarantee finds the corpus relocated into archive/ with no succession record and no corrected licence or identity mapping.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (TENSION) for the archive move; the redirect's identity loss is unmarked.

### F-12 — The licence map no longer matches the tree it claims to cover, and the deployed viewer is outside it entirely
- Topics: KG-01.2, KG-04.6
- Evidence: LICENSING.md grants AGPL to "pipeline/, root .github/, static/, docs/, examples/" (LICENSING.md:18), but the pages and pipeline "are now under archive/" and "the archive notice does not restate grants" (KG-01.2 DRIFT); the archived workflow likewise sits outside "root .github/" (archive/github-workflows/build.yml:1). The MIT grant and WebVOWL NOTICE cover knowledgeGraph explorer/ only (LICENSING.md:24, NOTICE:25), while the viewer actually published comes from visionGraph (F-07) — a tree no licence statement in this material covers.
- Failure: Anyone redistributing the repository or the site cannot determine the licence of the archived pipeline code or of the shipped viewer; AGPL/MIT/ODbL compliance analysis fails, and the project carries licence-breach exposure it believes is documented.
- Confidence: high for the path drift (stated); medium, inferred, for the visionGraph-copy coverage gap.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DRIFT) for the path drift; the deployed-copy gap is unmarked.

### F-13 — A different repository holds the only write path to this site's content
- Topics: KG-01.1, KG-05.3
- Evidence: The sole configured publisher is visionGraph's workflow, which "copies its own static/ tree into site-data, including contexts" (publish.yml:262) and deploys to "knowledgeGraph gh-pages … narrativegoldmine.com" (publish.yml:526); knowledgeGraph's own workflow is archived (archive/github-workflows/build.yml:1). Overlay content therefore originates in visionGraph, and no knowledgeGraph-side publish or verification path is shown.
- Failure: A change, misconfiguration or compromise in visionGraph (overlay files, dependency pins, workflow edits) alters narrativegoldmine.com with no corresponding knowledgeGraph commit and nothing that detects it; if visionGraph's workflow is disabled or deleted, knowledgeGraph cannot republish or roll back its own site.
- Confidence: medium, inferred — the architecture is stated; the absence of any alternative publish path is inferred from the sources listed.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no (the overlay's timing is flagged DEBT in KG-05.3; the authority concentration is not)

### F-14 — Agent grounding drifts from the published corpus with no alarm, and silently degrades
- Topics: KG-06.1, KG-06.2
- Evidence: The post-deploy corpus-sync is "Request delivery, not ingestion completion" (publish.yml:536); the reconciler separately builds "committed vault HEAD" (hp-loom-reconcile.sh:133) and compares Loom only against its own build's source SHA (:198) — never against the deployed site's generation. Loom with no commit marker serves "unattested degraded identity" and rejects during "in-flight promotion" (bundle.rs:117); unavailable adapters "degrade separately" (loom-facade lib.rs:84); a non-empty VAULT_ROOT silently selects a local corpus (corpus_source/mod.rs:190).
- Failure: The site publishes generation N while agents keep answering from N-1, from an unattested bundle, or — on one misconfigured instance with VAULT_ROOT set — from a stale local checkout, all while appearing healthy; a crash mid-promotion leaves the marker absent and agents serving degraded data with no operator-visible failure.
- Confidence: high (each mechanism is stated); the absence of cross-route consistency checking is the stated boundary, not an inference.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-15 — Four governing documents describe components that no longer exist
- Topics: KG-05.5, KG-05.3, KG-04.1, KG-06.2
- Evidence: build-and-gates.md — the `governing` doc for the publication topic — "still names the retired root workflow" (build-and-gates.md:3; the workflow is archived at archive/github-workflows/build.yml:1). Canon ADR-2013 "still names VisionClaw crates/vault as sole compiler" (ADR-2013:52-58) while the publisher fetches ontomark v0.7.0 (publish.yml:48-51, 98-117). The explorer architecture "still names an in-tree Rust crate" (docs/architecture/explorer.md:10) against the external vowl-wasm v0.1.1 pin (explorer/modern/package.json:20). ecosystem.md:102 still describes a static-Turtle fetch route with no current evidence (KG-06.2 DRIFT).
- Failure: During a publish incident the on-call follows build-and-gates.md to a workflow that does not run; engineers and auditors relying on the canon's compiler claim analyse the wrong toolchain; integrators build against a fetch route that does not exist; dependency review misses the real wasm dependency.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DRIFT, all four)

## Not judgeable from this material

1. Which viewer actually serves production traffic right now, and the deployed visionGraph pool's behaviour beyond four transfers — no deployment receipt exists (F-05, F-07, F-09).
2. Whether any boundary upstream of assertScope (the ontomark build or generators) validates scope-array lengths and consistency before publication (F-08).
3. The reconciler's schedule, alerting and last-success receipt — i.e., how stale agent grounding can become before anyone notices (F-14).
4. Whether private-identifier screening exists anywhere outside the gates enumerated in publish.yml, and the inventory of files the gzip step produces — the exact set the credential scan cannot see (F-01, F-02).
5. Licence and NOTICE coverage of the visionGraph WasmVOWL publication copy, and the overlay's conflict semantics — which file wins when visionGraph static/ and compiler output name the same context (F-03, F-12, F-13).

## knowledgegraph / premortem

# Post-mortem: client-loss incident (data/custody/security class)

Basis: the five KG documents only. Every claim below carries the citation the material itself gives; an engineer should re-verify each against the cited file and line before acting. Sequence and ordering are asserted only where a diagram asserts them (notably KG-04.4 and KG-05.3, which are sequence diagrams). Where I reason past the material, I say "inferred".

---

### R-1 — Credential scan cannot see gzipped artefacts; a secret in compressed output reaches the public site unscanned
- Topics: KG-05.3
- Chain of events: A credential (token, key, internal URL with auth) enters content that flows into the assembled `www` tree — through Quartz notes, machine outputs, or the tracked static contexts. The workflow copies machine files and verifies copied hashes (integrity check, not content inspection), then gzips selected files (`publish.yml:347-373`), then runs the browser smoke, credential scan and layout gate (`publish.yml:492-520`). The final scanner skips binary files (`check-secrets.sh:150`), and the material states the authors demonstrated that the gzip step hides a synthetic canary from this scan. The gzipped file is deployed to `knowledgeGraph gh-pages` / narrativegoldmine.com (`publish.yml:526-533`) with the credential publicly retrievable to anyone who inflates it — which the publishing viewer itself knows how to do (`fetchArtefact.ts:22-38`).
- Evidence: KG-05.3 note over V,S: "DEBT: final scanner skips binary files, check-secrets.sh:150; Actual gzip step hides a synthetic canary from this scan". The sequence asserts scan runs after gzip. `check-urls.sh:90-95,115-126` presence/count checks also do not inspect content.
- Warning signs visible today: The authors' own synthetic-canary demonstration — the gate hole is proven, not hypothetical. The business line in KG-05 already concedes "a successful step is not proof that every intended page and byte reached the site."
- Confidence: High that the scan gap exists exactly as described (the material demonstrates it); the entry path of an actual secret and its external discovery are inferred.
- Severity: high

### R-2 — Push-trigger blind spots silently serve a stale corpus: `working/` and root `assets/` edits never trigger publication, and no deployment is individually verified
- Topics: KG-05.5, KG-05.3, KG-07.2
- Chain of events: A correction the client depends on is committed under `working/` (or root `assets/`, which is linked into Quartz staging at `publish.yml:195`). The push filter at `publish.yml:24-31` does not match those paths, so no workflow runs. The public site — and any consumer reading the site rather than the repository — continues to serve the previous generation indefinitely, because "manual or matching-path runs can still include it" but nothing forces one. When a deploy eventually does run, the gates that "verify" it compare file presence and minimum counts (`check-urls.sh:90-95,115-126`), so an equal-count substitution or a missing intended page passes, and the material marks per-deployment verification as OPEN (`publish.yml:528-533`). The client acts on superseded facts. The compiler receipt does not help: the overlay after marker creation means the receipt "does not inventory the final website."
- Evidence: KG-05.5 GAP node: "DEBT: a working-only edit is outside this push filter, publish.yml:24-30; Manual or matching-path runs can still include it." TRIG node: "working/ and root assets/ absent from push list, publish.yml:20-31"; `publish.yml:195`. KG-05.3 note over S,G: "OPEN: verify each deployment; DEBT: presence and count checks do not verify every output identity, check-urls.sh:90-95,115-126." KG-07.2 LIMIT: "Equal-count replacement passes the scoped probe."
- Warning signs visible today: The DEBT and OPEN markers on the trigger filter and deployment verification; the KG-05 business statement that count-based checks cannot prove every intended page arrived.
- Confidence: High that the trigger omission and gate limits exist as stated; the specific client-impact scenario is inferred.
- Severity: high

### R-3 — Agent grounding diverges from the published corpus: corpus-sync is a request with no completion receipt, and Loom will serve an unattested bundle rather than fail
- Topics: KG-06.1, KG-06.2
- Chain of events: Publication completes and dispatches a corpus-sync event with the source SHA to VisionClaw (`publish.yml:536`) — the material states this is "Request delivery, not ingestion completion." VisionClaw's ingestion depends on GitHub listing (Trees API with Contents API fallback, `github.rs:60`) and on `GitHubSyncService` filtering page identities "under configured base paths" (`github_sync_service.rs:290`); independently, corpus-source selection silently chooses a local vault whenever `VAULT_ROOT` is nonempty (`mod.rs:190`). If the event is lost, the listing degrades, the filter misconfigures, or a stale local root wins, the agent's vault index stays on an older or partial generation. Loom then loads its local generation data; when the commit marker is absent it proceeds in "unattested degraded identity" mode rather than rejecting (`bundle.rs:117`), and unavailable adapters "degrade separately" (`loom/crates/loom-facade/src/lib.rs:84`). The agent answers the client from stale, partial or unattested data with no failure signal anywhere on the path. A reconciliation route exists (`hp-loom-reconcile.sh` builds from committed HEAD and compares served generation to source SHA), but the material does not state when, or whether, it is scheduled.
- Evidence: KG-06.1 DISPATCH node ("Request delivery, not ingestion completion", `publish.yml:536`); KG-06.2 nodes for selection (`mod.rs:190`), listing (`github.rs:60`), filtering (`github_sync_service.rs:290`), and the bundle branch "Absent marker: unattested degraded identity" (`bundle.rs:117`). KG-06 business line: "A successful website publication does not prove that a graph consumer has refreshed or that an agent serves the same generation."
- Warning signs visible today: The dispatch node's own qualifier; the unattested branch in the bundle flow; the KG-06 business statement above.
- Confidence: Medium — each mechanism is asserted, but the silent-failure chain (event loss or listing/filter fault with no compensating run of the reconcile script) is inferred.
- Severity: high

---

### F-01 — Final notes gate compares counts, not identities, so a replaced or missing page passes
- Topics: KG-07.2, KG-05.3
- Evidence: "DEBT: final notes gate compares counts, not identities, check-urls.sh:115-126; Equal-count replacement passes the scoped probe" (KG-07.2 LIMIT node); presence checks at `check-urls.sh:90-95`.
- Failure: A regeneration drops or substitutes the page a client needs; the count matches the previous build; the gate passes; the gap is discovered by the client.
- Confidence: High (the material states the probe result directly).
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt)
- Severity: high

### F-02 — Static-context overlay runs after marker creation, so the deployed site is not what the compiler receipt attests
- Topics: KG-05.3
- Evidence: "V->>V: overlay tracked static contexts AFTER marker creation, publish.yml:257-262"; note: "DEBT: overlay can change a manifest-listed context; Compiler receipt does not inventory the final website."
- Failure: A context file is modified via the overlay after attestation; the site serves bytes that no manifest or certificate accounts for; a later audit cannot reconcile deployed content to any receipt.
- Confidence: High (ordering asserted by the sequence diagram; custody consequence stated by the authors).
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt)
- Severity: high

### F-03 — `assertScope` checks only count ceilings: no finite-integer, non-negative, array-length or endpoint validation; inconsistent overview arrays can be published
- Topics: KG-04.3
- Evidence: GAP node, `explorer/modern/src/types/scope.ts:144-157`; "Inconsistent overview arrays can be published"; overview arrays are caller-supplied (`scopeStore.ts:512-543`); selection can truncate before the guard runs (`scopeStore.ts:172,303`).
- Failure: Caller-supplied overview data with NaN/negative coordinates or mismatched array lengths passes the 1500/4000/300 count checks and renders a wrong or broken graph; the visionGraph publishing tree has its own `scope.ts` (listed as a KG-04 source), so the same contract shape may govern the deployed site — inferred.
- Confidence: High for the retained viewer; inferred for the publishing copy.
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt)
- Severity: high

### F-04 — Retained viewer's buffer pool rejects returned transfer objects; two sends exhaust the pool and positions stop arriving
- Topics: KG-04.2
- Evidence: "DEBT: release requires original object identity, positionTransport.ts:76-82; A real transferred return is a new object, two sends exhaust pool"; a Node MessageChannel probe reproduces exhaustion. Business line: "The retained viewer can stop receiving positions."
- Failure: In any deployment of this tree, the second simulation round-trip exhausts the two-buffer pool and the graph freezes without an error surfaced to the user. Note the configured public site builds the visionGraph tree, whose pool reclaims transfers (KG-04.6) — so this defect does not transfer to production as currently configured.
- Confidence: High (probe-reproduced in the material).
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt)
- Severity: medium

### F-05 — Loom proceeds with "unattested degraded identity" when the commit marker is absent, instead of failing
- Topics: KG-06.2
- Evidence: VERIFY node: "Commit marker present: verify artefacts; Absent marker: unattested degraded identity; In-flight promotion: reject" (`loom/crates/loom-facade/src/bundle.rs:117`); adapters "degrade separately" (`lib.rs:84`).
- Failure: A partially transferred or tampered generation directory reaches Loom without a marker; Loom serves it as a degraded-but-active identity; the agent grounds client answers in data with no attestation and no hard failure.
- Confidence: High that the branch exists as described; the arrival of such a directory is inferred.
- Marked by authors: no
- Severity: high

### F-06 — Nonempty `VAULT_ROOT` silently selects a local vault, so a stale local copy can shadow GitHub without any signal
- Topics: KG-06.2
- Evidence: SELECT node: "Explicit local or github wins; Otherwise nonempty VAULT_ROOT selects local" (`src/services/corpus_source/mod.rs:190`).
- Failure: An operator leaves `VAULT_ROOT` set from earlier testing; VisionClaw quietly reads a months-old local vault while everyone believes it ingests from GitHub; the agent's answers diverge from the published corpus with no error or log stated anywhere in the material.
- Confidence: High on the selection behaviour; the misconfiguration scenario is inferred.
- Marked by authors: no
- Severity: high

### F-07 — Legacy ontology redirect preserves the query string but drops the path's nodeId
- Topics: KG-04.1
- Evidence: OLD node: "Legacy ontology path redirects to graph; query string preserved; path nodeId dropped, router.tsx:48-50,87-88"; narrative: "does not translate the path's node identity."
- Failure: Every legacy deep link shared in a client's records lands on the generic graph view; the referenced entity is effectively unrecoverable from the link — a silent loss of addressing, not an error page.
- Confidence: High (stated behaviour).
- Marked by authors: no
- Severity: medium

### F-08 — Unmatched or index-unavailable pages render as a "concept-exists stub", asserting existence for content that may not exist
- Topics: KG-04.4
- Evidence: "no match or unavailable index → return concept-exists stub, no page API attempt, pageService.ts:267-274"; business line: the viewer "can describe an unavailable page as an unfinished concept."
- Failure: A client is told a concept exists and is merely unfinished, when the actual condition is a missing document or an index outage; decisions get made on a fabricated state.

### F-09 — Successful fetches are cached for the module lifetime with no generation expiry
- Topics: KG-04.4
- Evidence: "DEBT: ... successful caches have no generation expiry, pageService.ts:58-59,127,158-173,233-243."

### F-10 — GitHub-raw fallback triggers on network error and invalid bodies, not just 404; a malformed search document can throw during lookup; index outage is indistinguishable from absent content
- Topics: KG-04.4
- Evidence: Fallback condition "non-OK, network error or invalid HTML-like body" → raw fetch (`pageService.ts:134-151`); "DEBT: malformed search document can throw during lookup, pageService.ts:184-216; Index outage is not distinguished from absent content."

### F-11 — Silent truncation inside selection: CSR edges stop at MAX_EDGES and expanded members are budget-truncated before any guard runs
- Topics: KG-04.3
- Evidence: CUT node: "Expanded members are budget-truncated, scopeStore.ts:303; CSR edges stop at MAX_EDGES, scopeStore.ts:172"; "Selection can truncate before the guard runs."
- Failure: A dense domain renders with edges and members silently dropped to fit budgets; the client reads the graph as complete; no count assertion fires because truncation happens upstream of `assertScope`.
- Confidence: High on the truncation points; the "read as complete" harm is inferred.

### F-12 — Agent vault index is built from a listing filtered under configured base paths, with an API fallback whose completeness is unstated
- Topics: KG-06.2
- Evidence: "GitHubSource lists Markdown, Trees API, Contents API fallback" (`github.rs:60`); "GitHubSyncService filters page identities under configured base paths; Full listing builds the vault index" (`github_sync_service.rs:290`). The material says nothing about the fallback's pagination or equivalence, or about validating the configured paths.
- Failure: A misconfigured base path, or a fallback listing that returns fewer entries, yields a partial vault index; the agent silently grounds in a subset of the corpus.
- Confidence: Low (inferred — the mechanisms are stated, the partial-index behaviour is not).

### F-13 — Retained viewer and publishing viewer are different trees (different dependency pins, six vs nine domains, different pool); findings and fixes do not transfer
- Topics: KG-04.6
- Evidence: Retained: vowl-wasm v0.1.1, six domains (`explorer/modern/package.json:20`, `types/scope.ts:73-80`); publisher: vowl-wasm v0.2.2, ontomark-graph v0.2.0, nine domains (`WasmVOWL/modern/package.json:24-25`, `types/scope.ts:76-86`); "INVARIANT: implementation findings belong to a source revision; OPEN: workflow configuration does not prove a live deployment."
- Failure: A defect is reproduced and fixed in the retained tree; the fix never reaches the deployed tree (or vice versa: a production defect is "closed" by a passing test on the wrong tree); the domain-count difference shows the two already disagree on scope.
- Confidence: High on the divergence; the wasted-fix scenario is inferred.

- Topics: KG-05.5

- Failure: During an incident, an operator follows the build guide and invokes a workflow that no longer exists, or assumes VisionClaw is the compiler when the publisher actually runs ontomark v0.7.0; remediation is applied to the wrong component and the outage extends.
- Confidence: High that the documents are stale; the response-failure scenario is inferred.

### F-15 — Archived OntoCast adapter, if ever executed again: dangling symlinks can escape staging, and fallback label-derived references can alias a rejected duplicate onto a retained entity
- Topics: KG-06.4
- Evidence: "DEBT: dangling symlink can escape staging; Dual type can cause partial output and failure, ontocast_import.py:142,156,256"; "DEBT: fallback label-derived references can alias a rejected duplicate to a retained entity, ontocast_import.py:119,151"; "blank-node descendants are omitted, ontocast_import.py:210". The material says the probes were reproduced ("as the retained synthetic probes demonstrate") but the adapter "remains historical code" and is no build input.

- Confidence: High that the defects exist in the archived code (probe-reproduced); any production exposure is inferred and currently foreclosed by the archive boundary.
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt)

## nostr-rust-forum / critical

### F-01 — Zone read and write authorization fails open when the D1 channel-zones lookup errors
- Topics: NF-17.2, NF-03.14, NF-03.12
- Evidence: NF-17.2 states "Absent row OR database/bind/query failure: None" → "None skips this zone gate, event reaches Deliver if other gates pass." The write-side gate at NF-03.12 shows the same mechanism: a None zone lookup yields UNSCOPED where "any whitelisted member may post." The zone memo (`cached_channel_zone`, nip_handlers.rs:2189) caches positive results only, so each D1 fault re-queries and re-fails. There is no distinction between "no row" (legitimately unscoped) and "query failed" (should deny).
- Failure: A transient D1 error during the `channel_zones` read lets an event from a hidden or write-restricted zone be delivered to a non-member (read) or stored from a non-authorised author (write) because the gate is skipped rather than failing closed.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no (NF-17.2 calls it a "source-only failure finding" but assigns no Tension/Debt/Drift marker)

### F-02 — SSRF guard is DNS-rebinding-vulnerable in the default deployment and has no timeout
- Topics: NF-07.6, NF-07.5
- Evidence: "without PREVIEW_ALLOWED_HOSTS the guard is denylist-only and DNS-rebinding-vulnerable" (ssrf.rs:13), and "there is NO wall-clock timeout — only redirect-count and body-size limits bound a fetch" (NF-07.6 N3). The template at nostr-bbs-preview-worker/wrangler.toml:13 declares only ALLOWED_ORIGIN, never PREVIEW_ALLOWED_HOSTS.
- Failure: An attacker crafts a URL whose DNS resolves to a Cloudflare-internal or metadata address after first resolution. The denylist cannot catch a rebinding attack because it checks the hostname/IP at validation time, not at connect time. No timeout means a slow-loris target holds the Worker open until the platform kills it.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (N4 acknowledges the DNS-rebinding gap in-code; N3 marks the timeout as DIVERGENCE; but neither is tracked in the anomaly register or findings ledger)

### F-03 — Recovery sheet injects unescaped display name into downloadable HTML containing the private key
- Topics: NF-22.2, NF-05.8
- Evidence: "format! builds HTML [with] raw display name, identity and recovery text" (recovery_sheet.rs:138). "Chrome executes the synthetic display-name marker in this formatter output… This is active-content injection in an exported secret-bearing document" (NF-22.2). The display name is validated only for trimmed byte length 2–50, not for HTML content (signup.rs:241).
- Failure: An attacker sets their display name to include a `<script>` tag. When they (or anyone viewing a sheet that includes their name) download the recovery HTML, the script executes in the browser context with access to the DOM containing the private key and QR code. A `fetch` to an attacker server exfiltrates the nsec.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no (the finding is stated but carries no Tension/Debt/Drift/Open marker)

### F-04 — Both passkey clients omit the required NIP-98 header on registration verify, so the ceremony cannot complete
- Topics: NF-23.1, NF-05.3, NF-02.3
- Evidence: "Forum registration verify fetch_json_post" → "JSON request has Content-Type, no NIP-98 Authorization" (passkey.rs:173, http.rs:156). "BBS registration verify also plain JSON POST" (bbs-client/passkey.rs:356). The worker at webauthn.rs:836 returns 401 before attestation validation when the header is missing.
- Failure: No user can complete passkey registration. The call returns 401 before any credential is stored. The entire passkey identity path is unreachable from either shipped client.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high (blocks a primary authentication path)
- Marked by authors: yes (NF-23 and NF-05.3 qualify the sequence)

### F-05 — Server stores a different PRF salt from the one used at creation, so login derives a different identity key
- Topics: NF-23.2, NF-02.3
- Evidence: Registration options generates random salt A (webauthn.rs:739), client derives identity using A (passkey.rs:153). Verify ignores submitted A and hashes a server secret, credential ID and pubkey into salt B (webauthn.rs:1021). Login options returns stored B (webauthn.rs:1128), client derives with B (passkey.rs:264). "Same-identity guarantee is broken."
- Failure: Even if F-04 is fixed, a user who registers and later logs in derives a different keypair on login than on registration. They appear as a different pubkey. Their forum identity, DMs, and zone keys are unreachable.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high (custody loss — the user cannot recover their own identity)
- Marked by authors: yes (NF-23.2, NF-02.3 notes)

### F-06 — Retained BBS signer handle produces valid signatures after logout or account switch
- Topics: NF-19.3, NF-19.1
- Evidence: "get_signer clones an Rc handle" (signer.rs:106). "Logout clears stored handles, external clones remain alive" (signer.rs:229). "Two native counterexamples produce strictly verified signatures with retained local signers after logout and account replacement" (NF-19.3). The composer captures the handle when send starts and later publishes the returned event with no current-account check (screens.rs:1385, screens.rs:1400).
- Failure: User A starts composing a message, then logs out. User B logs in on the same tab. The pending send completes with A's retained signer, publishing an event signed as A while the UI shows B as the active account. Conversely, a user who signs out on a shared machine leaves a window where a prior in-flight operation still publishes under their key.
- Confidence: high (confirmed by native test)
- Severity: high (custody: actions attributed to the wrong account)
- Marked by authors: no

### F-07 — Invite revocation always returns 404 because the client sends the code but the handler looks up by ID
- Topics: NF-25.4
- Evidence: "Create invite ID: 8 characters, code: 16 characters" (invites.rs:361). "Client sends code in revoke URL" (admin/invites.rs:175). "Handler looks up WHERE id = input" (invites.rs:733). "No row: 404 Invite not found" (invites.rs:742). "The SQL fixture misses by code and succeeds by ID."
- Failure: An operator attempts to revoke a leaked invite. The request always returns 404. The invite remains redeemable. There is no error message distinguishing "wrong identifier format" from "invite does not exist."
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium (security control is non-functional but the invite system is opt-in)
- Marked by authors: no

### F-08 — Zone encryption keys persist in IndexedDB after logout, leaking on shared machines
- Topics: NF-27.4
- Evidence: "Root hydration loads all stored zone keys, no account or relay selection" (zone_crypto/store.rs:187). "Logout clears identity and notifications, zone keys and sync state are not reset here" (auth/mod.rs:676). IndexedDB database is origin-scoped, keyed by zone and epoch (indexed_db.rs:25).
- Failure: User A logs out on a shared computer. User B logs in. B's session hydrates all zone keys from IndexedDB, including keys granted to A. B can now decrypt zone-encrypted messages that were intended only for A's cohort.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high (confidentiality bypass of end-to-end encryption)
- Marked by authors: no

### F-09 — Governance read endpoints verify NIP-98 with no membership check, so authenticated is anonymous
- Topics: NF-10.11 (KF-2, KF-2a), NF-06.10
- Evidence: "the governance READ endpoints verify a NIP-98 signature with no membership check, so authenticated is effectively anonymous, and the admin-gated reviewer aggregate can be reconstructed from the un-gated decisions read" (known-findings.md:23–24).
- Failure: Anyone with any Nostr key (not necessarily a forum member) can enumerate all governance cases, decisions, and agent activity. The admin-gated reviewer telemetry can be reconstructed by correlating the un-gated decision records, defeating the purpose of the admin gate.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium (information disclosure, not direct write access)
- Marked by authors: yes (KF-2, KF-2a in known-findings)

### F-10 — No static admin bootstrap in a by-the-book deployment because ADMIN_PUBKEYS is declared in no relevant template
- Topics: NF-08.7, NF-10.6 (DD3)
- Evidence: "ADMIN_PUBKEYS is DECLARED in exactly one wrangler template — the search worker" (nostr-bbs-search-worker/wrangler.toml:41), "yet is read by the auth worker (admin.rs:71) and the relay (auth.rs:185)." "The relay template asserts the opposite: there is no ADMIN_PUBKEYS reader in src/" (relay-worker/wrangler.toml:18-21). SETUP.md:147–151 never lists it.
- Failure: An operator follows SETUP.md exactly. No ADMIN_PUBKEYS is set on auth or relay. The first user to claim a username becomes the only path to admin (via whitelist set-admin), but there is no documented bootstrap mechanism. The system starts with zero admins and no way to create one through the documented flow.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high (operational lockout — no one can administer the system)
- Marked by authors: yes (DOC-DRIFT DD3, anomaly in NF-08.7 N4/N5)

### F-11 — Relay-only admin demotion leaves the auth-worker admin grant intact
- Topics: NF-08.7 (N3C), NF-25.3
- Evidence: "Relay set-admin changes only whitelist" (whitelist.rs:451). "Auth members can retain its grant" (admin.rs:90). NF-25.3 confirms: "whitelist.is_admin becomes 0" but "Auth members.is_admin remains 1, auth resolver still returns true."
- Failure: An operator demotes a compromised admin account through the relay admin panel. The relay stops treating them as admin, but the auth worker still does. The compromised account can still call auth-worker admin endpoints: register agents, provision agent cohorts, grant governance roles, and access admin-only membership data.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high (incomplete security revocation)
- Marked by authors: yes (N3C marked as DRIFT, NF-25.3 describes the mechanism)

### F-12 — Production edge runs an older kit with known-vulnerable crypto dependencies while main has the fixes
- Topics: NF-01.4, NF-06.18
- Evidence: "Production is not fixed: the edge still runs kit 9c36b60, which resolves crypto-bigint 0.5.5 and k256 0.13.4" while main (1b27f79) has "k256 and p256 are 0.14.0" fixing RUSTSEC-2026-0387. The edge kit pin is recorded at kit-compatibility-record.md:30.
- Failure: A vulnerability in the older crypto-bigint or k256 versions affects the live deployment. The fix exists on main but has not been deployed. The window between "fixed in the repo" and "fixed in production" is unbounded and untracked.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium (depends on the specific RUSTSEC severity, but the gap itself is confirmed)
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension in NF-01.4)

### F-13 — Trust promotion UPDATE writes the pre-read snapshot level, clobbering a concurrent admin grant
- Topics: NF-24.3, NF-03.8
- Evidence: "Snapshot is not TL3, compute TL2" (trust.rs:221, trust.rs:225). "UPDATE by pubkey only, writes TL2" (trust.rs:238). "Execution errors are ignored, returns planned level" (trust.rs:268). The concurrent admin grants TL3 between the read and write.
- Failure: An admin grants TL3 to a trusted moderator. Simultaneously, the moderator's activity triggers a promotion check that reads TL0, computes TL2, and overwrites the TL3 grant. The moderator loses their administrative trust level. The separate audit INSERT logs "TL0 to TL2" even though the actual transition was TL3→TL2.
- Confidence: medium (requires specific interleaving but is confirmed by SQLite fixture)
- Severity: medium (correctness: unwarranted privilege loss, no data exposure)
- Marked by authors: no (NF-24.3 describes it but no marker is applied)

### F-14 — Device-key list endpoint returns f64 timestamps/flags that the client parser rejects, so any non-empty device list fails
- Topics: NF-20.2
- Evidence: "Server row uses f64 for created_at and revoked" (devices.rs:326). "List serialises those rows as JSON" (devices.rs:470). "Client parser expects u64 and u8, non-empty server list fails" (utils/devices.rs:264). "Five native contract tests use the two extracted producer expressions and actual server/client types and parser."
- Failure: A user with even one registered device opens the device management page. The API returns data the client cannot parse. The device list appears empty or the page errors, making it impossible to see or revoke devices through the UI. An empty list parses fine, concealing the defect in testing.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium (security control UI is broken for the case that matters)
- Marked by authors: no

### F-15 — Extension signing path does not verify that the returned event matches the requested event or the expected author
- Topics: NF-19.4
- Evidence: "sign_event delegates to JS bridge, stored public key is not compared" (nip07.rs:109). "Await signEvent Promise, rejection becomes error" (nip07.rs:191). "Deserialize returned event, no signature or input-equivalence check" (nip07.rs:202). "Composer forwards result to relay" (screens.rs:1400).
- Failure: A browser extension (or a compromised extension) returns a completely different event than the one the user approved — different content, different author. The client publishes it without checking. The relay verifies the signature is valid for whatever event was returned, not that it matches what the user intended. An extension bug or supply-chain attack publishes arbitrary content under the user's session.
- Confidence: medium (source-traced; no malicious extension was exercised)
- Severity: high (arbitrary content publication under user's authority)
- Marked by authors: no

---

## Not judgeable from this material

1. **Actual D1 partial-failure behavior in production**: The zone fail-open (F-01) depends on whether Cloudflare D1 can return an error on one query while succeeding on another within the same request. I would need to observe live D1 behavior under load or partial outage to confirm the exploitability window.

2. **Real passkey authenticator PRF behavior across vendors**: The salt mismatch (F-05) and PRF evaluation requirements depend on how real authenticators (YubiKey, Apple, Android) handle the `prf` extension. No hardware authenticator was tested.

3. **Extension ecosystem risk surface for NF-05/19**: The severity of F-15 depends on which NIP-07 extensions users actually install and whether any have known account-switching or response-tampering behavior. I would need to survey the extension landscape.

4. **Production relay deployment configuration**: Whether the production edge actually ships with a blank RELAY_URL (NF-03.2, NF-08.8), whether ADMIN_PUBKEYS is set out-of-band (NF-08.7), and whether PUSH_ENABLED or ENCRYPTION_ENABLED are flipped — all of these change which findings are live versus dormant.

5. **Whether the `secp256k1-sys` cross-compile with `libc6-dev-i386` (NF-09.1 N3) introduces any side-channel or constant-time regression** in the wasm32 build compared to a native build — I would need to inspect the compiled artifact and benchmark timing variance.

## nostr-rust-forum / premortem

# Post-mortem — nostr-rust-forum production incident (twelve months on)

Method note for the engineers verifying this: every claim below is taken from the area distillation and its cited `path:line` stamps, at the revision each topic declares. Be aware that the topics do not all pin the same commit — main is `1b27f79d`, several audit topics pin `c39f41f`, the wallet topics pin the edge kit `9c36b60`, and NF-28 describes a **local upgrade branch** (`0b7979c`) that is explicitly not main and is excluded here. NF-01.4 records that production runs `9c36b60` while main is ahead, so line numbers cited at `1b27f79` may not match the edge exactly. Where I reason past what a diagram asserts, I say "inferred".

---

### R-1 — Passkey identity is unusable and, once "fixed", silently mints a different identity per login
- Topics: NF-23.1, NF-23.2, NF-02.3, NF-02.4, NF-05.3
- Chain of events:
  1. Operator promotes passkey signup/login (the public routes in NF-02.2).
  2. User completes the browser creation ceremony; the forum client POSTs `/auth/register/verify` as plain JSON — `Content-Type` but no NIP-98 `Authorization` (`nostr-bbs-forum-client/src/auth/passkey.rs:173` via `nostr-bbs-forum-client/src/auth/http.rs:156`). The BBS client does the same (`nostr-bbs-bbs-client/src/passkey.rs:356`).
  3. The worker requires the header and a matching author and returns 401 **before attestation validation** (`nostr-bbs-auth-worker/src/webauthn.rs:836`) — no user on either client can register.
  4. Under pressure a fix lands that adds the header only. Registration now succeeds. But the client derived the identity key using the **issued** random salt A (`passkey.rs:153`, salt from `webauthn.rs:739`), while the server stored a **different** salt B hashed from its own secret + credential ID + pubkey, ignoring the submitted salt (`webauthn.rs:1021`).
  5. On a later login, the server returns salt B (`webauthn.rs:1128`) and the client derives with B (`passkey.rs:264`) — a different key. Native controls prove A and B yield different roots even with identical PRF output (NF-23.2).
  6. The returning user authenticates as a *new* pubkey: not whitelisted, no cohorts, prior posts/DMs/pod orphaned. Account custody is lost silently, per user, at first re-login.
- Evidence: NF-23.1 (caller/handler mismatch, both clients, cited lines above); NF-23.2 (salt divergence, `webauthn.rs:739` vs `:1021` vs `:1128`); NF-02.3 note "Worker requires NIP-98, so this normal request is rejected" and "Same-identity login is not established (`webauthn.rs:739` `webauthn.rs:1021`)"; NF-05.3 qualification note; NF-23 dev note: "No live Worker, browser ceremony or authenticator ran" — the path was never proven end-to-end.
