- Warning signs visible today: the entire NF-23 audit exists; NF-02.3/02.4 carry the salt-mismatch notes; the estate review `2026-11-01-forum-passkey-contracts.md` is already linked from NF-02.3/04 and NF-05.3.
- Confidence: high — both contract breaks are asserted by the material; the step-4 "partial fix" unfold is inferred.
- Severity: high

### R-2 — Removing an administrator on the relay leaves their auth-worker admin authority intact
- Topics: NF-08.7, NF-25.3, NF-06.9, NF-02.6, NF-10.11 (KF-2)
- Chain of events:
  1. A staff admin departs compromised or under a cloud. The operator demotes them through the members UI, which sends `set-admin false` to the **relay** endpoint (`nostr-bbs-forum-client/src/admin/mod.rs:439`).
  2. The relay sets `whitelist.is_admin = 0` (`nostr-bbs-relay-worker/src/whitelist.rs:450-451`). Relay-side authority is now the static set union whitelist only (`nostr-bbs-relay-worker/src/auth.rs:185`, `:194`).
  3. The auth D1 `members.is_admin` row is untouched. The auth worker's `is_admin` is the union of three sources — static, relay whitelist, **and its own members table** — and returns true if any source grants (`nostr-bbs-auth-worker/src/admin.rs:57`, `:76`, `:90`, `:103`).
  4. The demoted admin retains full auth-worker admin: governance `agents/register` (`governance_api.rs:381`, INSERT OR REPLACE), `agents/provision` which **MERGEs the agent into the relay whitelist through the shared `RELAY_DB` binding** (`governance_api.rs:31`, `:466`), `roles/grant` (`governance_api.rs:670`), `admins/add` (`admins.rs:161`), `welcome/set-bot-key`.
  5. They register an agent pubkey they control and provision it — re-inserting themselves into forum membership and the agent registry on the relay the operator just cleaned.
  6. Reconnaissance is cheap because the governance read endpoints verify NIP-98 with **no membership check** — "authenticated is effectively anonymous" (KF-2, `docs/security/known-findings.md:23-24`).
- Evidence: NF-08.7 N3C ("REVOCATION GAP: relay set-admin changes only whitelist `whitelist.rs:451`; Auth members can retain its grant `admin.rs:90`"); NF-25.3 fixture executes the write and both store queries and traces both resolvers; NF-06.9 provisioning writes; NF-08.7 N4/N5 — `ADMIN_PUBKEYS` is declared in no auth/relay template and no SETUP step, so the *dynamic* sources are the only removable ones, which is exactly the broken path.
- Warning signs visible today: the N3C REVOCATION GAP label; the NF-25.3 fixture; KF-2 in the owned-findings table; the auth resolver's documented behaviour that "an error in one source is not a denial from the union".
- Confidence: high that the gap exists (asserted and fixture-demonstrated); the persistence chain is inferred but each step is a cited endpoint.
- Severity: high

### R-3 — Sealed-history purge reports success while plaintext rows remain on the relay
- Topics: NF-26.1, NF-26.3, NF-26.4, NF-26.6, NF-11.16 (context)
- Chain of events:
  1. Operator runs the ADR-2017 migration to move a zone's plaintext history into sealed envelopes, then purges the originals (NF-11.16, NF-12.11).
  2. Discovery can be incomplete: when a page's inclusive-timestamp cursor hits an ambiguous second, the engine steps past it and records `incomplete_seconds` (`nostr-bbs-zone-migrate/src/relay.rs:167`, `engine.rs:97`); the CLI only **prints a warning** (`cli.rs:462`). Sealing proceeds on the recorded set — plaintext messages can remain undiscovered.
  3. The purge gate requires confirmation and all entries Verified or Purged (`engine.rs:424`), re-fetches and opens the chunk (`engine.rs:470`), then asks the relay to delete the original IDs (`engine.rs:485`).
  4. Every ID **not skipped** is marked Purged, *including an unaccounted-for ID* (`engine.rs:499`); a deleted-count mismatch **only warns** (`engine.rs:503`).
  5. Later verification **excludes Purged entries**, so a zero-check run reports no failures (`engine.rs:366`).
  6. The relay-side delete handler compounds this: it returns 200 when no delete failed, its audit-receipt write error is discarded (`nostr-bbs-relay-worker/src/admin_events.rs:294`, `:307`; insertion itself can fail at `audit.rs:38`).
  7. Result: the operator's report says migration complete; plaintext kind-42 rows remain readable on the relay. Member experience degrades in parallel — readers fail on envelopes whose `zk` pubkey mismatches their held key (NF-26.2) and permalinks/suggestion queries by original ID find nothing (NF-26.6, `note_view.rs:106`). A client audit finds "encrypted" history still in plaintext.
- Evidence: every step above is a node in NF-26.1/26.3/26.4 with the cited engine/relay/audit lines; NF-26.1's own node text: "recorded-set success can leave plaintext undiscovered"; NF-26 business line: "A single success counter does not establish all four."
- Warning signs visible today: the entire NF-26 audit exists with synthetic fault cases; NF-12.11's note "key-tag binding gap remains: NF-26.2".
- Confidence: medium — the engine behaviour is asserted line-by-line; that a production run actually hit the incompleteness/partial-delete path is inferred.
- Severity: high

---

### F-01 — Active-content injection in the exported recovery sheet that contains the master key
- Topics: NF-22.2, NF-05.8
- Evidence: display name is length-checked only, HTML content not rejected (`signup.rs:241`); `format!` builds the downloadable HTML with the raw display name, identity and recovery text (`recovery_sheet.rs:138`); JS Blob/object URL (`recovery_sheet.rs:229`); the audit's isolated Chrome run **executed the synthetic display-name marker** in this formatter output; the same document contains the master nsec and connect fragment (`recovery_sheet.rs:288`).
- Failure: any path that lands attacker-chosen text in a display name yields script execution in a secret-bearing exported document; if that document runs with the app's origin it can read the remember-me session key persisted in localStorage (`auth/session.rs:156`). Origin inheritance and real exfiltration were not demonstrated — that part is inferred.
- Confidence: high (injection demonstrated; impact path inferred)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-02 — Case-sensitive pubkey guards let an agent decide its own case or supersede with a re-cased key
- Topics: NF-10.11 (KF-6), NF-06.15, NF-06.6, NF-06.13
- Evidence: "the self-review and original-signer guards compare pubkeys case-SENSITIVELY while NIP-98 returns the pubkey verbatim" (`docs/security/known-findings.md:29`); the same key can legitimately present in two casings and both verify (`governance_api.rs:98-100`, NF-06.15 N1); self-review is enforced at `governance.rs:1709` and on the supersession path `governance.rs:1806`; the registry canonicalised this class in NF-06.15 but the core aggregate was left open.
- Failure: a broker publishes a 31403 (or superseding 31403) under the upper-cased form of its own key; the case-sensitive guard fails to match it against the stored creator/signer, so the self-review and original-signer prohibitions do not fire.
- Confidence: high (comparison defect asserted; the one-step bypass is inferred from NF-06.15 N1)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt, KF-6 in the owned-findings table)

### F-03 — Invite revocation can never succeed: client sends the code, server matches the id
- Topics: NF-25.4, NF-02.2
- Evidence: create emits an 8-char id and a 16-char code (`invites.rs:361`); the client puts the **code** in the revoke URL (`admin/invites.rs:175`); the handler looks up `WHERE id = input` (`invites.rs:733`) and 404s (`invites.rs:742`); the SQL fixture misses by code and succeeds by id.
- Failure: a leaked or compromised invite cannot be revoked by any operator action; every outstanding invite is effectively immortal and redeemable.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-04 — `ENCRYPTION_ENABLED` is declared in no wrangler template: zones flagged encrypted silently store plaintext
- Topics: NF-08.2, NF-08.9, NF-03.12, NF-05.12
- Evidence: "DOC-DRIFT: ENCRYPTION_ENABLED is read from env at `zone_config.rs:86` but declared in NO wrangler.toml [vars] block in any worker … a deploy that forgets it silently leaves encryption OFF" (NF-08.2 N4); `is_encrypted = gate AND zone.encrypted AND NOT is_public_read` (`zone_config.rs:133`); with the gate off the relay's encrypted-zone ciphertext requirement never fires (NF-08.9) and accepts plaintext kind-42; clients gate on their own flag (`screens.rs:906-918`), so clients can disagree.
- Failure: operator sets `zone.encrypted = true` in `forum.toml`, never sets the undeclared relay var; members post plaintext into a zone everyone believes is end-to-end encrypted; the BBS reader on a differently-flagged build reads it in the clear.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DOC-DRIFT)

### F-05 — Hidden-zone channel metadata (kind 41) is withheld from history but delivered live
- Topics: NF-17.3, NF-03.14, NF-11.5
- Evidence: live `needs_gate` includes kinds 40 and 42, calendar and kanban, and **omits 41** (`broadcast.rs:76`); kind 41 skips zone context and a matching subscription gets the original event (`broadcast.rs:162`); the audit's native dispatcher "sends kind 41 despite a fixture authoriser that would withhold"; history-side kind 41 in a hidden zone is withheld from non-members (`nip_handlers.rs:2354`).
- Failure: a Hidden zone's channel create/rename metadata leaks to any subscriber with a matching live filter, defeating the "omitted entirely" design (`zone_config.rs:152`).
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-06 — The zone read gate fails open on a D1 error: absent binding and database failure share one outcome
- Topics: NF-17.2, NF-03.14, NF-11.17
- Evidence: "Absent row OR database/bind/query failure: None" (`trust.rs:584`); "None skips this zone gate; event reaches Deliver if other gates pass" (`nip_handlers.rs:2372`); the authors flag it as a "Source-only failure finding: distinguish unavailable from unbound".
- Failure: a transient D1 fault during live broadcast authorization causes kind-40/42 events from a Locked/Hidden zone to be delivered to non-member subscribers.
- Confidence: medium (fail-open logic asserted; requires a runtime DB fault the audit did not execute)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-07 — No admin bootstrap in a by-the-book deploy, and a real pubkey shipped as the default search admin
- Topics: NF-08.7, NF-07.2, NF-10.6 (DD3)
- Evidence: `ADMIN_PUBKEYS` is declared in exactly one template — the search worker — with "a REAL 64-hex pubkey" (`nostr-bbs-search-worker/wrangler.toml:41`); it is read by the auth worker (`admin.rs:71`) and the relay (`auth.rs:185`); the relay template asserts there is no reader (`nostr-bbs-relay-worker/wrangler.toml:18-21`); `SETUP.md:147-151` never lists it; `/ingest` is the admin-gated mutating route (`search-worker/src/auth.rs:86`).
- Failure: (a) fresh deployments have no static admin and no documented way to create one; (b) any deployment that does not override the search template grants the template key's holder admin ingest over the search index — an external party's key is the only non-generic default in any template.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DOC-DRIFT; DD3)

### F-08 — Device-key enrollment is broken in both directions even when enabled
- Topics: NF-20.2, NF-02.7, NF-22.4, NF-10.7 (S4)
- Evidence: both clients send only `device_pubkey` and `label` (`utils/devices.rs:144`, `:186`) while the handler requires `device_proof` and returns 400 (`devices.rs:385`); the server stores `created_at`/`revoked` as f64 (`devices.rs:326`) and serialises them (`devices.rs:470`) while the client parser expects u64/u8 (`utils/devices.rs:264`) — "non-empty server list fails"; five native contract tests confirm; empty lists pass, concealing the mismatch.
- Failure: an operator enabling `DEVICE_KEYS_ENABLED` as the lost-phone control gets a feature that cannot enroll a device and whose list view always errors on real data — a security control that silently does not work.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-09 — Zone-key rotation does not fence stale writers, and the relay cannot tell epochs apart
- Topics: NF-27.1, NF-08.9, NF-03.12
- Evidence: rotation is local latest+1 with grants sent afterwards, "no shared epoch activation barrier" (`zone_encryption.rs:273`); a stale writer "still holds older latest key" (`store.rs:359`) and encrypts with the locally supplied key (`mod.rs:568`); the relay "checks ciphertext and zk shape, no current epoch or key binding here" (`zone_config.rs:208`) and "cannot authenticate ciphertext" (`zone_config.rs:204-207`); "old key holder can decrypt obtained ciphertext" (`mod.rs:465`).
- Failure: rotation used to eject a member does not stop remaining members' stale tabs/BBS clients from publishing new content under the old epoch; the relay accepts it; the ejected key holder who obtains the ciphertext reads the zone's new content. The read-ACL cache (60 s, NF-11.17) is, in the material's own words, "a separate boundary".
- Confidence: medium (each node asserted; end-to-end leak inferred)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-10 — Governance case content leaves the deployment to a third-party model endpoint by default
- Topics: NF-06.19, NF-02.2, NF-08.8 (N8), NF-07.9
- Evidence: the checked-in auth manifest sets `JEV_RATIONALE_CHECK = "1"` (`nostr-bbs-auth-worker/wrangler.toml:53-58`); the handler reads the case row's title, subject, summary, tier and task properties (`rationale_check.rs:279`); `local_only` defaults **off**, "so the proxy or TypeSafe may answer" (`rationale_check.rs:38`, `:303`); all three templates ship the Jev flags on pointing at TypeSafe `jev-latest` with `TYPESAFE_API_KEY` (NF-08.8 N8); the overlay deploy "run 41028875471, with no receipt for the auth Worker's secret".
- Failure: appeal and moderation case summaries — kind-31402 content — are transmitted to an external endpoint under operators who never evaluated the switch; discovery of this egress is itself a client-ending data event.
- Confidence: high (configuration state asserted; the unaware-operator incident is inferred)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Open)

### F-11 — The BBS backup exports whatever key is in storage, not the account on screen
- Topics: NF-19.5, NF-19.1, NF-05.11
- Evidence: backup is offered whenever `local_secret_hex` returns a value (`screens.rs:1972`); it only requires *some* active public key (`signer.rs:291`) and reads the forum secret from storage "no active-public-key comparison" (`signer.rs:295`); audit: "With forum key A still stored and local signer B installed, the backup path can show A while the account label names B", and "an in-memory pasted key has no later backup route when forum storage is empty".
- Failure: a user active on an imported/pasted key backs up a sheet for a different key and later "restores" the wrong identity — irreversible custody loss with a green confirmation on screen.
- Confidence: medium (source-traced; authors state browser reproduction remains open)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-12 — Invite redemption's read-modify-write silently erases a concurrent cohort grant
- Topics: NF-25.2, NF-08.3
- Evidence: invite reads cohorts `members` (`invites.rs:261`); a concurrent shared-SQL grant commits `members + zoneB` (`whitelist_sql.rs:73`); the invite merges its stale snapshot with `zoneA` (`invites.rs:268`) and replaces the stored cohorts — `zoneB` is lost (`invites.rs:271`); the serial control keeps all three.
- Failure: an admin grants a member a zone cohort at the moment the member redeems an invite; the grant vanishes; the member loses read/write access to that zone with no error anywhere.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-13 — Wallet delivery ACK gates pending persistence: a sent-but-unACKed spend leaves no record
- Topics: NF-15.4, NF-15.3
- Evidence: "DEBT: zero ACKs says nothing was sent; timeout cannot establish non-delivery" (`wallet/mod.rs:661`); "Only after ACK insert pending and persist; no account epoch or early coin reservation" (`wallet/mod.rs:673`); publish counts matching accepted event IDs (`wallet/relays.rs:135`).
- Failure: user sends a tip; the extension signs and the relay accepts, but the ACK is missed; the client reports failure and stores nothing; the coin is spent on-chain while the wallet still lists it — the next build double-spends the input or the balance is silently wrong. (Chains here are the pinned testnets, which bounds the blast radius.)
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DEBT)

### F-14 — Member deletion is partial, untombstoned, and reported as complete
- Topics: NF-25.5, NF-25.1
- Evidence: relay deletion must succeed (`admin/mod.rs:488`) but pod deprovision (`:506`) and auth member cleanup (`:524`) failures only log warnings and the UI reports "Deleted" (`:533`); `DELETE` leaves no removed-state tombstone (`user_admin.rs:199`); "The storage fixture can grant a new row after deletion … without demonstrating unauthorised re-admission".
- Failure: a removed member is re-admitted through a still-valid invite (compounding F-03, which makes that invite unrevokable), and their pod data survives a "deleted" outcome — retention beyond the deletion the client was promised.
- Confidence: medium (components asserted; the re-admission combination is inferred)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-15 — Promotion's unguarded write overwrites a concurrent TL3 grant and audits the wrong transition
- Topics: NF-24.3, NF-03.8, NF-03.7
- Evidence: `check_promotion` reads TL0 with qualifying counters (`trust.rs:207`), computes TL2 because the snapshot is not TL3 (`trust.rs:221`, `:225`), UPDATEs by pubkey only (`trust.rs:238`), then a separate INSERT claims TL0→TL2 (`trust.rs:252`); execution errors are ignored and the planned level returned (`trust.rs:268`); NF-03.8 already carries "its unguarded write can overwrite a concurrent TL3 grant".
- Failure: an admin grants TL3 (which carries kind-5 delete-others privilege, NF-03.7) concurrently with an activity-triggered promotion; the promotion writes TL2 over it, the audit row records a transition that did not reflect the granted state, and the member silently loses the granted privilege.
- Confidence: medium (SQLite fixtures cover the schedule; no live Worker race executed)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

## ontomark / critical

### F-01 — The legacy VisionClaw producer still runs in production with the fail-open redactor, body-only credential scan, first-vault-only gate and non-atomic publish
- Topics: OM-00.3, OM-00.2, OM-05.5
- Evidence: OM-00.3 EXT states "VisionClaw's vault build job still ships S1.1, S1.2, S1.5, S2.3 and S3.1 unchanged until VC-60 retires under its four gates" (`ADR-OM-005-estate-alignment-one-producer-explicit-projections.md:11`), where S1.1 is "prose redactor failed OPEN on a regex compile error" (`vault/src/projection.rs:230`), S1.2 "credential scan was body-only but render publishes frontmatter" (`vault/src/build/publish.rs:159-160,196`), S1.5 "gate/conflicts first vault only" (`vault/src/main.rs:595-598`), S2.3 non-atomic `publish_out` (`vault/src/build/publish.rs:284`). OM-05.5 confirms the live consumer: `cargo build -p vault`, `vault --repo vault-source build --vault knowledge --out output/vault`, output feeds `pack-pod-resources.py` (`ontology-publish.yml:167-175,247`), and n2 says ADR-OM-005 decision 2 retires the job "gated, so it keeps running".
- Failure: A regex compile failure in the legacy redactor silently publishes private prose to the pod-resource pipeline; a credential placed in a frontmatter value reaches the published render unscanned; working-only faults are invisible because the gate reads only the first vault.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (EXTERNAL, in OM-00.3 and OM-05.5)

### F-02 — A private working note's name survives verbatim in a public knowledge page title
- Topics: OM-11.2, OM-06.2
- Evidence: OM-11.2 GAP: "TENSION: bare private working name can survive in a public knowledge title — knowledge redactor is not the union redactor (`projection.rs:750,850,890`)". The probe showed the same private working-note title masked in a public working title but retained in a public knowledge title, in both public and machine builds; only the private body canary stayed absent.
- Failure: A user drafts a sensitive project name as a working note; the knowledge page whose title mentions it publishes that name to the public site and the machine bundle. Neither `--scope public` nor `--scope machine` prevents it, because the knowledge projection is frozen before the union marker set is built.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension)

### F-03 — The held-page identity set is narrower than the hold-back rule, so deep-held and `misc/` pages escape the collision gate and the machine twin rule
- Topics: OM-03.3, OM-02.5, OM-06.2, OM-00.1
- Evidence: `is_unpublished` covers `_misc` at top level only (`input/page.rs:47,641`), while `held_back` applies `UNPUBLISHED_DIRS` at any depth plus `HELD_BACK_DIRS` `misc` (`build/publish.rs:134,138-148`); OM-03.3 states "the census and `excluded_identities_of` still use the top-level-only `is_unpublished` (`projection.rs:114,231`), which is the divergence that remains". OM-02.5 says `excluded_identities_of` "is what stops a link from publishing a held page: its values join `private_ids` (`projection.rs:543`)"; in machine scope "union_extra = held identities of both vaults (`projection.rs:754-755`)" feeds the collision check that refuses "a public identity also private in either vault" (`projection.rs:776`).
- Failure: A page deliberately held at `misc/foo.md` or `_misc/nested/foo.md` is absent from the identity set. Under `--scope machine` a public working twin of that held knowledge page is neither refused nor yielded — the held page's identity publishes through the working side. In a plain build its identity is missing from the collision probe.
- Confidence: medium, inferred (the divergence is stated; the twin-gate consequence is assembled from OM-02.5 and OM-06.2)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: partially — the divergence itself is marked (OM-00.1 H1 DIVERGENCE, ER-18 note); the publication consequence is not

### F-04 — The public Graph adapter silently drops unsupported shapes and unreadable definitions before the refusal check, breaking the ADR-OM-004 refusal promise at that boundary
- Topics: OM-10.1, OM-04.3
- Evidence: OM-10.1 DROP: "TENSION: unknown shapes and unreadable definitions can be omitted before refusal check (`ontomark-compile/src/reason.rs:283,401`)" — the probe showed "a raw universal restriction produced zero compiler refusals, while the equivalent typed axiom was refused", and "a malformed graph definition was also omitted". OM-04.3 repeats it as AUDIT LIMIT: "extraction precedes the shared refusal check".
- Failure: A caller feeding the Graph API an unsupported axiom gets a silently smaller closure: the published `ontology-inferred.ttl` omits subsumptions with no refusal, no warning and no count, while the same axiom through the typed facade is refused. The mode/capability signals (`classify.rs:113-114`) cannot report what extraction never handed them.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension in OM-10.1; Audit limit in OM-04.3)

### F-05 — Vocabulary-declared relations beyond the built-in twelve are silently dropped from every relation-bearing artefact
- Topics: OM-02.1, OM-04.2, OM-04.5, OM-04.6, OM-00.1, OM-01.6
- Evidence: `Corpus::build` extracts relations only from "12 RELATION_KEYS plus disjointWith" (`ir/model.rs:363-373`, `ir/model.rs:36`); the vocabulary accepts arbitrary relation keys ("is_known_key = relation OR scalar OR reserved", `input/vocabulary.rs:682-686`) and Turtle declares vocabulary-driven properties (`turtle.rs:678-691`); the stub list is "hardcoded 13 names… a relation the vocabulary adds is declared but never stubbed" (`turtle.rs:446-460`); WebVOWL iterates a hardcoded `REL_MAP` (`webvowl.rs:30-42`); OM-00.1 records four hand-kept orderings of the same twelve.
- Failure: The vocabulary adds `influences`; pages validate and carry the key; `Corpus::build` never extracts it. The property appears in the Turtle header with zero assertions and is absent from scaffold, page API, OKF concepts and tiers — silent, unreported data loss into every consumer, discovered only by a reader noticing missing edges.
- Confidence: medium, inferred (the mechanism is stated; whether production vocabularies use extra relations is not)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: partially — the table duplication and stub-list divergence are marked (OM-00.1 R4/R5 DIVERGENCE); the silent drop from all artefacts is not

### F-06 — Governed application can publish a change that is not the approved proposal, with a receipt that names a different page or case
- Topics: OM-07.1, OM-07.2, OM-07.3
- Evidence: OM-07.1 X: "explicit promote checks request tag, but lacks approve's proposal-digest equality check (`local-process-manager.js:444,400`)". OM-07.2 GAP: "staged title B can write B.md while adapter receipt names proposal page A (`ontology-apply.js:570` vs `write/create.rs:258`)" and "the creation CLI receives no proposal object to verify". OM-07.3 OTHER: "writer can add a commit after count; push then includes it (`ontology-apply.js:872,879`)"; LEDGER: attestation "failure is returned after write (`ontology-apply.js:742,900`)".
- Failure: A promote outcome applies proposal X while the signed decision names case Y; the ledger attests a digest for a page that was not the one written; an unrelated commit rides the governance push. The published corpus and its audit trail disagree, with no reconciliation step.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension, Drift, Open across OM-07)

### F-07 — The ingest review bundle is replaced non-atomically under a stable ID, with no snapshots retained and removals invisible to the host
- Topics: OM-08.3
- Evidence: "Remove existing manifest directory (`ingest_cmd.rs:771,772`)" then write pages and evidence; B TENSION: "incomplete uncached rerun can replace complete bundle under same ID (`ingest_cmd.rs:723,769,772`)"; Open: "Complete source snapshots, prior target bytes and model requests are not retained in the bundle (`ingest_cmd.rs:802-838`). Replay requires matching inputs from outside it"; DRIFT: the endpoint is in the parser and response cache but "omitted from request ID (`ingest_cmd.rs:606`)"; LOST: removed-source classification is implemented in the library (`ledger.rs:224,255`) but "dropped from host result (`ingest_cmd.rs:841,843`)".
- Failure: A network failure mid-rerun deletes a complete review bundle and leaves a partial one under the same ID; the accepted evidence and model requests needed to reconstruct it were never stored; an operator switching endpoints silently overwrites a bundle produced by a different model. A crash during the remove/write window loses the bundle outright.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension, Drift, Open)

### F-08 — Certificate evidence is written outside the bundle transaction, before the consistency gate, and is orphaned or destroyed by the promote
- Topics: OM-03.1, OM-03.8, OM-10.2, OM-04.1
- Evidence: OM-03.1: "certificate files are written directly to certificate_out before consistency, no bundle transaction covers them (`mod.rs:715-717`)". OM-10.2: writes precede the consistency refusal — "old bundle can retain new certificate beside it (`mod.rs:744`)" — and LOST: "certificate inside output directory is removed by successful replacement (`mod.rs:311,313,316`)"; output overlap "is not refused" (`mod.rs:715-717`). OM-04.1 NC DRIFT: C3 lists no certificate artefact (`sovereign-corpus-contracts.md:111`) while ADR-OM-009 decides every published closure carries one; OM-03.8 Open: without the flag "nothing written, nothing checked" (`mod.rs:730`).
- Failure: An unsatisfiable build leaves a fresh certificate beside the previous bundle — a reader re-verifies a warrant for a closure that was never published, or pairs the wrong warrant with the live bundle. A successful promote into a directory overlapping `certificate_out` silently deletes the certificate. A bundle built without the flag carries no warrant at all.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension in OM-03.1/OM-10.2; Drift and Open in OM-04.1/OM-03.8)

### F-09 — `publish_out` runs after the promote, so a failure there leaves the new bundle beside the previous publication tree as two live generations
- Topics: OM-03.5, OM-05.5
- Evidence: OM-03.5 N2: "write_to runs AFTER promote, so a failure there leaves the NEW bundle beside the PREVIOUS publication tree that swap_in kept, the two no longer one generation (`build/publish.rs:356`, `mod.rs:1018-1020`)". The production consumer uses exactly this path: `ontomark build --vault all --out site-data --publish-out quartz/content` (`publish.yml:165-171`).
- Failure: The publish-out write fails on disk full or permissions after the bundle promoted: the public site serves markdown of generation N−1 beside `data/` of generation N, with indexes, digests and Turtle disagreeing across the two trees, and nothing rolls either side back.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Divergence)

### F-10 — `content_digest` and the generation stamp omit the profile, roster and attribution inputs, and trust caller-supplied commit labels
- Topics: OM-11.3, OM-03.6, OM-03.2
- Evidence: OM-11.3 MISS: "profile is absent from content_digest inputs; same digest can accompany changed output (`mod.rs:963-983`)" — the probe changed a page namespace, preserved generation ID and content digest, and changed page IRIs and artefact hashes; TRUST: "Caller-provided commit and dirty flag are accepted directly (`mod.rs:925,926`)". OM-03.6 N1: the roster shapes Turtle and the page API but is not a digest input; N3: the roster is read from `vault_root.parent()/ontology` while dirtiness reads `repo_root/ontology`, "two roots for one directory"; OM-03.2 Debt: for a flat vault the roster is looked up one directory above the repository.
- Failure: A consumer cache keyed on the digests (as C3 itself advises, `sovereign-corpus-contracts.md:148-152`) serves stale vectors or stale Turtle after a profile or roster change; an audit cannot reconstruct which configuration produced a bundle; a wrong commit label is stamped unchallenged; a flat-layout build silently reads the wrong directory's roster or none.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension in OM-11.3; Divergence and Debt in OM-03.6/OM-03.2)

### F-11 — In the Obsidian host, persistence and indexing are separate events and abort is not enforced at the boundaries
- Topics: OM-09.4, OM-09.3, OM-06.4
- Evidence: OM-09.4 FAIL: "rejection can follow persisted write; upsert is outside write-error catch (`api.ts:248,253`)"; WAIT: "Timeout races host request; no abort handle (`ingest-transport.ts:85,91,92`)" — "The timeout stops waiting; it does not recall a request"; "no second abort check after optional Jev judging and before confirmation"; OM-09.3 COLL: "two blocks in one note collide (`sparql-block.ts:71`)" on a channel keyed by note path, so "latest request per channel wins" can render one block's result in another.
- Failure: A user confirms an edit, the file is written, the index update fails and the plugin reports failure — the user retries and double-applies or abandons a change that did land. A timed-out model request keeps running and its late response surfaces in a later flow. Two SPARQL blocks in one note overwrite each other's results.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension in OM-09.3/09.4; Open in OM-06.4)

### F-12 — A negated relation passes the evidence check, and dropped model chunks leave `complete` true
- Topics: OM-08.2
- Evidence: N TENSION: "mention check does not prove relation meaning; a negated relation can pass (`accept.rs:533`)" — the probe's negative sentence grounded an accepted positive relation. Unmarked part: a malformed response is a "dropped chunk only (`ingest_cmd.rs:735`)" and "No skipped pair or stop added — complete can remain true (`ingest_cmd.rs:821`)".
- Failure: The model replies "X does not influence Y"; the mention check finds the value in the quote and the negated relation enters the proposal as positive. A whole chunk's reply fails to parse and vanishes while the run summary reports the source finished and complete, so the reviewer never learns assertions were lost.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: partially — the negation gap is marked (Tension); the complete-stays-true accounting is stated but unmarked

### F-13 — The independent checker accepts an empty derivation file, and the certificate writer's four sequential writes can leave mixed files
- Topics: OM-10.3
- Evidence: OMIT: "omitting all steps still passes; soundness does not establish closure completeness (`oo_cert.rs:55,56`)" — the checker "accepted an empty derivation file after a one-step certificate was truncated"; MIX: "failed second write can leave new assertions with old digest (`rdfs-certificate/src/lib.rs:589,590`)" — four sequential writes, no atomic replacement; scope.tsv and asserted.sha256 are "not inputs to this checker invocation".
- Failure: A truncated derivations.tsv ships and passes the pinned checker, so a "verified" bundle's warrant covers none of its inferences; a crash mid-write leaves asserted.tsv and asserted.sha256 disagreeing, failing every later verification with no recovery path.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension, twice)

### F-14 — The same `--vault` word means a different scope per verb, read verbs never exit 2, and `retrieve`/`tree`/`edit` silently read knowledge-only under `all`
- Topics: OM-05.1, OM-05.2, OM-05.3
- Evidence: "`repair` defaults to `all` while `validate`, `gate`, `conflicts`, `build` and the query verbs default to `knowledge` (`main.rs:152,169,182,382`)"; n2 DIVERGENCE: "retrieve, tree and edit call `.next()` on the loaded vaults, so under all they read knowledge alone (`main.rs:923-926,948-951,971-974`)"; OM-05.3 n2: "validate, gate and conflicts never return 2 from their own code… a refusal is distinguishable only on the writing verbs (`main.rs:675,738,782`)"; `--stats` is documented as "print the counts" but only forces the JSON report (`main.rs:394,875`).
- Failure: An operator runs `retrieve`/`tree` with `--vault all` to inspect working content, gets knowledge-only answers and concludes working is clean; automation that treats exit 2 as "refused" cannot distinguish a validate refusal from a crash; a script's `--stats` output is JSON it did not ask for.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Divergence, Doc-drift)

### F-15 — Source fetching has no locality or budget policy in any mode, and "plan-only" is not network-free
- Topics: OM-08.1
- Evidence: The model-endpoint locality check is skipped for plan-only ("Unless plan-only, check model endpoint declared local, local-looking host or opt-in", `ingest_cmd.rs:508`; `config.rs:141,231`), yet "Plan-only returns after reading sources" (`ingest_cmd.rs:627`) — sources are still fetched: "URL source: HEAD then GET, five redirects, bounded body (`url.rs:57,105,124,154`)"; "Discovery's cap is per source; explicit targets are separate"; the "Why" concedes the budgets "constrain model requests, not the total cost of acquiring and rendering sources".
- Failure: An operator treats `--plan-only` as the offline dry-run and runs it with untrusted URLs: the process performs server-side HEAD/GET with redirect following against arbitrary hosts, an internal-network probing channel the endpoint-locality policy exists to prevent for model calls; explicit target lists are unbounded in count.
- Confidence: medium, inferred (depends on ingest running host-side with operator-supplied URLs)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no (the behaviour is stated in prose but carries no marker)

## Not judgeable from this material

1. Which code revision each consumer actually runs. visionGraph pins ontomark v0.6.0 by digest (`publish.yml:48-51`), but ADR-2136's close-out says Decision 1's repin of the `vault` source was not implemented in the running image (`ADR-2136…:97-102`), and OM-12 says the VisionClaw dependency bump to compiler 0.6.3 has no final lock or release receipt. I would need deployment receipts and running-image digests before signing off — this also decides whether F-01 is live today.
2. Real-corpus redaction completeness. The material shows probe-level canaries only; I would need the actual corpus and an independent scan of published bytes for private terms before trusting the redaction boundary, given F-02 and F-03.
3. Concurrency guarantees around `promote`, `write_to` and certificate writes. The PID-named staging directories suggest no cross-process lock, but the material never asserts whether two builds can target one `out` or one `publish_out`; I need the orchestration's single-writer guarantee or a code-level lock before judging the crash/concurrency cases in F-08 and F-09.
4. The Obsidian plugin's published release assets. OM-09.1 says workflow presence is not a verified attestation of the published `main.js`/`manifest.json`/`styles.css`; I would need the release artifact digests to compare against a rebuilt bundle.
5. Whether the production vocabulary declares relations beyond the built-in twelve, and whether `misc/` or nested `_misc/` pages exist in the real corpus — these determine whether F-05 and F-03 are live defects or latent ones, and I cannot tell from the distillation.

## ontomark / premortem

# Post-mortem — publication of private material with an unverifiable evidence chain

Summary of the failure as the evidence supports it: a client-facing publication carried names the operator classified as private, and when the operator tried to establish exactly which bytes had shipped and prove their derivation, neither the generation markers nor the derivation certificate could be tied to the artefacts on disk. The three root causes below are ranked by likelihood; each is a defect already documented or reproduced in the estate's own audits at the pinned revisions.

---

### R-1 — Private working-note names survive in the public knowledge projection because the knowledge redactor is not the union redactor
- Topics: OM-11.2, OM-06.2, OM-05.5
- Chain of events: a knowledge page's title or label contains the bare name of a private working note → the knowledge projection is built first through its own record set, so its prose-marker set contains only knowledge-private identities (`ir/projection.rs:750,751`) → the union marker set over both vaults is built later (`ir/projection.rs:837-850`) but applied only to the surviving working plain records (`ir/projection.rs:869-875`) → the knowledge record passes unmasked → the retained name flows into every public surface that takes a title or label from the projected record: page API (`build/page_api.rs:89`), search index, scaffold label fallback (`build/indexes.rs:129`), Turtle labels, WebVOWL, OKF and the `publish/` markdown tree → the public consumer (visionGraph `build --vault all`, `publish.yml:165-171`) serves it to the client site.
- Evidence: OM-11.2 marks this as a TENSION — "bare private working name can survive in a public knowledge title; knowledge redactor is not the union redactor (`projection.rs:750,850,890`)" — and the audit **reproduced** it: "The same private working-note title was masked in a public working title and retained in a public knowledge title. Both public and machine builds retained the latter; the private body's canary stayed absent." The design reason given is D4 (ADR-OM-005): the knowledge projection must stay byte-identical across scopes, so the union redactor cannot be retro-applied to it.
- Warning signs visible today: the reproduced disclosure at the pinned revision (`0caae5d`); the explicit TENSION label; a public website consumer with redaction on by profile, never passing `--no-redact-private-links` (`estate/profile.yaml:226`, `build/mod.rs:615-617`).
- Confidence: high — the disclosure is reproduced; only the client-facing consequence is inferred from the existence of the public consumer in OM-05.5.
- Severity: high

### R-2 — The legacy VisionClaw producer still ships the unfixed SEV-1 boundary: fail-open redactor and a frontmatter-blind credential scan
- Topics: OM-00.2, OM-00.3, OM-05.5, OM-05.6
- Chain of events: ADR-OM-005 decision 2 retires the VisionClaw `vault` build job but "gated, so it keeps running" (`ADR-OM-005-estate-alignment-one-producer-explicit-projections.md:11`) → the workflow keeps building the in-tree `vault` crate (`ontology-publish.yml:167-168`) and publishing `--vault knowledge` output into Pod resources (`ontology-publish.yml:172-175`, `:247`) → that binary carries the inherited faults unchanged: the prose redactor that failed **open** on a regex compile error (`vault/src/projection.rs:230`, S1.1), the credential scan that reads only the body while render publishes the frontmatter (`vault/src/build/publish.rs:159-160`, `:196`, S1.2; d5c1188 wrapped redaction round the render, not the scan), the first-vault-only gate/conflicts under `--vault all` (`vault/src/main.rs:595-598`, S1.5) and the remove-then-write `publish_out` (S2.3) → a credential placed in a frontmatter value on a public page, or any redactor compile failure, publishes private material into the Pod feed; a failed legacy publish deletes the previous publication tree without replacing it.
- Evidence: OM-00.3 EXT states plainly: "VisionClaw's vault build job still ships S1.1, S1.2, S1.5, S2.3 and S3.1 unchanged until VC-60 retires under its four gates." Every one of these was confirmed INHERITED from the vault sources and fixed only on the ontomark side at 8d654d1. The ADR-2136 close-out of 2026-10-30 recorded status `proposed / partial / inactive` and that Decision 1's repin of the `vault` source was **not** implemented (`ADR-2136-pin-the-shared-ontology-toolchain.md:5-7`, `:97-102`) — the retirement was unfinished at the last recorded status.
- Warning signs visible today: the EXT note itself at visionclaw pin `8692b12`; the stalled ADR-2136 close-out; a dual-producer estate where one producer's SEV-1 fixes exist only in the other producer.
- Confidence: medium — the defects' presence at the pinned VisionClaw commit is documented; that this path, rather than ontomark, carried the incident is inferred from the unfinished retirement.
- Severity: high

### R-3 — The evidence layers are not transactionally tied to the bundle: certificate written before the gates and outside staging, publish tree written after promote, markers stamp hashes of files that may never be written
- Topics: OM-10.2, OM-10.3, OM-03.5, OM-03.8, OM-04.1
- Chain of events: a build with `--certificate-out` writes the certificate files directly to the caller's directory **before** the consistency gate and before any staging (`build/mod.rs:715-717`; OM-03.1 marks this a Tension: "no bundle transaction covers them") → an unsatisfiable corpus refuses afterwards, leaving a new certificate beside the old, still-promoted bundle (`mod.rs:743-749`) → if the certificate directory lies under `out`, the promote swap removes it wholesale (`mod.rs:311,313,316`) → independently of the flag, the root marker stamps the `publish/` external artefacts with their sha256s (`mod.rs:1009-1013`, `:361-363`), but `write_to(publish_out)` runs **after** promote, and its failure leaves the new bundle beside the previous publication tree — "the two no longer one generation" (`build/publish.rs:356`, `mod.rs:1018-1020`) → the promoted marker now describes publication bytes that were never written → the certificate writer itself performs four sequential filesystem writes, so a mid-sequence failure leaves new assertions with an old digest (`rdfs-certificate/src/lib.rs:589-592`) → the independent checker verifies only the soundness of supplied steps and accepts an empty derivation file (`rdfs-certificate/tests/oo_cert.rs:55,56`) → when the operator attempts to prove what shipped, every evidence layer can disagree with the bytes on disk.
- Evidence: all component defects are stated with citations above; the certificate audit "reproduces failures that separate the certificate from the surviving bundle" (OM-03.1) and the probes "distinguish a valid parity refusal from these publication defects" (OM-10.2). visionGraph's pipeline depends on this evidence: it passes `--certificate-out` and runs an oo-cert re-check (`publish.yml:165-171`, `:210-227`). OM-03.8 leaves open that a bundle published without the flag carries no warrant at all (`mod.rs:730`), and C3 names no certificate artefact (`sovereign-corpus-contracts.md:111`) while ADR-OM-009 decides every published closure carries one (`ADR-OM-009...md:14`).
- Warning signs visible today: the OM-03.1 Tension, the OM-10.2/10.3 reproduced probes, the OM-03.5 N2 divergence, and the C3/ADR-OM-009 listing mismatch.
- Confidence: medium — every component is documented or reproduced; the compound custody failure as the client-costing mechanism is inferred.
- Severity: high

---

### F-01 — Governance receipt names a different page than the one written
- Topics: OM-07.2, OM-07.1
- Evidence: the adapter reconstructs staged Markdown from added diff lines and its receipt uses the original proposal page (`ontology-apply.js:578`, `:634`), while `ontomark create` derives the target filename from the **staged title** (`write/create.rs:248,258`) — the DRIFT is stated: "staged title B can write B.md while adapter receipt names proposal page A (`ontology-apply.js:570` vs `write/create.rs:258`)". The optional `page_sha256` check (`ontology-apply.js:586`) is not a semantic proposal-digest check.
- Failure: an approved change applies to page B while the forum ledger and reviewer trail record page A; a later audit or rollback targets the wrong page.
- Confidence: high — the A/B discrepancy is reproduced in the audit fixture.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift)

### F-02 — Explicit promote lacks approve's proposal-digest equality check
- Topics: OM-07.1
- Evidence: approve requires the proposal digest to equal the decision tag (`local-process-manager.js:379,397,400`); explicit promote checks only the request tag and signed IRI (`local-process-manager.js:444,450`) — the TENSION is stated at `:444` vs `:400`.
- Failure: a caller invoking the promote path with a valid but different proposal digest applies an amendment that does not correspond to the case it is recorded under.
- Confidence: high — the missing check is stated; the audit notes it does not establish a relay bypass.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension)

### F-03 — Ledger attestation failure is returned after the write has persisted
- Topics: OM-07.3
- Evidence: "Attest caller's case and digest; failure is returned after write (`ontology-apply.js:742,900`)"; ADR-2109 is cited as deliberately keeping ledger failure from undoing a completed write, but the boundary produces contradictory records rather than compensating ones.
- Failure: the ledger records a failed attestation for a change that is live in the corpus; an operator acting on the ledger re-applies or re-approves, producing a duplicate or divergent amendment.
- Confidence: high.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-04 — Push can include a commit added after the ahead-count check
- Topics: OM-07.3
- Evidence: "writer can add a commit after count; push then includes it (`ontology-apply.js:872,879`)" — marked TENSION.
- Failure: an unrelated concurrent commit is published under a governance decision's receipt, attributing unvetted content to an approved case.
- Confidence: high — stated as a reproduced interleaving in the audit.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension)

### F-05 — Amendment compare-and-rename is not atomic; no writer lock
- Topics: OM-07.3
- Evidence: the apply path writes a synced temporary file, re-reads the original, then renames (`write/apply.rs:395,401,411`); the OPEN item states "compare and rename are separate; no writer lock at this boundary".
- Failure: two governance applications to one page interleave between compare and rename; one patch is silently lost or applied over a moved base.
- Confidence: medium — the race window is source-derived, not reproduced.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Open)

### F-06 — Ingest review bundles are replaced in place under a stable request ID
- Topics: OM-08.3
- Evidence: the request ID excludes the endpoint and budgets (`ingest_cmd.rs:606` vs `:446`, `:570` — DRIFT), the default directory is keyed by that ID and the existing manifest directory is removed before rewriting (`ingest_cmd.rs:769,771,772`), and the TENSION states "incomplete uncached rerun can replace complete bundle under same ID (`ingest_cmd.rs:723,769,772`)". Removed sources are classified by the library (`ledger.rs:224,255`) but dropped from the host result (`ingest_cmd.rs:841,843`).
- Failure: a reviewer opens what they believe is the complete evidence bundle for a proposal; it has been silently replaced by an incomplete rerun from a different endpoint, and the removal of prior sources is not reported.
- Confidence: high — reproduced in the audit.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension, Drift)

### F-07 — Certificate writer performs four sequential, non-atomic file writes
- Topics: OM-10.3, OM-03.8
- Evidence: "failed second write can leave new assertions with old digest (`rdfs-certificate/src/lib.rs:589,590`)" — TENSION; the four files are written sequentially at `:589-592`.
- Failure: a torn certificate ships with `asserted.tsv` from the new run and `asserted.sha256` from the old; downstream verification either fails spuriously or, if the digest is not checked, validates the wrong assertions.
- Confidence: high — stated as a source-level TENSION.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension)

### F-08 — The independent checker proves soundness only; an empty derivation file passes
- Topics: OM-10.3
- Evidence: "omitting all steps still passes; soundness does not establish closure completeness (`oo_cert.rs:55,56`)"; `scope.tsv` and `asserted.sha256` are not inputs to the checker invocation (`oo_cert.rs:54,55,56`).
- Failure: a truncated or emptied `derivations.tsv` (e.g. after F-07 or a partial copy) re-verifies cleanly, giving false assurance that the published closure is warranted.
- Confidence: high — reproduced: the checker accepted a truncated one-step certificate.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension)

### F-09 — `content_digest` excludes the profile, so one digest can accompany changed output bytes
- Topics: OM-11.3, OM-03.6
- Evidence: TENSION — "profile is absent from content_digest inputs; same digest can accompany changed output (`mod.rs:963-983`)"; the audit reproduced a page-namespace change that preserved generation ID and content digest while changing page IRIs and artefact hashes; the attribution roster is likewise not a digest input (`mod.rs:680-685`, OM-03.6 N1).
- Failure: a consumer cache or audit keyed on `content_digest` (as C3 contemplates, `sovereign-corpus-contracts.md:159-163`) treats two different outputs as the same corpus.
- Confidence: high — reproduced.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension)

### F-10 — Feature-off builds retain the pre-fix path semantics, including the reproduced root-symlink role aliasing
- Topics: OM-12.3, OM-01.1, OM-01.2
- Evidence: "Feature-off builds retain legacy behaviour for compatibility comparison, including weaker link handling" (OM-12.3); the layout audit "reproduced journal duplication with empty exclusions and role aliasing through a root symlink" (OM-01.1 reconciliation note, 2026-11-01).
- Failure: a downstream deployment compiled without `shared-corpus-layout` aliases the working root onto the knowledge role through a symlink and publishes working content as public knowledge; on the fixed path this is refused, on the legacy path it is not.
- Confidence: medium — the primitive is reproduced; the deployment misconfiguration is inferred.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-11 — The held-back rule exists in two shapes: top-level-only for the census and identity set, any-depth for staging
- Topics: OM-03.3, OM-02.5, OM-00.1
- Evidence: `is_unpublished` covers `_misc` at top level only (`input/page.rs:641`), used by the census and `excluded_identities_of` (`ir/projection.rs:114,231`), while `held_back` applies `UNPUBLISHED_DIRS` at any depth plus site-only `misc` (`build/publish.rs:134,138-148`); OM-00.1 leaves **Open** whether the `misc` entry belongs in `HELD_BACK_DIRS` at all.
- Failure: a public page under a nested `_misc/` path is projected public into the page API and search index (its identity never enters `private_ids`) while `publish/` skips it — one folder is half-published across artefacts.
- Confidence: high — the divergence is stated with citations.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Open)

### F-12 — Graph extraction drops unsupported shapes and unreadable definitions before the refusal check
- Topics: OM-10.1, OM-04.3
- Evidence: TENSION — "unknown shapes and unreadable definitions can be omitted before refusal check (`ontomark-compile/src/reason.rs:283,401`)"; probes showed a raw universal restriction produced **zero** compiler refusals where the typed equivalent was refused, and a malformed graph definition was silently omitted.
- Failure: an authored restriction is silently ignored; the classifier, the structural closure and the certificate all operate on the same reduced graph, so parity holds while the published taxonomy is incomplete relative to intent — nothing downstream detects the omission.
- Confidence: high — reproduced via Graph API probes.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension)

### F-13 — NGG1 collapses all twelve relations to one edge byte and drops edges to undeclared targets
- Topics: OM-04.4, OM-06.5, OM-00.5
- Evidence: every relation key becomes `EDGE_RELATION` and the dedup key merges two distinct relations between one pair (`build/graph_tiers.rs:283,313-315`; `ontomark-ngg1/src/lib.rs:142-144` is a published wire value); edges to targets that are not selected nodes are "silently dropped" (`graph_tiers.rs:297-298,309-310`) while `stats.json` reports declared against shipped (`graph_tiers.rs:285-286`); recorded as OPEN S3.1 pending an NGG2 format bump.
- Failure: a client reading the explorer tiers sees one edge where the corpus asserts two different relations, and no edge at all for cross-vault references — silent semantic data loss into a consumer.
- Confidence: high.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Open)

### F-14 — Obsidian host: index-update rejection can follow a persisted write
- Topics: OM-09.4, OM-06.4
- Evidence: "rejection can follow persisted write; upsert is outside write-error catch (`api.ts:248,253`)" — TENSION; OM-06.4's open note records that there is no atomic file-write/index transaction and the host compares the complete base text rather than rehashing.
- Failure: a confirmed ingest edit is written to disk, the engine index update fails, and the user receives a rejection for a change that persisted — the note and the index then disagree with no recovery path.
- Confidence: high — reproduced in the plugin audit.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension)

### F-15 — SPARQL result blocks in one note share a single channel; the latest request wins
- Topics: OM-09.3
- Evidence: the query channel is keyed by note path (`sparql-block.ts:71`) while the guard keeps only the latest request per channel (`guard.ts:46,68`) — TENSION: "two blocks in one note collide"; unload only unsubscribes and clears the timer, it does not invalidate an in-flight render (`sparql-block.ts:51,54`).
- Failure: two query blocks in one note race; one block's render is clobbered or abandoned, and the user reads results belonging to the other block's query — incorrect data displayed as the answer to a different question.
- Confidence: medium — the collision is stated and the lifecycle cases were reproduced; the exact mis-render is inferred.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension)

## sidestr-rs / critical

Findings are ranked by production damage: security and custody first, then correctness, operability, maintainability. All citations are the material's own path:line references.

### F-01 — Withdrawal obligations can be double-satisfied by one output; the shortfall is payable to the signer
- Topics: SR-03.1, SR-03.4
- Evidence: The required invariant is stated ("Complete withdrawal payment is a required invariant") but the predicate "find[s] any matching output per withdrawal" and "does not consume an output match" (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-evm/src/state.rs:511-514`), while the coinbase allowance "sums every obligation" (`state.rs:73`, `rule.rs:105`). The probe is explicit: "a full-validator probe accepts two 10,000-sat withdrawals with one 10,000-sat recipient output and the remaining allowance paid to the signer." SR-03.4 adds "The pinned reference also contains the withdrawal-matching gap" — oracle parity enforces the defect rather than catching it.
- Failure: On any EVM-enabled chain, a producer pays one of N duplicate-matching withdrawals and routes the remaining allowance to itself. Validation passes on both the Rust and reference implementations; the recipient's shortfall is permanent and consensus-silent. Fixing only one implementation breaks parity.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Open, SR-03.1)

### F-02 — Hitch watcher accepts a foreign genesis and replacement at fork zero; it watches the wrong chain
- Topics: SR-04.5
- Evidence: "The watcher intentionally does not validate transactions, but it also drops the document's genesis pin" (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-agent/src/hitch/follow.rs:133`); native scenarios "reproduce same-signer foreign-genesis acceptance and replacement at fork zero" (`follow.rs:121-140`, follower-probe.json). The kernel design assumes the watch boundary detects closes to trigger penalties and claims (SR-04.4).
- Failure: A counterparty or compromised feed presenting a different chain signed by the same signer switches the follower. The real close or revoked-state spend on the actual chain is never observed; to_local delays and HTLC timeouts lapse and the counterparty takes the outputs uncontested.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Open, "pin the watched genesis")

### F-03 — A close classified while a revocation secret was missing stays misclassified after the secret arrives; the penalty is never taken
- Topics: SR-04.3, SR-04.4
- Evidence: "Recorded closes/claims are checked only by transaction ID" on restore (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/snapshot.rs:341-353`); the audit reproduces "missing-secret close classification that persists after a late reveal" and "damaged-witness rebroadcasts." The penalty branch exists in the kernel (`chain.rs:276-306`) but is only reachable if the close is classified as revoked.
- Failure: The counterparty broadcasts an old commitment while your revocation secret for that state is missing (e.g., crash before the reveal was persisted). The reveal arrives on resync, but the machine still treats the close as unrevoked; the penalty transaction is never constructed; the thief keeps the balance while your claims rebroadcast with damaged witnesses.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt, SR-04.3)

### F-04 — Snapshot code bytes are keyed by claimed hash and never rehashed; execution diverges under a passing root check
- Topics: SR-03.3
- Evidence: "Account roots contain code hashes; execution looks up code bytes separately without rehashing them" (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-evm/src/world.rs:111`, `world.rs:184-192`); "A probe changes a contract answer from 42 to 43 under the same root." Restore validation is internal: "selected world root equals snapshot block record" — the snapshot's own record (`state.rs:305-350`) — and "does not authenticate that time, receipts or an external chain commitment." Also "Selecting genesis also leaves future block/receipt records queryable."
- Failure: A node restoring from any externally sourced snapshot (backup, peer sync) executes tampered contract code; the root-equality check passes; the node then computes balances and verdicts that diverge from every honest node — or reports attacker-chosen results — with no error raised anywhere.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-05 — Reserve attestation counts outputs at the current tip; the estate's confirmation-depth obligation is not implemented
- Topics: SR-05.1, SR-05.3
- Evidence: The adapter "includes an output in the current tip block"; "No depth parameter enters `credits` or `attest`" (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-bridge-liquid/src/attest.rs:103-107`), while ADR-2117:57 makes "configured `bridgeConfirmations` before signing" an obligation. ADR-0003:50's "Liquid finality" wording "is broader than the implemented height filter." The adapter also "documents reliance on the public server for completeness and freshness" (`attest.rs:14-25`). Today the CLI prints unsigned JSON (`main.rs:179-181`), so the gate is unclosed rather than firing.
- Failure: When the signer is wired in, it signs an aggregate including depth-0 outputs; a one-block reorg removes them and issued units become unbacked. A malicious or stale public server fabricates the tip and UTXO set in either direction.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high (contingent on activation)
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension)

### F-06 — A validly signed attestation proves neither the amount originally tallied, nor authority, freshness, or uniqueness
- Topics: SR-05.2, SR-05.3
- Evidence: The struct "says 'Built only by attest', but its fields are public; `check` preserves field syntax and duplicate rejection, not the original total" (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-reserve/src/lib.rs:468-485`, `lib.rs:529-544`); "A caller can change the amount before signing and obtain a valid signature over that new statement." And "`SignedAttestation.verify` has no expected-authority, clock or replay-set argument" (`lib.rs:688`).
- Failure: A weeks-old attestation replays and verifies; or a compromised caller component inflates the aggregate before invoking the signer; a downstream consumer relying on signature validity alone over-issues against a reserve that never existed at that amount.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high (contingent on activation)
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift) for the mutable amount; the missing verify arguments are unmarked

### F-07 — The EVM profile disables block-gas enforcement and returns zero for BLOCKHASH
- Topics: SR-03.4
- Evidence: "The profile disables block-gas enforcement, aborts at KZG and returns zero for BLOCKHASH" (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-evm/src/exec.rs:168-170`, `exec.rs:78`, `world.rs:209-210`); two KZG refusal fixture cases "deliberately skip intermediate-root equality" (`oracle.rs:180`). The estate chain document does not select EVM today (`chain.json:28`).
- Failure: On an EVM-enabled chain, a single carrier transaction with effectively unbounded gas stalls every validating node (no block cap), and contracts using BLOCKHASH silently read zero — consensus-valid but economically wrong outcomes. The divergences are stated; the DoS scaling is inferred from "cap disabled".
- Confidence: high for the stated divergences; the exhaustion consequence is inferred
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-08 — Rejected blocks leave candidate state and verdicts cached with no stated bound
- Topics: SR-02.1, SR-03.2
- Evidence: "Distinct candidate snapshots accumulate until `applied` clears them, even when another check rejects the block" (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-core/src/assets.rs:535`, `pool.rs:316`, `markets.rs:434`). For EVM: commit "clears pending candidates but leaves the verdict map" (`state.rs:561-582`) and "The former discard-on-refusal arrow overstated cleanup: the native probe retains 64 rejected outer candidates, then still reads all 64 verdicts after application."
- Failure: A peer streams blocks that pass early rules and fail a late one; each stages a world or overlay snapshot that is never cleared; a long-running validator grows without bound and is exhausted under sustained adversarial traffic.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Open, SR-02.1) for the core overlays; the EVM-side retention is unmarked

### F-09 — A clock failure evicts valid transactions by exact bytes and suppresses their resubmission
- Topics: SR-02.4
- Evidence: "The current fallback also treats a producer-wide future-header failure as transaction invalidity: both Rust paths reproduce that gap" (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-evm/src/rule.rs:364`, `state.rs:874`, `state.rs:759`); the diagram note says "clock failure can wrongly evict a valid transaction"; eviction is by exact bytes and "suppresses repeated invalid forms while admitting a witness repair" (`state.rs:742`, `eviction.rs:16`).
- Failure: Clock skew makes the next header appear future-dated; the block build fails wholesale and every candidate transaction is evicted byte-for-byte; identical resubmission is suppressed, so users must mutate a witness to retry — time-sensitive spends such as HTLC sweeps miss their windows.
- Confidence: high on the mechanism; an incident additionally requires a clock fault
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no (stated only in a diagram note)

### F-10 — Collided or failed updates keep HTLCs bound indefinitely; updates are buffered before signature verification
- Topics: SR-04.2
- Evidence: "A shape-valid next update can be buffered before signature verification" (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/machine.rs:833-836`); "The blanket no-write-before-full-verification invariant does not cover the buffer"; the native check confirms "a misbound signature survives buffering/restore but is rejected on replay." Separately, a set-aside or rejected state's HTLCs "stay bound until that state number is revoked or the funding is spent by something else" (`protocol/mod.rs:20`, `machine.rs:561`).
- Failure: A peer sends a misbound or colliding update and then stalls: the victim persists unverified peer data into snapshots, and the set-aside HTLC value has no protocol path to release except on-chain closure — a griefing vector that forces force-close fees and delay.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Boundary) for buffering; the HTLC binding is unmarked

### F-11 — Short channel IDs omit the funding output index; batch-funded channels collide
- Topics: SR-04.1
- Evidence: "The short channel ID uses only the transaction ID, so different funding outputs share it" (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/wire.rs:37-40`); "protocol IDs omit the output index."
- Failure: A hub batch-funds two channels in one transaction; both share a short channel ID; announcements, routing records and watch bookkeeping conflate them, applying payments or close handling to the wrong channel's state.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt)

### F-12 — Everything actually running is one unaudited JavaScript engine; the Rust producer has never run and has known gaps
- Topics: SR-01.4, SR-02.4
- Evidence: Both live chains are "produced by the upstream JavaScript engine"; "The observed producers have no checkpoint arguments, consistent with default-zero configuration; historical anchoring was not inspected"; "no native Rust producer or channel lifecycle was demonstrated by this observation"; "A responding tip is not evidence of current block progress, mirror freshness, successful grants or Hitch activation." SR-02.4 separately records the Rust producer's rejection-path gaps.
- Failure: Taking over production means either continuing on a single implementation with no redundancy and no anchoring evidence (its only audited property here is that it shares the withdrawal gap), or promoting a Rust producer with zero production history and known eviction bugs.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Open)

### F-13 — 0.2.0 is breaking: 0.1 snapshots cannot be restored, and 0.1 channels carry the owner-penalty cheat until closed with old binaries
- Topics: SR-04 (intro), SR-04.1
- Evidence: 0.1's key "let a commitment's owner spend its own revocation leaf, which is the flaw this release fixes" (`CHANGELOG.md:45`, `:52`; new construction at `lib.rs:740`); "a 0.1 channel cannot be restored by 0.2"; "Any channel opened with the earlier version has to be closed with that version."
- Failure: If any 0.1-era channel or pilot snapshot exists, a 0.2 binary refuses the restore mid-incident, forcing on-chain recovery during the outage; any still-open 0.1 channel stays exposed to the cheat. Exposure is contingent — no live channels are evidenced anywhere in the material.
- Confidence: high on the facts; the production exposure is inferred and unknown
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no (stated as release facts, no marker)

### F-14 — Recorded claims contradict the running configuration, and parity suites can pass without running
- Topics: SR-01.2, SR-01.3, SR-01.4
- Evidence: "ADR-0001 still says four references; CI now pins six" (`ADR-0001...md:27` vs `ci.yml:17`); the second chain table "is enabled despite the README's older disabled-default statement" (`agentbox.toml:1976` vs `sidechain/README.md:188`); TODO-unified's "claim that Hitch has no host is now stale" (`ADR-0002...md:144` vs `host.rs:647`); tests "deliberately allow absent oracles" (`ci.yml:58`, `interop.rs:18`, `pegtweak.rs:406`) so "a reported pass alone is insufficient parity evidence."
- Failure: An operator following the README believes the txbt4 chain is disabled while it produces and mirrors; an engineer verifying against ADR-0001 audits four of six pinned checkouts; a green CI run that silently skipped every oracle suite is read as compatibility proof and ships a parity break.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: no (stated as plain facts)

### F-15 — The shipped host pays expired invoices and runs a non-hub router the kernel audits did not cover
- Topics: SR-04.3
- Evidence: "The present agent host constructs a non-hub router, and its payee lookup supplies no invoice Unix-expiry filter" (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-agent/src/hitch/host.rs:680`, `host.rs:1676-1683`); "Invoice wall-clock expiry policy and durable hub integration remain explicit acceptance tasks."
- Failure: The host pays invoices that expired wall-clock long ago — funds leave the channel for a counterparty that will never deliver — and the routing path exercised in production (non-hub) is not the hub-forwarding path the pure router was validated on, so kernel audit results do not transfer to what actually runs.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (the Debt paragraph names expiry policy and hub integration; the missing filter itself is unmarked prose)

## Not judgeable from this material

1. **Activation reality.** Whether any EVM, Hitch, overlay or reserve capability is enabled on any chain beyond the two research chains — the actual chain documents and per-capability acceptance receipts that would turn findings F-01 through F-07 from contingent into live.
2. **The JavaScript engine's own source.** Siding, the Hitch reference and ethereumjs are the only code actually running; nothing here audits them beyond noting they share the withdrawal-matching gap.
3. **Host persistence and crash behavior.** Whether save-before-send is actually implemented in the agent host, mempool durability across restarts, relay transport authentication, and key custody boundaries are cited as obligations but never reviewed.
4. **The real CI run contents.** Which oracle suites executed versus skipped in run 41128639012, the pinned revisions, and fixture regeneration behavior — the receipt "does not prove release enforcement or chain activation."
5. **Historical anchoring and mirror integrity.** Whether the live producers ever ran with non-zero checkpoints, and what freshness or integrity the Git mirror loops guarantee — both explicitly uninspected.

## sidestr-rs / premortem

# Post-mortem — sidestr estate production incident

**Basis.** This report is written against the SR-01…SR-05 baseline (verified commits recorded in each file's front matter). The actual incident mechanism is not known to this document; the three root causes below are ranked from defects the baseline material already states or reproduces, and each is labelled with how far the material carries it. Every finding carries the baseline's own citations so it can be checked against code and tests before action.

---

### R-1 — EVM withdrawal predicate accepts one output for many obligations, paying the difference to the block signer
- Topics: SR-03.1, SR-03.4
- Chain of events:
  1. An EVM-enabled sidestr chain is activated (today the sealed estate document does not select an EVM rule — `../project/agentbox/config/sidechain/dreamlab/chain.json:28` — so this step is assumed for the incident).
  2. A block carries two withdrawal obligations of 10,000 sats each to the same recipient script. The coinbase allowance is the **sum** of all obligations (20,000 sats) (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-evm/src/state.rs:73`, `../sidestr-rs/sidestr-evm/src/rule.rs:105`).
  3. The block contains one 10,000-sat output to that script. Withdrawal matching tests only that a matching output **exists** per obligation and does not consume the match (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-evm/src/state.rs:511-514`), so both obligations are satisfied by the single output.
  4. The remaining 10,000 sats of the summed allowance are paid to the block signer, inside validation.
  5. The block validates; the payee is underpaid by 10,000 sats and the difference is credited to the signer. The client's loss is discovered on reconciliation.
- Evidence: The material's own probe: "a full-validator probe accepts two 10,000-sat withdrawals with one 10,000-sat recipient output and the remaining allowance paid to the signer" (SR-03.1, Open). Matching is per-obligation existence at `../sidestr-rs/sidestr-evm/src/state.rs:511-514`; the allowance sums every obligation (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-evm/src/rule.rs:105`). The material also states "The pinned reference also contains the withdrawal-matching gap" (SR-03.4), so the reference oracle cannot catch this class of block.
- Warning signs visible today: The full-validator probe result recorded in the [EVM audit](../../estate-review/2026-11-01-sidestr-evm-accounting-and-snapshots.md); the divergence between summed allowance (`rule.rs:105`) and existence-only matching (`state.rs:511-514`); the material's own statement that "Complete withdrawal payment is a required invariant that the current predicate does not enforce."
- Confidence: high that the defect exists and reproduces; inferred that it caused this incident, since no EVM-enabled chain is selected by the sealed document today.
- Severity: high

### R-2 — Legacy Hitch 0.1 channel exploited through the owner-spendable revocation leaf
- Topics: SR-04 (intro), SR-04.1
- Chain of events:
  1. A channel was opened under sidestr-hitch 0.1, whose revocation construction "let a commitment's owner spend its own revocation leaf" (SR-04 intro).
  2. 0.2.0 (sidestr-rs `5a1e89ae`, 1 Oct) shipped the two-party revocation key as a **breaking** change: "a 0.1 channel cannot be restored by 0.2" and "Any channel opened with the earlier version has to be closed with that version" (SR-04 intro; SR-04 business section).
  3. A still-open 0.1 channel therefore remains serviced by 0.1 code and keeps the flawed key construction (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/CHANGELOG.md:45`, `../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/CHANGELOG.md:52`).
  4. One party broadcasts its own commitment and spends through its own revocation leaf — the hole the material describes as "a party could cheat with its own penalty path" — taking funds that the delay/penalty design was supposed to protect.
  5. The counterparty's 0.2 host cannot restore the channel to mount a response, because the 0.1 construction is unrestorable in 0.2.
- Evidence: SR-04 intro states the 0.1 flaw and the unrestorable/breaking nature of 0.2.0; `CHANGELOG.md:45` and `:52` tie the two-party revocation key to the changed commitments; SR-04.1 gives the new construction (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/lib.rs:740`, `:753`), implying the old one differed exactly in owner knowledge of the revocation secret.
- Warning signs visible today: A published, breaking fix for a money-bearing flaw with an explicit population of channels that cannot be migrated, only closed; the baseline records no receipt that all 0.1 channels were closed before the incident window.
- Confidence: high that the 0.1 flaw exists and is exploitable on any open 0.1 channel; inferred that such a channel remained open and was exploited.
- Severity: high

### R-3 — Hitch chain follower drops the genesis pin and accepts a same-signer foreign chain
- Topics: SR-04.5 (with SR-04.4)
- Chain of events:
  1. The agent host's `ChainFollower` "intentionally does not validate transactions" but, critically, "drops the document's genesis pin" (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-agent/src/hitch/follow.rs:133`).
  2. The host is fed a chain whose blocks verify under the same signer keys but descend from a different genesis — the exact acceptance the baseline probe reproduces (`follow.rs:121-140`; [follower probe](../../estate-review/evidence/2026-11-01/crate-boundary/follower-probe.json), "same-signer foreign genesis acceptance and replacement at fork zero").
  3. The follower tracks channel funding/close state against the wrong chain. Either it misses a genuine old-state close on the true chain (no penalty broadcast, counterparty steals channel funds), or it misclassifies spends on the foreign chain and broadcasts claims/penalties against the wrong ledger (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/chain.rs:176`, `:364`).
  4. Channel funds are lost or stranded; the host's spend-key binding checks (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-agent/src/hitch/host.rs:652`) never fire, because they protect the spend identity, not the chain identity.
- Evidence: `follow.rs:121-140` and `:133` for the dropped pin; the native probe for same-signer foreign-genesis acceptance and fork-zero replacement (SR-04.5, with evidence path above); SR-04.5's Open item: "pin the watched genesis."
- Warning signs visible today: The reproduced follower probe in the baseline; the material's explicit statement that "chain identity needs its own enforced anchor at the watch boundary"; the watcher's stated trust of transaction validity compounding the missing identity check.
- Confidence: high that the follower accepts same-signer foreign genesis; inferred that this acceptance was exercised in the incident.
- Severity: high

---

### F-01 — Snapshot contract code is keyed by claimed hash and executed without rehashing
- Topics: SR-03.3
- Evidence: "code bytes keyed by claimed hash"; "execution reads supplied bytes" at `../sidestr-rs/sidestr-evm/src/world.rs:184-192`, with account roots carrying only code hashes (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-evm/src/world.rs:111`). The baseline probe "changes a contract answer from 42 to 43 under the same root" (SR-03.3).
