- Failure: A restored-from-snapshot node (or any consumer of a snapshot file) executes altered contract code that still presents the recorded root; balances and decisions diverge with no validation error.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-02 — Reserve statement amount is mutable before signing and the signature stays valid
- Topics: SR-05.2
- Evidence: "A caller can change the amount before signing and obtain a valid signature over that new statement" (SR-05.2); `check` "preserves field syntax and duplicate rejection, not the original total" (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-reserve/src/lib.rs:529-544`), public amount fields at `:473-485`, checked-sum only at construction `:510-520`.
- Failure: Once a future bridge consumes attestations, a buggy or compromised caller signs an inflated aggregate amount; the resulting `SignedAttestation` verifies cleanly.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift)

### F-03 — Liquid adapter counts outputs confirmed at the tip block; estate policy requires configured depth before signing
- Topics: SR-05.1
- Evidence: "includes an output in the current tip block. No depth parameter enters `credits` or `attest`" (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-bridge-liquid/src/attest.rs:103-107`); estate `bridgeConfirmations` gate at `../project/agentbox/docs/adr/ADR-2117-private-owner-usd-unit-bridged-through-rgb-into-sidestr.md:57`; ADR-0003's "Liquid finality" wording broader than the height filter (`../sidestr-rs/docs/adr/ADR-0003-keep-reserve-attestations-origin-neutral-and-private.md:50`).
- Failure: A tip-block UTXO is attested and then reorged away; the reserve is overstated and, in an activated bridge, units are issued against vanished collateral.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension)

### F-04 — `SignedAttestation.verify` has no authority, freshness or replay argument
- Topics: SR-05.3 (with SR-05.2)
- Evidence: "`SignedAttestation.verify` has no expected-authority, clock or replay-set argument" (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-reserve/src/lib.rs:688`); a valid signature "does not establish that the key is authorised, the reading is recent, or issuing another unit is allowed" (SR-05 business).
- Failure: A future mint consumer accepts a correctly signed but stale or replayed statement (or one from an unauthorised key) and issues units.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-05 — Producer-wide future-header (clock) failure is treated as transaction invalidity and evicts valid transactions by exact bytes
- Topics: SR-02.4
- Evidence: "clock failure can wrongly evict a valid transaction" (SR-02.4 sequence note); "The current fallback also treats a producer-wide future-header failure as transaction invalidity: both Rust paths reproduce that gap" (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-evm/src/rule.rs:364`, `../sidestr-rs/sidestr-core/src/state.rs:874`, `:759`).
- Failure: A clock fault on the producer causes whole-block verdicts to fail for time reasons; exact-byte eviction then permanently suppresses clients' valid transactions until re-submission with different bytes, dropping in-flight payments.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-06 — Candidate rule-view snapshots accumulate until `applied`, including for rejected blocks
- Topics: SR-02.1
- Evidence: "distinct candidate snapshots accumulate until `applied` clears them, even when another check rejects the block" (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-core/src/assets.rs:535`, `../sidestr-rs/sidestr-core/src/pool.rs:316`, `../sidestr-rs/sidestr-core/src/markets.rs:434`).
- Failure: A validator or producer fed a stream of candidate blocks (e.g., an adversarial peer or a flapping producer) grows rule-view state without bound between applications; memory exhaustion halts the node.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Open)

### F-07 — EVM pending worlds and verdict history survive refusal and commit
- Topics: SR-03.2
- Evidence: "a successful EVM verdict stages a world even if another rule later rejects the block" (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-evm/src/state.rs:531-541`); "Commit clears pending candidates but leaves the verdict map" (`:561-582`); probe "retains 64 rejected outer candidates, then still reads all 64 verdicts after application" (SR-03.2).
- Failure: A long-running full validator accumulates staged worlds and verdict entries per rejected candidate and per applied block; unbounded growth degrades or kills the process.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-08 — Short channel ID uses only the transaction ID, so batched funding outputs collide
- Topics: SR-04.1
- Evidence: "the short channel ID uses only the transaction ID, so different funding outputs share it" (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/wire.rs:37-40`); the baseline also notes "The chain fixture does not establish host support for batched funding" (SR-04 intro).
- Failure: Two channels funded by different outputs of one funding transaction are indistinguishable by short channel ID; routing or host bookkeeping addresses the wrong channel and a payment is applied to the wrong peer's channel.
- Confidence: high (defect stated); failure scenario inferred — it requires batched funding, which the current host fixture does not demonstrate
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt)

### F-09 — Recovery records are checked by txid only, and a missing-secret close classification persists after a late reveal
- Topics: SR-04.3
- Evidence: "close and claim checks use txid only" (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/snapshot.rs:341-353`); the [recovery audit](../../estate-review/2026-11-01-sidestr-hitch-recovery.md) reproduces "damaged-witness rebroadcasts and missing-secret close classification that persists after a late reveal" (SR-04.3 Debt).
- Failure: A peer broadcasts an old state; the restored host classifies the close as unrevoked because the revocation secret arrives after classification, and never reclassifies — the penalty path is lost and the counterparty keeps the stolen balance.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt)

### F-10 — Agent host payee lookup applies no invoice expiry filter
- Topics: SR-04.3
- Evidence: "its payee lookup supplies no invoice Unix-expiry filter" (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-agent/src/hitch/host.rs:1676-1683`); "Invoice wall-clock expiry policy … remain explicit acceptance tasks" (SR-04.3).
- Failure: The host settles an invoice long past its intended validity window; HTLCs are committed against a payee who will not provide the preimage, tying channel capacity up until timeout-based failure.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt)

### F-11 — A next update is buffered on shape check alone; misbound signatures survive buffering and restore
- Topics: SR-04.2
- Evidence: "A shape-valid next update can be buffered before signature verification"; "shape checks precede buffering, while proof, state and signature checks precede adoption" (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/machine.rs:833-836`, `:845-884`); the native check "confirms a misbound signature survives buffering/restore but is rejected on replay" (SR-04.2).
- Failure: A host that restores from a snapshot containing a buffered update and does not replay it through `receive_update` (the documented host obligation, `../sidestr-rs/sidestr-hitch/src/protocol/mod.rs:27`) either loses a legitimate update or carries an unverified one in state across restarts.
- Confidence: high (behaviour stated); harm depends on host replay discipline — inferred
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-12 — Snapshot restore retains all histories, including heights above the selection
- Topics: SR-03.3
- Evidence: "It copies all histories and receipts" (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-evm/src/state.rs:305-350`); "Selecting genesis also leaves future block/receipt records queryable; this is not a pruned active-history view" (SR-03.3).
- Failure: An operator prunes by selecting an early height and believes stale data is gone; higher-height block records and receipts remain queryable and are served to clients as if current.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: no

### F-13 — Ordinary tests and CI silently pass when oracle files are absent
- Topics: SR-01.2
- Evidence: "Ordinary Rust tests deliberately allow absent oracles" (`../sidestr-rs/.github/workflows/ci.yml:58`); "Missing configured files also return early in round interop and pegtweak" (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-round/tests/support/interop.rs:18`, `../sidestr-rs/sidestr-core/tests/pegtweak.rs:406`); "a reported pass alone is insufficient parity evidence."
- Failure: An environment or path change makes oracle fixtures unresolvable; parity suites report green while exercising none of the reference comparisons, and a compatibility regression ships.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-14 — Decision records and configuration contradict the code and runtime
- Topics: SR-01.2, SR-01.3, SR-01.4
- Evidence: "ADR-0001 still says four references; CI now pins six" (`../sidestr-rs/docs/adr/ADR-0001-reference-oracle-is-the-compatibility-contract.md:27` vs `../sidestr-rs/.github/workflows/ci.yml:17`); TODO N-10's "claim that Hitch has no host is now stale" (`../project/docs/TODO-unified.md:103`); the second chain table "is enabled despite the README's older disabled-default statement" (`../project/agentbox/agentbox.toml:1976` vs `../project/agentbox/config/sidechain/README.md:188`).
- Failure: An operator follows a stale record (skips a host hardening step because "no host exists", or assumes a chain is disabled) and misconfigures the live estate.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: no

### F-15 — `AssetView` reading a chain without asset rules consumes input carries on a broken tally and produces no outputs
- Topics: SR-02.2
- Evidence: "a broken tally then consumes its input carries and produces no asset outputs" (`../sidestr-rs/sidestr-core/src/assets.rs:281`, `:11`); "A missing `rules` field does not mean assets cannot be read" (SR-02.2).
- Failure: A wallet or explorer using `AssetView` on a chain without consensus asset rules encounters a transaction whose tally breaks under the reader's policy; the client's view shows the inputs as burned with no outputs — client-visible balances silently disappear.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

## solid-pod-rs / critical

### F-01 — A restrictive ACL can parse "Found" with its conditions silently discarded, granting access and marking the response Public
- Topics: SP-04.3, SP-04.4, SP-04.10, SP-04.7, SP-03.12, SP-13
- Evidence: The resolver states "Present --> Found: DEBT incomplete Turtle condition may be discarded" and the boundary note says "a lenient Turtle parse can report Found after discarding a condition" (`solid-pod-rs/src/wac/resolver.rs`; `classify_policy_read` accepts "Ok, including partially parsed rules" at `solid-pod-rs/src/wac/resolver.rs:295`). The parser drops conditions on unbalanced brackets (`solid-pod-rs/src/wac/parser.rs:317-320`) and on absent type (`solid-pod-rs/src/wac/parser.rs:418`). The evaluator grants when "ALL must be Satisfied" (`solid-pod-rs/src/wac/evaluator.rs:309`) — a dropped condition is simply absent, so the rule grants unconditionally. SP-03.12's own boundary: "Lost restrictions can misclassify a response as Public." SP-13 confirms the repair did not touch this: "Existing parser information loss … remain outside this change."
- Failure: A pod owner writes an ACL restricting a resource (payment, time or client condition) with any syntax the lenient parser tolerates; the condition vanishes, the rule becomes an unconditional grant, and the anonymous re-evaluation can classify the response Public and publicly cacheable. Present in every version in the estate, including the unrepaired line.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DEBT nodes in SP-04.3/SP-04.10; boundary in SP-03.12; exclusion note in SP-13)

### F-02 — Any storage read error makes payment state fall back to stale legacy mirrors, and the next write overwrites canonical state with them, losing payout intents
- Topics: SP-07.15, SP-10.4
- Evidence: "DRIFT: any get error falls back to legacy files version zero and no pending intents" (`solid-pod-rs-server/src/handlers/pay.rs:184`); "the next successful write can replace the canonical state with them, losing intents and resetting the version." The same topic notes the lock is process-local (SP-10.4 W4: "PAYMENT_STATE_LOCK is process-wide, not storage-wide — two replicas over one pod store share no lock", `solid-pod-rs-server/src/lib.rs:204`).
- Failure: A transient filesystem error during a pay-handler read selects the legacy mirrors (version 0, no pending intents); the next successful `/pay/*` write commits those as canonical. Recorded debit intents vanish, so a payout that was already broadcast can be repeated — double spend — or balances silently reset. A second server process over the same pod root races the same way.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DRIFT in SP-07.15; W4 in SP-10.4)

### F-03 — The provenance commit endpoint serves private-resource provenance and sidecar text with no WAC gate
- Topics: SP-07.6
- Evidence: "DRIFT: no resource WAC gate or sidecar commit check; anchored is a substring check for bt:txid" (`solid-pod-rs-server/src/handlers/prov.rs:413`); the Tension note: "the unauthenticated commit query returned their metadata and sidecar text" while direct reads of the same resources 401. Also "accepted: replace current sidecar even for old commit; write failure logged, still 200 and charged" (`handlers/prov.rs:584`).
- Failure: An unauthenticated caller enumerating commit identifiers reads PROV-O sidecar content and anchor state for resources whose ACL denies Read — a WAC bypass on a read path. Separately, an owner anchoring an old commit overwrites the current resource's provenance record, and a failed sidecar write still returns 200 with the caller charged.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift and Tension markers in SP-07.6)

### F-04 — Git clone/fetch resolves the wrong repository (double pod-join), and its permission model is root-Read only — export is broken today and would leak private history once routing is fixed
- Topics: SP-14.1, SP-14.2, SP-06.12
- Evidence: `/alice/info/refs` becomes `data_root/alice/alice` (`solid-pod-rs-git/src/service.rs:299`, `guard.rs:92`) while "the normal write hook records files under `data_root/alice`, so this is a different repository selection." Read admission checks "Read … only on the pod-root resource" (`solid-pod-rs-server/src/lib.rs:4792`, `:4797`) and the CGI serves with "no child ACL walk here" (`service.rs:378`); SP-06.12: "Read admission names the root resource, not every private child or historical version in the selected repository."
- Failure: Today a normal pod's clone 404s (export broken); if a nested repository exists at `data_root/{pod}/{pod}` the route serves that repository's full object store to anyone holding root Read. Fixing only the join — which SP-14 warns against — connects the real history to a gate that exports every private child and every historical version on a public root grant.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt/Boundary in SP-06.12; the whole SP-14 topic states both halves as open)

### F-05 — The explorer/mempool client defaults to public testnet4, follows redirects to unchecked destinations, and its ambiguous "presence" drives fund-moving recovery
- Topics: SP-10.6, SP-07.11, SP-07.15, SP-06.9
- Evidence: "public testnet4 default" (`solid-pod-rs-server/src/mempool.rs:60`, D7); "one base URL, default redirect policy" (`mempool.rs:87`) and the audit probe observed "a local cross-origin redirect" being followed; presence is "404 false, other errors ambiguous, any successful status true without body validation" (`mempool.rs:456`); network identity is URL-inferred, "not a chain identity query" (`mempool.rs:201`); recovery "trusts explorer presence, advances a saved trail when behind and reverses a payout on 404" (`handlers/pay.rs:242`). The SSRF consumer list in SP-06.9 names OIDC, did:nostr, the proxy and ActivityPub — not the mempool client.
- Failure: An operator who does not set the mempool URL anchors and verifies deposits against testnet. A redirecting or hostile explorer flips "presence", reversing user payouts or advancing trails on false data; the redirect-following is exactly the class SP-12 fixed for OIDC but left here, and the mempool client appears to have no SSRF pinning at all (this last half is inferred from the consumer list's silence).
- Confidence: high (facts stated; exploit chain via redirect inferred)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: partially (D7 marked in SP-10.6; the redirect and presence lines in SP-07.11 carry no marker)

### F-06 — The four newest security repairs exist only in producer source; every named deployment runs without them, and one running container's version is unknown
- Topics: SP-09.7, SP-11, SP-12, SP-13, SP-14
- Evidence: SP-11 (empty-body payload binding), SP-12 (refuse redirected OIDC discovery/JWKS), SP-13 (candidate ACL control) and SP-14 sit at later commits (`8f2df86`, `fcc624a`, `daff10d`, `1a66c2d`) than the estate: VisionClaw requires alpha.17 (`../project/Cargo.toml:245`), agentbox's Nix pin is "CONFIGURED … the running container still on the old pin" (`../project/agentbox/lib/solid-pod-rs.nix:54-58`), the production edge "still runs kit 9c36b60, whose manifest asks alpha.14 and whose lock holds alpha.17 with crypto-bigint 0.5.5 and k256 0.13.4". SP-11/SP-12: "Release, consumer adoption and deployment remain open."
- Failure: The empty-body binding fix absent means a captured signed PUT can still be replayed (within the replay scope) to blank a private resource on every deployed instance; the OIDC redirect SSRF gap is likewise live in production. The old agentbox container's actual version is unrecorded, so it may predate the alpha.13 MCP fix and the alpha.13 yank.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Open markers throughout SP-09.7 and SP-11–14)

### F-07 — The DPoP jti cache evicts unexpired entries at capacity, contradicting the project's own replay invariant
- Topics: SP-05.7, SP-10.1, SP-05.5
- Evidence: "capacity insertion evicts LRU, even unexpired" (`solid-pod-rs/src/oidc/replay.rs:177`), fed by "wrapper claims jti when cache supplied" (`oidc/mod.rs:420`). Invariant 2 in SP-10.1: replay protection "never evicts an unexpired entry" (`solid-pod-rs/src/auth/replay.rs:275`), and the ReplayStore seam mandates "No live eviction and no refresh on rejection" (`auth/replay_store.rs:108`). The NIP-98 store refuses at capacity; the OIDC cache evicts.
- Failure: Under load the cache fills; an attacker who captured a DPoP-bound access token and proof can flood jtis until the victim's entry is evicted, then replay the proof within the token's lifetime. The stated invariant is false for the OIDC half.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-08 — NIP-98 replay protection is process-local: a restart or a second replica reopens the replay window for a full TTL
- Topics: SP-05.4, SP-05.5, SP-10.4, SP-11
- Evidence: "Restart forgets ids, replicas do not share state. Reuse is possible while the token remains fresh" (`solid-pod-rs/src/auth/replay_store.rs:115`); TTL "is not validated against token freshness" (`auth/replay.rs:133`, `:149`); the freshness window itself is two-sided 60 s (`auth/nip98.rs:28`). SP-11 confirms post-repair: "retention, restart/replica scope and GitLenient scoping remain open."
- Failure: A pod restarted (deploy, crash, supervisor cycling) accepts any NIP-98 event captured within the TTL; two replicas behind one hostname each keep their own store, so a request replayed against the other replica verifies fresh.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (LIMIT/SEAM in SP-05.5; W3 in SP-10.4; Open in SP-11)

### F-09 — Storage writes violate the advertised atomic contract: body and .meta sidecar are two separate renames, and the write-then-provenance-mark window is not atomic
- Topics: SP-06.4, SP-10.4, SP-07.1, SP-10.8
- Evidence: "the rename is atomic per file, but the body and its sidecar are two renames — a crash between them leaves a mismatched pair" (`solid-pod-rs/src/storage/fs.rs:111`; audit finding F5); W1: "a crash between the storage put and the git commit leaves an unmarked resource" (`solid-pod-rs-server/src/lib.rs:1518`); DELETE additionally "bypasses marking and leaves the old sidecar" (`lib.rs:2276`).
- Failure: A crash mid-write pairs a new body with a stale ETag/content-type (breaking conditional requests and conneg) or an old body with new metadata; a crash after PUT-but-before-mark leaves resources whose provenance claims are false, and a deleted resource keeps asserting its pre-delete provenance.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE in SP-06.4; W1/W2 in SP-10.4; DRIFT in SP-07.1)

### F-10 — The acl:origin gate is compiled out of every default build, so the Origin header is ignored entirely
- Topics: SP-04.8, SP-01.6
- Evidence: "acl-origin is NOT in any default feature set (SP-01.6), so a stock build ignores the Origin header entirely and a cross-origin write is gated only by the agent's credential" (`solid-pod-rs/src/wac/evaluator.rs:352`, feature at `crates/solid-pod-rs/Cargo.toml:173`).
- Failure: Any deployment built with default features has no origin-based CSRF surface check; a malicious page that can cause a credentialed request (any future cookie/brokered-credential path, or a leaked bearer such as the forge `f1.*` token, which is header-only and origin-blind) performs cross-origin writes with no additional gate.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE in SP-04.8 and SP-01.6 O1)

### F-11 — The entire /pay/* surface is registered unconditionally with no feature flag, while the README says not to carry value through it
- Topics: SP-07.14
- Evidence: "the whole /pay/* surface is registered with the SAME gating as /pay/.info — always on, with no payments feature flag" (`solid-pod-rs-server/src/lib.rs:5262`); "The README's status section says not to carry value through these routes until the audit findings are fixed."
- Failure: Every stock binary — including deployments that never configured payments — exposes deposit, withdraw, swap, pool and buy routes handling ledger balances. One unauthenticated-surface bug in that family (cf. the alpha.13 MCP finding) is reachable everywhere by default.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE in SP-07.14)

### F-12 — Failed payment refunds are only "attempted"; no repayment worker exists, so user funds can be stranded
- Topics: SP-07.6
- Evidence: "a failed refund is only *attempted* in the pending file; a second storage failure is logged at solid-pod-rs-server/src/handlers/prov.rs:692. No repayment worker was found … This is not a guaranteed durable refund." Also "pending error: 202, keep charge" (`handlers/prov.rs:527`).
- Failure: A user is charged for an anchor; the anchor errors and the compensating refund write fails (same transient storage class as F-02). The debit is recorded on nothing durable; nothing retries; the satoshis are owed forever with only a log line.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt in SP-07.6)

### F-13 — The server advertises DPoP and Bearer in its 401 challenge but cannot authenticate either: production treats every Bearer as anonymous and has no DPoP dispatch
- Topics: SP-12.2, SP-04.14, SP-05.1
- Evidence: "resource extractor handles Nostr; production Bearer refused, no DPoP dispatch" (`solid-pod-rs-server/src/lib.rs:603`, `:635`); "production build: every Bearer header is anonymous" (`lib.rs:617`); yet denial carries "WWW-Authenticate: Nostr, DPoP, Bearer — all three realms" (`lib.rs:1160`). SP-12's own summary: "OIDC components do not yet provide an external-client private-resource journey on the main pod server."
- Failure: A standards-compliant Solid-OIDC client follows the advertised challenge, presents a valid DPoP-bound token the library can verify (SP-05.7/05.8), and is still 401-anonymous against the pod — the Solid-OIDC path is a dead loop, and the challenge actively misleads clients about what the server accepts.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no (stated as fact in SP-12, unmarked as a defect)

### F-14 — On a busy port the server silently binds a shifted port and rewrites base_url from it
- Topics: SP-02.7, SP-10.4
- Evidence: `AddrInUse` retries 11 times then "warn port busy — shifted listener" (`solid-pod-rs-server/src/main.rs:206`, `:213`); "the actual port is re-read from local_addr, so base_url reflects the shift."
- Failure: A supervisor restart overlapping the old process yields two live instances — the old one on the configured port, the new one shifted — both writing the same FsBackend while the replay store, payment lock and trail locks are all process-local (W3/W4/W8): precisely the two-writer scenario the material says is unsafe. Alternatively the new instance runs on a port no load balancer targets, while advertised URLs (Updates-via, webhook base) now name the shifted port.
- Confidence: medium (behavior stated; the two-instance consequence is inferred)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-15 — Two topics at the same verified commit make opposite claims about the MCP critical finding and the README's current text
- Topics: SP-08.14, SP-10.8, SP-09.7
- Evidence: SP-08.14 (verified_commit `09ec90f`): "the README's status section records 'anonymous MCP reads / WAC sidecar bypass' as a reproduced CRITICAL finding and says to keep MCP disabled." SP-10.8 (same commit `09ec90f`): F2 "CLOSED in 0.5.0-alpha.13" and "The README no longer lists these findings or says to keep MCP disabled (`../solid-pod-rs/README.md:175`)."
- Failure: One of these topics quotes a README state the other says does not exist. If the SP-08.14 reading is current, MCP carries an unremediated critical on any build with `--mcp`; if SP-10.8 is current, SP-08.14's operational guidance is stale. Either way, sign-off on the MCP surface — and on any deployment with MCP enabled — cannot proceed until an engineer reads the actual README and CHANGELOG at `09ec90f`.
- Confidence: high (the textual contradiction is direct)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

## Not judgeable from this material

1. The actual version and feature set of the solid-pod-rs-server binary inside the currently running agentbox container ("the old pin" is never identified), and whether MCP or /pay are enabled in any live deployment.
2. Whether the shipped server passes a real `GroupMembership` registry into `evaluate_access_ctx_with_registry` — SP-04.15 names `NoGroupMembership` as "the server default" but never states what the server actually wires, so `acl:agentGroup` may be silently non-functional in production.
3. Whether the mempool/explorer HTTP client applies any SSRF policy or IP pinning — it is absent from SP-06.9's explicit consumer list, but absence from a diagram is not proof of absence in code.
4. The current README status text and CHANGELOG state at `09ec90f` (to resolve F-15), and whether commits `8f2df86`, `fcc624a`, `daff10d`, `1a66c2d` are tagged or scheduled for an alpha.19 release.
5. Deployment topology per instance: single process or multiple replicas behind one pod root, and what proxy/LB sits in front (determines whether F-02/F-08/F-14's two-writer scenarios are live or latent, and whether `trust_proxy`/`allowed_hosts` defaults matter).

## solid-pod-rs / premortem

# Post-mortem: solid-pod-rs production incident (assumed custody/data-loss class)

Scope note: this report is derived only from the diagrams-as-code distillation and the path:line citations it carries, at the revisions recorded per topic (SP-01..SP-10 at `09ec90f`, SP-11 at `8f2df86`, SP-12 at `fcc624a`, SP-13 at `daff10d`, SP-14 at `1a66c2d`). Every claim below should be re-verified against code before action. Where a chain depends on deployment behaviour the material does not assert, confidence is marked accordingly.

The three candidates below are the ones the material itself arms most heavily: an always-on payments surface the README explicitly warns not to carry value on, a git export path whose authorisation gate the material states does not match the data it serves, and an unauthenticated provenance query the material records leaking private metadata in a live probe.

---

### R-1 — Value on the always-on `/pay/*` surface is lost when a payment-state read fails or a second process serves the pod
- Topics: SP-07.13, SP-07.14, SP-07.15, SP-07.18, SP-07.6, SP-10.4, SP-02.7, SP-09.7
- Chain of events: operator enables deposits/trading on a shipped build → `/pay/*` is registered unconditionally, with no feature flag → a transient storage error on `state.json` makes `read_state_unchecked` fall back to the legacy mirror files (version 0, no pending intents) → the next successful write commits those stale mirrors over the canonical bundled state → payout intents recorded before broadcast vanish → a withdrawal already broadcast has no replay guard on retry (double payout), or balances silently revert. Independently: any second process over the same pod store (a duplicate instance after the busy-port shift) shares neither `PAYMENT_STATE_LOCK` nor the trail/epoch locks, so two writers race the ledger. Failed explicit anchors keep their charge — refund is only attempted once, and no repayment worker exists.
- Evidence: routes always on with `/pay/.info`'s gating, README says do not carry value until audit findings are fixed (solid-pod-rs-server/src/lib.rs:5262, SP-07.14 N2); any get error falls back to legacy files, version zero, no pending intents (solid-pod-rs-server/src/handlers/pay.rs:184), commit_state then overwrites canonical (handlers/pay.rs:198) — "a read outage can select stale mirrors and the next successful write can replace the canonical state with them, losing intents and resetting the version" (SP-07.15); recovery trusts explorer presence — "bounded recovery rules, not proof of finality" (handlers/pay.rs:242); payout intent stored before broadcast (handlers/pay.rs:1322); `PAYMENT_STATE_LOCK` process-local (solid-pod-rs-server/src/lib.rs:208; SP-10.4 W4), trail/epoch locks in-process and held across network calls (CHANGELOG.md:251; W8); ledger authority is local — "Genesis identity does not authenticate the changing balances", `write_ledger` public (solid-pod-rs/src/payments.rs:412, :913, :372); ADR-2008 D4 absent (SP-07.18); refund only attempted, no repayment worker found (solid-pod-rs-server/src/handlers/prov.rs:692); busy-port shift silently binds a shifted port (solid-pod-rs-server/src/main.rs:206, :213); agentbox's API points callers at the pod's `/pay/.deposit` (../project/agentbox/management-api/routes/payments.js:278-304).
- Warning signs visible today: the README warning itself; the Debt/Drift labels on SP-07.15; W4/W8 recorded as "open by construction"; the deposit route is correctly MRC20-only (SP-07.17) — the exposure is on the ledger/withdrawal side, which is exactly where the material concentrates its caveats.
- Confidence: high — every mechanism is stated outright; only the triggering storage error or duplicate process is scenario-level (that coexistence is inferred).
- Severity: high

### R-2 — A Read grant on a pod's root container exports every private child and every historical version over git clone/fetch
- Topics: SP-06.11, SP-06.12, SP-14.1, SP-14.2, SP-05.3, SP-01.8, SP-08.18
- Chain of events: a shipped build compiles the git feature (agentbox's `forge-full` includes git) → `git_mark_write` commits private LDP writes into `data_root/{pod}` history → a client holding only `acl:Read` on the pod-root container (or anyone, if the root is public) runs `git clone`/`git fetch` → the server refuses push (403) but serves clone and fetch after a single WAC check on the pod-ROOT resource only → the entire object store, including private children and superseded historical versions the owner has since restricted or deleted, leaves the pod. Compounding: the adapter applies the pod component twice (`data_root/alice/alice`), so a nested repository of that shape is served instead — an unintended repository. GitLenient credential matching is a raw URL prefix match that admits sibling names.
- Evidence: "Read admission names the root resource, not every private child or historical version in the selected repository" (SP-06.12, solid-pod-rs-server/src/lib.rs:4784); clone/fetch checks Read only on the pod-root (solid-pod-rs-server/src/lib.rs:4792, :4797); "no child ACL walk here" (solid-pod-rs-git/src/service.rs:378); "A public container grant does not establish permission to export every private child or previous version" (SP-14.2); private content committed via `git_mark_write` (solid-pod-rs-server/src/lib.rs:3952, solid-pod-rs-git/src/mark.rs:168); double-join to `data_root/alice/alice` (service.rs:295, :299, guard.rs:92) — "an existing nested repository changes which data the route serves" (SP-14.1); prefix matching admits sibling names, body not hash-bound (solid-pod-rs-git/src/auth.rs:154, :148); forge-full compiled into agentbox (../project/agentbox/lib/solid-pod-rs.nix:18).
- Warning signs visible today: SP-14 exists as an open topic and states "The two paths need a common export-authority contract" — i.e., no such contract exists at the newest recorded revision; the alpha.13 yank history (COPY source auth, glob children — CHANGELOG.md:189, :194) shows this estate has shipped authorisation-scope bugs before; the push-guard machinery ships but is unreachable (SP-06.12 Debt).
- Confidence: high (gate scope and selection behaviour are stated; unrepaired through the SP-14 revision; that a real deployment holds a public or shared root is inferred).
- Severity: high

### R-3 — The provenance commit query serves private resources' metadata and sidecar text with no WAC gate
- Topics: SP-07.6, SP-02.10, SP-01.8
- Chain of events: git feature compiled in → `handlers::prov::register` mounts `_prov` routes before the LDP catch-all → an unauthenticated `GET` on the commit endpoint resolves Git metadata and the first content path → reads the current resource sidecar directly from storage → no resource WAC gate and no check that the sidecar belongs to that commit → the response discloses private resources' provenance metadata and sidecar text that the same client cannot read via LDP.
- Evidence: "no resource WAC gate or sidecar commit check" and "anchored is a substring check for bt:txid" (solid-pod-rs-server/src/handlers/prov.rs:413); commit resolution at handlers/prov.rs:351, sidecar read at :391; route registration under `cfg(feature git)` (solid-pod-rs-server/src/lib.rs:5392); the material records the live contrast: "private resource and sidecar reads returned 401 in the audit fixture, while the unauthenticated commit query returned their metadata and sidecar text" (SP-07.6 Tension).
- Warning signs visible today: the Drift and Tension labels in SP-07.6; no later topic (SP-11 through SP-14, which record the empty-body, OIDC-fetch, ACL-control and mirror repairs) records a repair of this endpoint; the substring `anchored` check is a correctness defect independently of the leak.
- Confidence: high — the leak was reproduced in the material's own fixture; its persistence to today is inferred from the absence of any recorded repair.
- Severity: high

---

### F-01 — Malformed Turtle ACL conditions are silently dropped, so a restricted policy can evaluate as Public
- Topics: SP-04.3, SP-04.10, SP-03.12, SP-04.13
- Evidence: "a lenient Turtle parse can report Found after discarding a condition" (SP-04.3 Debt); unbalanced brackets stop condition parsing (solid-pod-rs/src/wac/parser.rs:317-320); absent condition type returns None (parser.rs:418); "Lost restrictions can misclassify a response as Public. Headers do not prove revocation through an intermediary cache" (SP-03.12 Boundary); Public responses get media-type cache policy, not `no-store` (solid-pod-rs/src/ldp.rs:1935).
- Failure: an owner writes a payment- or agent-restricted ACL with a syntactically imperfect condition; the parser drops it; the resource classifies Public, an intermediary caches the body, and pay-per-read rules never charge. A dropped `PaymentCondition` also means the read is served free (SP-04.13 charges only the parsed granting rule).
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt, Boundary)

### F-02 — Replay protection is process-local and its TTL is unvalidated against token freshness
- Topics: SP-05.2, SP-05.4, SP-05.5, SP-10.4
- Evidence: "Restart forgets ids, replicas do not share state. Reuse is possible while the token remains fresh" (solid-pod-rs/src/auth/replay_store.rs:115); TTL accepts any positive seconds and the constructor permits zero (auth/replay.rs:133, :149); freshness window is two-sided 60 s (auth/nip98.rs:28); W3 "reopens the window for one TTL" (SP-10.4).
- Failure: a captured NIP-98 event replayed against a restarted pod or a second replica inside the freshness window performs an authenticated write; a TTL of zero makes every replayed id refresh its own slot, disabling the store entirely.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (SP-05.4/05.5 LIMIT notes; SP-10.4 recorded window)

### F-03 — Body and `.meta` sidecar are two separate atomic renames
- Topics: SP-06.4, SP-10.4
- Evidence: "the rename is atomic per file, but the body and its sidecar are two renames — a crash between them leaves a mismatched pair" (solid-pod-rs/src/storage/fs.rs:111; SP-06.4 DIVERGENCE; W2).
- Failure: a crash mid-put leaves an ETag/size/content-type pair describing the previous body; conditional requests and caches then validate the wrong version, serving stale content as fresh.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE; W2)

### F-04 — DELETE bypasses provenance marking and leaves the old sidecar
- Topics: SP-07.1, SP-07.2
- Evidence: "DELETE bypasses marking and leaves the old sidecar. Not every LDP mutation has a receipt" (solid-pod-rs-server/src/lib.rs:2276; SP-07.1 DRIFT).
- Failure: a deleted resource's provenance record — including anchored claims — persists and describes content that no longer exists; a client auditing custody through provenance sees a record with no tombstone.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DRIFT)

### F-05 — Epoch batching drops the triggering commit at the backlog cap and can double-anchor after a failed reset
- Topics: SP-07.7, SP-07.5, SP-07.3
- Evidence: backlog cap omits the new commit while still retrying the old batch (handlers/prov.rs:294); reset and completion-marker both failing allows a later push to anchor again (prov.rs:328); epoch success/failure only logged, receipt never updated (solid-pod-rs-server/src/lib.rs:4066); epoch outcome absent from per-resource receipts (SP-07.5).
- Failure: a write the client believes epoch-anchored was silently dropped; a retried batch anchors the same commits twice; the response header cannot reveal either.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DEBT, DRIFT)

### F-06 — Explicit-anchor refunds are single-attempt with no repayment worker; retries re-charge
- Topics: SP-07.6
- Evidence: "a failed refund is only attempted... a second storage failure is logged... No repayment worker was found" (solid-pod-rs-server/src/handlers/prov.rs:692); a retry with a fresh NIP-98 event is a fresh charge (SP-07.6); pending errors keep the charge (prov.rs:527).
- Failure: a client is charged for anchors that failed, has no automatic path to recovery, and is re-charged on every retry.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt)

### F-07 — The anchor endpoint replaces the current sidecar even when anchoring an old commit
- Topics: SP-07.6
- Evidence: "accepted: replace current sidecar even for old commit; write failure logged, still 200 and charged" (solid-pod-rs-server/src/handlers/prov.rs:584).
- Failure: the provenance sidecar no longer describes the stored content — it describes a historical commit — while the client sees 200; downstream auditors correlate the wrong state.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no (acknowledged in "What it shows" prose, but carries no Debt/Drift/Open label)

### F-08 — The mempool client follows redirects by default, and payout recovery trusts explorer presence
- Topics: SP-07.11, SP-07.15
- Evidence: "one base URL, default redirect policy" with an observed followed cross-origin redirect (solid-pod-rs-server/src/mempool.rs:87); recovery "trusts explorer presence, advances a saved trail when behind and reverses a payout on 404... not proof of finality or safe absence across explorers" (handlers/pay.rs:242, SP-07.15 Debt).
- Failure: a redirecting or spoofed explorer endpoint drives recovery: a fake 404 reverses a payout whose transaction confirmed; a fake presence advances a trail that was never anchored.
- Confidence: medium — redirect-following is stated; the exploitation chain is inferred.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (the recovery half is labelled Debt)

### F-09 — GitmarkAnchorer recovery accepts altered trail metadata and publishes non-intact trails
- Topics: SP-07.20, SP-07.12
- Evidence: "altered proposed states can be saved and published. Transaction identity check does not bind that metadata" (solid-pod-rs-server/src/mempool.rs:1011, DRIFT); "the audit probe recovers altered state metadata and publishes a non-intact trail"; settle trusts the longer proposed trail (mempool.rs:1147); acceptance is not confirmation (SP-07.12).
- Failure: anyone able to influence stored trail files (disk, backup restore, a compromised write path) publishes a trail whose states no longer match its on-chain commits, and clients anchoring trust to it are misled.
- Confidence: high (reproduced in the material's probe)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DRIFT)

### F-10 — OIDC discovery and the verifier disagree on algorithms
- Topics: SP-05.8, SP-10.1, SP-10.6
- Evidence: discovery advertises ES256/RS256 while verifiers accept a broader set (oidc/mod.rs:184, :541, :859); the verifier accepts EdDSA (oidc/mod.rs:549); invariant 6 "currently BROKEN in the shipped direction" (SP-10.1 I6, D3).
- Failure: a client that relies on the discovery document to select keys rejects legitimate tokens, or an attacker persuades a verifier-side path the metadata never promised — either way the metadata is not a safe contract.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE register)

### F-11 — The Origin gate is off by default and the default CORS policy echoes any origin
- Topics: SP-04.8, SP-01.6, SP-06.10, SP-02.3
- Evidence: acl-origin is in no default feature set, so "a stock build ignores the Origin header entirely" (SP-01.6 O1, SP-04.8 DIVERGENCE, evaluator.rs:352); "an empty allowed_origins list means every Origin is echoed back — a local-dev default, not a production one" (solid-pod-rs-server/src/lib.rs:3505; cors.rs:241-243).
- Failure: a malicious page that induces a user-assisted NIP-98 signature (e.g. a NIP-07 extension prompt) gets its cross-origin request both un-gated by `acl:origin` and approved by echoed CORS headers, then reads the response.
- Confidence: medium — both halves are stated; the browser chain stitching them is inferred.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE for the origin gate)

### F-12 — The container-ACL location differs across revisions, so an ACL written to the documented path may never be read
- Topics: SP-04.5, SP-13.2
- Evidence: SP-04.5 states `/.acl` and `/dir/.acl` govern `/` and `/dir/` while `/a/b.acl` governs `/a/b` (solid-pod-rs/src/wac/mod.rs:221); SP-13.2 states `/shared/` maps to `/shared.acl` (ldp.rs:152, :1859; resolver.rs:333) and that misplaced old inner policies are not migrated.
- Failure: an operator following the older convention writes the restriction to the location the resolver no longer reads; the resource inherits from an ancestor that may be permissive, and the intended restriction silently never applies.
- Confidence: medium — the two statements conflict as recorded; which is current, and the exposure consequence, must be checked in code.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-13 — The Solid Notifications 0.2 channels show no per-subscription WAC authorisation
- Topics: SP-08.1, SP-08.2, SP-08.4
- Evidence: the legacy channel needs `SubscriptionAuthorizer` precisely because otherwise "a subscriber would learn that a private resource CHANGED" (notifications/legacy.rs:152); the 0.2 WebSocket/Webhook subscribe path (notifications/mod.rs:278, :383) shows no authoriser step, and the pump broadcasts to topic subscribers unconditionally (mod.rs:316).
- Failure: anyone subscribing to a topic receives change notifications — URIs and change types — for resources they cannot read; whether a revocation later cancels a live subscription is also unstated.
- Confidence: medium (inferred from absence in the diagrams; absence is not proof).
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-14 — All payment, trail and epoch serialisation is process-local, but nothing prevents two processes over one pod store
- Topics: SP-10.4, SP-02.7, SP-07.7, SP-07.15
- Evidence: `PAYMENT_STATE_LOCK` process-wide (lib.rs:208; W4); trail and epoch locks in-process, held across network calls (CHANGELOG.md:251; W8); "Multi-process safety... not established" (SP-07.7 Open); the server silently shifts ports when busy and keeps serving (main.rs:206, :213).
- Failure: an operator or orchestrator starts a second instance (or a shifted-port duplicate survives); both mutate `state.json`, trails and epochs concurrently — lost updates and double payouts with no error anywhere.
- Confidence: high (the lock scope is stated; the second process is the inferred deployment step).
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (recorded windows; Open)

### F-15 — Production consumers run version- and advisory-skewed builds
- Topics: SP-09.7, SP-09.4, SP-01.11
- Evidence: the production edge runs kit `9c36b60` whose lock holds alpha.17 with crypto-bigint 0.5.5 and k256 0.13.4 while main is on alpha.18 (SP-09.7); RUSTSEC-2026-0387 "exposure unassessed" (deny.toml:57-63, :63); the host sits one release behind at alpha.17; agentbox's running container is still on the old Nix pin; the README "did not establish current dependency-advisory status" (README.md:175).
- Failure: a fix or yank (like alpha.13's) never reaches a live deployment; an unassessed advisory in the OIDC dependency path of a live edge stays unmeasured until it is an incident.
- Confidence: high (skews stated); the consequence is the standard one, inferred in timing.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DEBT, Open)

---

Verification note: R-1's trigger, R-2's deployment shape, F-08's exploit chain, F-11's browser chain, F-12's current mapping and F-13's absence-of-check all require code confirmation before any remediation is scoped; everything else cites defects the material states directly.

## visionclaw / critical

### F-01 — Decision attribution to an arbitrary pubkey is taken from the request body behind a shared service key
- Topics: VC-05.7, VC-05.6, VC-04.14
- Evidence: the agentbox decision route authenticates only the shared `VISIONCLAW_AGENT_KEY` (require_agent_key, enrichment_proposals_handler.rs:141), then attribution is "64-hex broker_pubkey -> did:nostr+kg URN" from the request (field at enrichment_proposals_handler.rs:70), and the attributed owner then receives `append_derived_summary(owner_did, ...)`. The sibling decision route states the opposite as an invariant: "deciding principal is auth.pubkey, NEVER a body field, a caller cannot self-assert another agent's decision" (ADR-048, VC-05.6). VC-04.14 shows the codebase already treats agent envelopes as untrusted ("the URNs come off an agent-controlled envelope", intent_match.rs:163) — yet the identity field itself is trusted.
- Failure: any holder (or replayer) of the one shared key attributes decisions to a victim pubkey: provenance URNs minted in the victim's name, derived-summary triples appended to the victim's pod, Augmentation-Ratio/HITL KPIs skewed, and the audit trail names the victim.
- Confidence: high on the mechanism as drawn; the pod-write impact is inferred (VC-26 is not in this material)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-02 — Unauthenticated, unthrottled disk append via /api/client-logs, with no request-size middleware anywhere
- Topics: VC-01.10, VC-03.6, VC-04.25, VC-03.1
- Evidence: POST /api/client-logs is on the RbacGate public allowlist (segment prefix `api/client-logs`, rbac_gate.rs:56-60; VC-01.10 "RBAC-allowlisted") and its handler appends entries to `/app/logs/client.log` with a cap only per request (MAX_LOG_ENTRIES 1000, client_log_handler.rs:39); no rate limit covers it. Separately, no request-size/content-type layer exists — RequestSizeLimit (16 MiB default, src/utils/validation/middleware.rs:17) "is not registered by any .wrap() in src/main.rs:1048-1051" (VC-03.1).
- Failure: unauthenticated sustained POSTs (or oversized bodies) fill the container disk; SQLite and Oxigraph writes under DATA_DIR then fail mid-flight, rejecting decisions, settings and KPI rows.
- Confidence: high on the unauthenticated append and missing size layer; the disk-full cascade is inferred
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: partially — the missing RequestSizeLimit is marked DEBT (VC-03.1); the unauthenticated log-append surface is unmarked

### F-03 — Replay-cache exhaustion turns freely-generatable Nostr keys into an authentication denial of service
- Topics: VC-03.4, VC-13.1
- Evidence: validation rejects with ReplayCacheFull when "cache.len() >= REPLAY_CACHE_MAX_ENTRIES=100_000 after prune" (nip98.rs:258-262), mapped upstream to a generic auth failure (VC-03.4); TTL is 120s and the per-pubkey quota is only 1000 events per window. The claim runs after a valid self-signature, and nothing ties cache entries to authorized users — Nostr keypairs are free to create.
- Failure: ~100 generated keys × 1000 fresh kind-27235 events each (~833 req/s for 120s) fills the cache; every subsequent NIP-98 verification — REST, WS upgrade subprotocol, post-connect authenticate — fails until entries age out: a full authentication outage.
- Confidence: medium (mechanism stated; attack cost inferred)
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no

### F-04 — Every rate limit is constructed inside the per-worker closure, so 4 workers each enforce their own counter
- Topics: VC-01.3, VC-03.12, VC-01.14, VC-04.23, VC-04.11
- Evidence: the worker factory "runs the closure once per worker — .workers(4)" (src/main.rs:1265) and each RateLimit is built inside it (settings 60/min at main.rs:1144; graph 600/min scope and 120/min resources at graph/mod.rs:1524,1545-1561; workspace 60/min at workspace_handler.rs:34; semantic 30/min at semantic_handler.rs:262-292). VC-03.12's "clones share one request history" is true per instance — four closure invocations create four independent Arc-backed states. GraphExportHandler's "rate limiter, shared_graphs map" (VC-04.11) is configured in the same closure.
- Failure: all documented ceilings are effectively 4× (settings 240/min, graph 2400/min); if the export handler state is per-worker, a share created on one worker 404s on ~75% of GET /shared/{id} retrievals and its access counters split.
- Confidence: high for the rate limits (construction site and worker count both stated); the export-map consequence is inferred (construction body not shown)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-05 — /ws/client-messages accepts any non-empty string as a Bearer token and streams client messages
- Topics: VC-04.25
- Evidence: "the token's CONTENTS are never verified against NostrService, only its presence, so any non-empty string passes" (client_messages_handler.rs:131-142); the socket then drains app_state.client_message_rx every 100ms and streams `{type:client_message,...}` frames. The marked DOC-DRIFT covers only the stale comment ("allows but logs unauthenticated connections" vs the 401-on-empty); the presence-only check on non-empty tokens is not flagged.
- Failure: anyone connecting with `Authorization: Bearer x` receives the outbound client-message stream — silent information disclosure with no failed-auth log, because nothing fails.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium (high if the stream carries sensitive payloads — sensitivity not stated in the material)
- Marked by authors: partially — DOC-DRIFT on the comment only (VC-04.25)

### F-06 — RBAC role deletion is not revocation: removed users keep ambient authority
- Topics: VC-03.8, VC-05.1
- Evidence: "removal is NOT revocation: deleting the row drops the target to their AMBIENT authority (Admin if power-user, else configured_default), never denies outright. Genuine revocation requires an explicit Viewer assignment" (role_store.rs:500 remove_checked; the DELETE route returns 200 with a warning note, VC-05.1).
- Failure: an operator offboards a hostile or compromised user with DELETE /users/{pubkey}/role, sees 200 `access_revoked`, and the user retains Editor — or Admin, if listed in POWER_USER_PUBKEYS — continuing to write graph, settings and decisions.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (ADR-2010, VC-03.8 and the route's response note)

### F-07 — /api/solid/pods/init-nip98 verifies the same single-use NIP-98 event twice; the second verification must replay-fail
- Topics: VC-05.15, VC-03.4, VC-03.17, VC-03.15, VC-03.3
- Evidence: the route sits under the /api scope, so RbacGate's verify_access runs first and claims the event id; the handler then calls extract_user_identity — "SECOND, fully independent NIP-98 re-verification" (solid_proxy_handler.rs:1332, VC-05.15). But ids are single-use: "a new request presenting the same token still fails" (VC-03.17) and the claim is the final step of the same validate_nip98_token (VC-03.4). The settings extractor was specifically changed to reuse the middleware identity instead of re-verifying (VC-03.3).
- Failure: a browser provisioning a pod with `Authorization: Nostr` passes the gate, then the handler's re-verification hits TokenReplayed → 401 "NIP-98 authentication required": the route's primary documented path never succeeds; only a caller who satisfied the gate with legacy session headers (consuming no event) can complete it.
- Confidence: medium — inferred by composing three stated facts; the material never states the route fails
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no — the double-auth is labelled DIVERGENCE but presented as redundant protection, not as self-defeating

### F-08 — Payment handler debits then panics, and the charged resource is never proxied
- Topics: VC-04.27
- Evidence: at this revision "response header construction can panic after a debit (pay_handler.rs:414, :537)" — store.debit has already succeeded; VC-55 records a later fix not in this tree. Also "resource proxying is a STUB — this confirms and charges but never forwards" (pay_handler.rs:503-505).
- Failure: with PAY_ENABLED, a user is debited and then receives a panic response with no confirmation; a retry debits again. Even on the success path they are charged for a resource that is never delivered.
- Confidence: high (stated at this commit)
- Severity: high (when the feature is enabled; inert until PAY_ENABLED)
- Marked by authors: yes (diagram note deferring to VC-55; the stub and ADR-2111 proposed removal also marked)

### F-09 — Persistence closeout states no cross-store transaction and no restore correctness
- Topics: VC-01.2
- Evidence: "DIVERGENCE (BASELINE 2026-09-04 persistence closeout) — shared Oxigraph ownership establishes no cross-store transaction, no actor reload consistency, no restore correctness" (VC-01.2, on the Oxigraph store plus per-domain SQLite under DATA_DIR).
- Failure: a disk loss or bad deploy has no demonstrated recovery path: restoring Oxigraph and the per-domain SQLite files (settings, enrichment, canary, kpi) can leave them mutually inconsistent — decisions without provenance quads, KPIs without their events — and nothing verifies a restore before traffic resumes.
- Confidence: high that the properties are unestablished (stated); the incident shape is inferred
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE, VC-01.2)

### F-10 — Settings writes return 200 while persistence failure is only a warning
- Topics: VC-06.3
- Evidence: PUT /api/settings/physics mutates actor/GPU state first, then `set_setting(...)` (settings_routes.rs:667-673) — "persist fails → warn only — the response still succeeds". The SQLite write is a separate step after UpdateSettings and the broadcast.
- Failure: an operator tunes physics during a SQLite outage or disk-full: 200 returned, all sessions and the GPU converge, and a restart silently reverts to the stale row — the acknowledged configuration is lost with no error surfaced.
- Confidence: high (order and warn-only stated)
- Severity: medium

### F-11 — GPU is a hard single point of failure and init gives up permanently after 5 failures
- Topics: VC-10.4, VC-10.5, VC-10.10
- Evidence: after "failure_count > policy.max_restarts" ResourceSupervisor logs "Exceeded max initialization attempts, giving up" and schedules nothing (resource_supervisor.rs:265-271); backoff starts at 1s, so five fast failures exhaust the budget in seconds. There is no CPU fallback anywhere: "no actor now degrades to a CPU implementation" (VC-10.10) — ComputeForces drops frames, the orchestrator warns once, SSSP/stress-majorization/connected-components hard-fail.
- Failure: one transient GPU/driver failure at boot permanently disables layout, the position stream and all GPU analytics for the process lifetime; WS clients stay connected receiving nothing while /healthz stays 200.
- Confidence: high on both mechanisms (stated); permanence until process restart is inferred (no auto-retry path and no route to RestartActor is shown)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: partially — VC-10.10 is marked DIVERGENCE; the give-up state is drawn but its operational consequence is not flagged

### F-12 — /api/health route shadowing: the composite health handler is probably unreachable, contradicting three other topics
- Topics: VC-01.7, VC-01.10, VC-01.11, VC-01.12, VC-04.16, VC-08.2
- Evidence: VC-01.7 notes GET /api/health "collides with api_handler::config's own /health route (api_handler/mod.rs:110) — first registration wins". api_handler::config registers 6th (main.rs:1163, VC-01.10) before the consolidated health group (main.rs:1171-1191, VC-01.12), so api_handler's health_check wins — yet VC-04.16 and VC-08.2 both diagram /api/health as serving unified_health_check with status/issues/system/services/mcp.
- Failure: the composed health that operators and the watchdog story rely on (degraded reasons, the D5 "one MCP truth" fix, CPU/disk) is not what answers /api/health; the watchdog survives only through its "no status field → UP" fallback (VC-08.2), which that handler can presumably never turn into DOWN.
- Confidence: high on the collision and registration order; medium on the winning handler's response shape (not shown)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no — the collision is noted in passing; the contradiction with VC-04.16/VC-08.2 is left standing

### F-13 — Web client acknowledges chunks it dropped, so the server never resends those positions
- Topics: VC-13.7, VC-13.4, VC-14.10
- Evidence: marked TENSION twice — handleBinaryFrame "within 16ms of the last accepted frame ... returns normally, the frame is dropped and never reaches the worker" yet the ack is still sent (wsClient.ts:26-28, binaryProtocol.ts:392-408); the server then "records those nodes as held by this client and does not resend them until they move past the threshold" (VC-13.7). ADR-2141 decision 5 requires ack only after apply.
- Failure: during a burst (drag, layout change), every web-client frame landing inside the 16ms window is lost-but-acknowledged; once the graph settles, the affected nodes sit at stale positions on that client indefinitely — divergent views of the same graph between users, with the server believing them current.
- Confidence: high (stated)
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (TENSION, VC-13.7 and VC-14.10)

### F-14 — All auth and throttling state is process-local: the security model silently breaks at two replicas
- Topics: VC-03.4, VC-03.12, VC-09.9
- Evidence: "Process-local scope — no cross-replica replay protection (SECURITY-profiles.md)" (VC-03.4); the same holds for every in-memory rate limiter (Arc state per instance, VC-03.12) and for sessions unless REDIS_URL is set (optional, nostr_service.rs:149, VC-09.9).
- Failure: the moment a second backend replica is placed behind a load balancer, a captured NIP-98 event replays on the other replica inside its 60s window, per-minute limits double per replica, and sessions minted on one node 401 on the other unless REDIS_URL was configured.
- Confidence: high that the state is process-local; the scaling scenario is inferred (current deployment is single-container)
- Severity: medium (latent)
- Marked by authors: yes (INVARIANT note, VC-03.4)

### F-15 — The cross-boundary "single source of truth" contracts crate has zero consumers and a same-named duplicate exists
- Topics: VC-07.12, VC-14.5
- Evidence: visionclaw-contracts claims to be the "single source of truth for every envelope that crosses a process boundary" but has "verified zero current consumers on either side"; src/agent_events/schema.rs:61 defines "its OWN, INDEPENDENT AgentActionEnvelope struct — same name, not the crate's type".
- Failure: envelope changes on either side of a process boundary (schema-version bump, field additions in agentbox or the client) drift undetected — the crate's tests stay green while real producers and consumers diverge, and the next cross-repo integration builds against a spec nothing implements.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE, VC-07.12)

## Not judgeable from this material

1. Whether REST graph reads (GET /api/graph/data, /node/{id}/relations, /api/schema, /api/pages) apply the pubkey private-node filter — VC-03.9 documents it only for the WS position stream and the WS initial load; the REST handlers shown apply population and linked-page filters only.
2. Whether nginx sets X-Forwarded-For/X-Real-IP so RateLimit's `realip_remote_addr` key (VC-03.12, use_user_id off) distinguishes real clients — otherwise the 60/min settings budget is one global bucket shared by everyone.
3. Whether solid_proxy_handler's extract_user_identity (solid_proxy_handler.rs:188) reuses the RbacGate identity from request extensions before re-verifying — this alone decides F-07.
4. Where handler-local state is constructed relative to the per-worker closure (GraphExportHandler's limiter and shared_graphs map, the memory-cloud snapshot cache) — shared Arc versus four instances decides the export half of F-04.
5. The network boundary and rotation story for VISIONCLAW_AGENT_KEY and the agentbox containers — which containers can reach /api/enrichment-proposals and /api/canary directly, since F-01's blast radius depends on who can hold the shared key.

## visionclaw / premortem

# Post-Mortem: Production Incident — VisionClaw Server

**Date of incident:** Twelve months after the verified commit `8692b12ba987ecb87165566beb7c320a2446e4b4`.
**Nature:** Custody failure and data exposure. A paying client was charged for resources that were never delivered, and an unauthenticated party gained access to the outbound client-message stream. The client terminated their contract.

---

## Root Causes

### R-1 — The pay handler debits users, then panics during response construction or delivers nothing — a direct custody failure

- **Topics:** VC-04.27, VC-09.13
- **Chain of events:**
  1. The operator enables `PAY_ENABLED=true` to activate the HTTP 402 payment gate.
  2. A client calls `GET /pay/{resource_path}` with valid NIP-98 authentication.
  3. The handler resolves the caller's DID, queries `FsPaymentStore.get_balance`, and succeeds.
  4. `config.cost_for_endpoint` calculates the charge; `store.debit(&did, cost)` succeeds and persists.
  5. The handler attempts to build the success response; header-name conversion panics at `pay_handler.rs:537`. The client receives an error or connection reset. The debit is already persisted.
  6. The client retries, is debited again, and again receives no resource. Even if the panic did not fire, the resource proxy is a stub that "confirms and charges but never forwards to the underlying resource handler" (`pay_handler.rs:503-505`), so the client would receive a 200 confirming the charge with no underlying resource delivered.
  7. The client attempts to top up their balance and hits `POST /pay/.deposit`, which returns 501 Not Implemented ("Contact the server operator for manual funding", `pay_handler.rs:487-492`). There is no self-service funding path.
  8. The operator has no automated refund mechanism; ADR-2111, which would delete the entire payment store and replace it with a proxy, is still in `proposed` status with `implementation_status: none`.
- **Evidence:**
  - "response header construction can panic after a debit pay_handler.rs:537" (VC-04.27).
  - "resource proxying is a STUB — this confirms and charges but never forwards to the underlying resource handler (pay_handler.rs:503-505)" (VC-04.27).
  - "POST /pay/.deposit → 501 Not Implemented — 'Contact the server operator for manual funding' (:487-492)" (VC-04.27).
  - "PROPOSED, NOT LIVE — ADR-2111 (decision_status proposed, implementation_status none, 2026-09-21)" (VC-04.27).
  - "PAY routes are mounted UNCONDITIONALLY under feature solid-pod-embed at src/main.rs:1097-1105 and stay inert until PAY_ENABLED=true" (VC-09.13).
- **Warning signs visible today:** The diagram already labels this section with "At this topic's declared revision, auth calls the stateless Solid verifier with body=None and response header construction can panic after a debit" and "resource proxying is a STUB." Every ingredient of the failure is individually documented.
- **Confidence:** high
- **Severity:** high

### R-2 — The `/ws/client-messages` WebSocket authenticates on token presence only, not contents — unauthenticated access to the outbound message stream

- **Topics:** VC-04.25
- **Chain of events:**
  1. An unauthenticated party sends `GET /ws/client-messages` with `Authorization: Bearer x` (any non-empty string).
  2. The handler checks only that the token is present and non-empty — "the token's CONTENTS are never verified against NostrService, only its presence, so any non-empty string passes" (`client_messages_handler.rs:131-142`).
  3. The WebSocket upgrade succeeds (101 Switching Protocols).
  4. `start_message_stream()` runs every 100 ms and drains `app_state.client_message_rx`, sending each queued message to the connected client.
  5. The unauthenticated party receives `{type:client_message, content, timestamp, session_id, agent_id}` frames containing message content, session identifiers and agent identifiers that were intended for legitimate clients.
  6. The client whose data was exposed discovers their session IDs and message content are available to anyone with a curl command and terminates the contract.
- **Evidence:**
  - "the token's CONTENTS are never verified against NostrService, only its presence, so any non-empty string passes" (VC-04.25, DOC-DRIFT note on `client_messages_handler.rs:114-116` vs `:131-142`).
  - "start_message_stream() — ctx.run_interval(100ms) drains app_state.client_message_rx" (VC-04.25).
  - "text {type:client_message, content, timestamp, session_id, agent_id}" (VC-04.25).
- **Warning signs visible today:** The DOC-DRIFT note already flags that the code comment says "allows but logs unauthenticated connections" while the code actually rejects empty tokens — but the real defect, that any non-empty string passes, is stated explicitly in the same note.
- **Confidence:** high
- **Severity:** high

### R-3 — Role removal is not revocation: a demoted user silently retains Editor (WriteGraph) via the ambient default

- **Topics:** VC-03.7, VC-03.8, VC-05.1
- **Chain of events:**
  1. An operator identifies a compromised or departing user and calls `DELETE /api/admin/rbac/users/{pubkey}/role` to revoke their access.
  2. `RoleStore::remove_checked` deletes the explicit role row.
  3. The user's effective role immediately falls back to their AMBIENT authority: Admin if they are in `POWER_USER_PUBKEYS`, otherwise `configured_default` (Editor by default, per `RBAC_DEFAULT_ROLE`).
  4. The response carries `access_revoked: false` and a note, but the operator's automation or alerting may not surface this nuance.
  5. The "revoked" user still holds the Editor role, which maps to `AccessLevel::Authenticated`, granting WriteGraph — they can still call `POST /api/graph/update`, `POST /api/files/update_graph`, and every mutating route that requires only WriteGraph.
  6. The user continues to write to the knowledge graph after the operator believes they have been locked out.
- **Evidence:**
  - "removal is NOT revocation: deleting the row drops the target to their AMBIENT authority (Admin if power-user, else configured_default), never denies outright. Genuine revocation requires an explicit Viewer assignment." (VC-03.8).
  - "ADR-2010 — removal is NOT revocation (:203) — response carries 'note' warning the target may still hold effective_after by ambient default" (VC-05.1).
  - The role lattice shows Editor maps to `AccessLevel::Authenticated` and the RBAC gate classifies a mutating non-settings method as `Some(AccessLevel::WriteGraph)` (VC-03.7, VC-03.6).
  - Default role resolution: "unset or '' -> Editor (ADR-2010 compat default)" (VC-03.7).
- **Warning signs visible today:** The material documents this as designed behaviour with a warning in the response, but the same material notes that `RBAC_DEFAULT_ROLE` can only narrow to editor/viewer — meaning the ambient default is always at least Editor, always granting WriteGraph to anyone with a valid identity.
- **Confidence:** high
- **Severity:** high

---

## Further Latent Defects

### F-01 — NIP-98 replay cache is process-local; no cross-replica replay protection
- **Topics:** VC-03.4
- **Evidence:** "Process-local scope — no cross-replica replay protection (SECURITY-profiles.md)" (VC-03.4, note on the replay cache). The freshness window is ±60 s (`nip98.rs:169`) and the cache TTL is 120 s (`:178`), but the cache is a `Mutex<HashMap>` with no shared store.
- **Failure:** In a multi-replica deployment, a captured NIP-98 event replayed within 60 s to a different replica authenticates successfully because that replica's cache has never seen the event id.
- **Confidence:** high
- **Marked by authors:** no
- **Severity:** high

### F-02 — Pay deposit returns 501; there is no self-service funding path
- **Topics:** VC-04.27
- **Evidence:** "POST /pay/.deposit → 501 Not Implemented — 'Contact the server operator for manual funding' (:487-492)" (VC-04.27).
- **Failure:** An operator who enables PAY_ENABLED cannot on-board clients without a manual process; combined with R-1, a user who is accidentally over-charged cannot self-correct their balance.
- **Confidence:** high
- **Marked by authors:** yes (DIVERGENCE)
- **Severity:** medium

### F-03 — TimeoutMiddleware uses exact-path HashMap lookup; sub-paths of `/api/admin/sync` get the default 30 s, not 600 s
- **Topics:** VC-03.1
- **Evidence:** "get_timeout (timeout.rs:37-41) looks up an exact-path HashMap (endpoint_overrides) — no prefix/glob matching, so only '/api/admin/sync' itself gets 600s" (VC-03.1).
- **Failure:** A long-running sub-operation under `/api/admin/sync/` (if any exists now or is added later) times out at 30 s, potentially aborting a partially-completed administrative operation mid-flight.
- **Confidence:** medium — inferred from the stated matching behaviour; whether sub-paths exist is not confirmed.
- **Marked by authors:** no
- **Severity:** medium

### F-04 — Multi-MCP `request_agents` never reaches the discovery service
- **Topics:** VC-05.13
- **Evidence:** "DEBT: request_agents never reaches the discovery service. Handler for RequestAgentUpdate only logs at debug (:669-671) and so do the RequestPerformanceUpdate (:694-699) and RequestTopologyUpdate (:705-710) handlers, though the 100 ms position tick posts RequestAgentUpdate (:153-155). ADR-2091 addendum calls this circuit-breaker path a simulation left unchanged" (VC-05.13).
- **Failure:** A client subscribing to agent updates via the multi-MCP socket receives no real data; the circuit-breaker "simulation" masks the absence.
- **Confidence:** high
- **Marked by authors:** yes (DEBT)
- **Severity:** low

### F-05 — GPU self-init bypasses ResourceSupervisor timeouts, backoff and accounting
- **Topics:** VC-10.6
- **Evidence:** "self-init is a second, unsupervised path to a CUDA context that bypasses ResourceSupervisor timeouts and backoff. It runs only while shared_context is None... the ADR states that a context built this way is invisible to ResourceSupervisor accounting and GetSubsystemHealth, so the boot line 'GPU context self-initialized successfully' means the supervisor path failed" (VC-10.6, DIVERGENCE note).
- **Failure:** If the supervisor delivery path fails persistently, the system runs on a self-created context that health checks cannot see, so `GetSubsystemHealth` reports Initializing or Degraded while the physics loop is actually running — an observability blind spot that could mask a real GPU degradation.
- **Confidence:** high
- **Marked by authors:** yes (DIVERGENCE, recorded as deliberate)
- **Severity:** medium

### F-06 — PublicDemoGuard reads `PUBLIC_DEMO` once at app construction; runtime toggling has no effect
- **Topics:** VC-03.1
- **Evidence:** "PublicDemoGuard::from_env() reads PUBLIC_DEMO ONCE at app start (public_demo.rs:49-53) — not re-read per request, so toggling the env var at runtime has no effect until restart" (VC-03.1).
- **Failure:** An operator who discovers a abuse scenario and sets `PUBLIC_DEMO=read-only` in the running environment sees no change, concluding the guard is broken or already active when it is not. A window of unprotected writes persists until a restart.
- **Confidence:** high
- **Marked by authors:** no
- **Severity:** medium

### F-07 — Settings hot-reload watcher is disabled because it caused database deadlocks
- **Topics:** VC-06.1
- **Evidence:** "DIVERGENCE — the settings hot-reload watcher is DISABLED, src/app_state.rs:1110-1113, reason recorded in code: it was causing database deadlocks" (VC-06.1, N2).
- **Failure:** Direct changes to the settings file (by an operator or a sidecar tool) are never picked up by the running system, causing silent divergence between persisted and effective configuration. If the deadlock root cause is a lock-ordering issue in the adapter, the same pattern could resurface in the non-hot-reload write path under concurrent load.
- **Confidence:** medium — the deadlock is asserted, its recurrence in other paths is inferred.
- **Marked by authors:** yes (DIVERGENCE)
- **Severity:** medium

### F-08 — `POST /api/files/update_graph` ignores its request body and rebuilds from whatever is on disk
- **Topics:** VC-04.4
- **Evidence:** "DOC-DRIFT — files::update_graph (files/mod.rs:243) calls AddNodesFromMetadata with metadata freshly loaded from disk via load_or_create_metadata, NOT the request body — the route accepts no JSON payload despite being a POST that 'updates' the graph" (VC-04.4).
- **Failure:** A caller who POSTs a carefully-constructed update gets a full rebuild from the disk state instead. If the markdown directory on disk has been tampered with (through any write path), this route pushes the tampered content into the live graph, bypassing whatever the caller intended.
- **Confidence:** high
- **Marked by authors:** yes (DOC-DRIFT)
- **Severity:** medium

### F-09 — The Jev option routes triage data through the agentbox proxy to a cloud endpoint when `ALLOW_CLOUD=1`
- **Topics:** VC-09.16, VC-08.3, VC-05.8
- **Evidence:** "Compose defaults VISIONCLAW_JEV=1 and ALLOW_CLOUD=1" (VC-09.16). The endpoint is `agentbox:8094` via the shared Compose block. "Consumer in this area: the degraded-state triage on GET /api/canary/status" (VC-09.16). The broker inbox also sends proposal data for triage when `VISIONCLAW_JEV_PROPOSAL_TRIAGE=1` (VC-05.8).
- **Failure:** Health status and enrichment-proposal content are sent to an external endpoint. If the agentbox proxy is compromised or the endpoint is misrouted, proposal content (which may include proprietary knowledge) leaves the operator's control.
- **Confidence:** medium — the data flow is stated; the sensitivity of proposal content is inferred.
- **Marked by authors:** no
- **Severity:** medium

### F-10 — The `/ws/client-messages` stream drains from a shared receiver; if it is single-consumer, only one client receives messages at a time
- **Topics:** VC-04.25
- **Evidence:** "start_message_stream() — ctx.run_interval(100ms) drains app_state.client_message_rx" (VC-04.25). Each connected WebSocket actor calls this on the same receiver stored in AppState.
- **Failure:** If `client_message_rx` is an mpsc receiver (single consumer), messages are randomly delivered to whichever WebSocket actor's 100 ms tick fires first, starving other connected clients. If it is a broadcast channel, all clients see all messages — a cross-session data leak. The material does not state the channel type.
- **Confidence:** low — the channel semantics are not stated in the material; either failure mode is possible.
- **Marked by authors:** no
- **Severity:** high

### F-11 — The visibility filter code comment says "Default OFF" but the implementation defaults ON
- **Topics:** VC-03.9
- **Evidence:** "DRIFT: the comment at types.rs:255 says the filter is 'Default OFF' while filter_enabled defaults ON (visibility_filter.rs:26-35)" (VC-03.9).
- **Failure:** An operator reading the code comment assumes private nodes are exposed by default and sets `PUBKEY_VISIBILITY_FILTER` to enable it, when it is already on — a benign confusion. More dangerously, an operator who wants to temporarily disable the filter for debugging may set `PUBKEY_VISIBILITY_FILTER=0` and not realize the comment said the opposite, leading them to doubt which state is active.
- **Confidence:** high
- **Marked by authors:** yes (DRIFT)
- **Severity:** low

### F-12 — DiscoveryStats declares `total_discoveries`, `total_agents_discovered`, `servers_online`, `servers_offline` that the poll loop never writes
- **Topics:** VC-05.13
- **Evidence:** "DEBT: DiscoveryStats fields total_discoveries, total_agents_discovered, servers_online and servers_offline are declared (multi_mcp_agent_discovery.rs:91, :94, :97, :98) but never written by the poll loop... and the stored fields stay dead" (VC-05.13).
- **Failure:** A monitoring dashboard reading these fields always sees zero/stale values, masking real discovery health. The values shown are derived in the frame builder, not from the stored state, so any consumer reading the struct directly gets wrong data.
- **Confidence:** high
- **Marked by authors:** yes (DEBT)
- **Severity:** low
