### F-13 — The speech socket allows 30 seconds of unauthenticated resource holding before the deadline fires
- **Topics:** VC-05.16
- **Evidence:** "ADR-2075 — AUTH_DEADLINE = 30s (:22, doc :19-21). A socket still carrying pubkey:None at the deadline is told 'authentication deadline exceeded' and stopped (:487-500), so an unauthenticated peer cannot hold a broadcast subscription open" (VC-05.16). "this upgrade no longer authenticates. ADR-2075 moved the check AFTER the upgrade because browsers cannot set WebSocket headers" (VC-05.16).
- **Failure:** An attacker opens many `/ws/speech` connections without authenticating, each holding a WebSocket, an actor, and a `run_later` timer for 30 seconds before being stopped. This is a resource-exhaustion vector — a slow-loris style denial of service against the WebSocket actor pool.
- **Confidence:** medium — the 30 s window is stated; the exploitability depends on actor-pool limits not shown in the material.
- **Marked by authors:** no
- **Severity:** medium

### F-14 — The enrichment `effective_tier` gate reads a forwarded tier the scope comment says has not landed
- **Topics:** VC-05.7
- **Evidence:** "DRIFT: the scope comment at enrichment_proposals_handler.rs:308-314 still says effective_tier has not landed while the request field and gate_tier at enrichment_proposals_handler.rs:70 and :382 read it" (VC-05.7).
- **Failure:** An agent that forwards a lower `effective_tier` than the case's recorded tier gets the stricter of the two — safe. But an agent that forwards a higher tier (e.g. "critical") when the case is "low" forces a 20-character rationale minimum on a case the operator classified as low, blocking quick triage decisions. The code accepts any forwarded tier value from an external bridge without validating it against the case's actual classification.
- **Confidence:** medium — the gate behaviour (stricter wins) is stated; the abuse scenario of a bridge sending an inflated tier is inferred.
- **Marked by authors:** yes (DRIFT)
- **Severity:** medium

### F-15 — Four settings categories (constraints, quality-gates, visual, profiles) never broadcast changes to other sessions
- **Topics:** VC-06.4
- **Evidence:** "Still silent by choice: constraints, quality-gates, visual, profiles" (VC-06.4).
- **Failure:** Two operators viewing the same graph have divergent constraint and quality-gate settings. Operator A tightens a quality gate; operator B's client does not learn of it and continues displaying nodes that A's gate has filtered. They make conflicting decisions based on different views of the same data.
- **Confidence:** high
- **Marked by authors:** no
- **Severity:** low

## visionclaw#2 / critical

### F-01 — Deployed compose grants Editor role to anonymous users
- Topics: VC-22.9
- Evidence: The code is fail-closed — `public_reads_enabled()` ends `.unwrap_or(false)` (rbac_gate.rs:135-141) and `main.rs:809-818` refuses to start owner-less unless `RBAC_ALLOW_OWNERLESS` is set. But the shipped `docker-compose.unified.yml:132, :133` uses `:-1` defaults that invert both, so "an unassigned pubkey resolves to Editor (role_store.rs:359)." The material states this is "ADR-2027's deliberate demo default and stays."
- Failure: Anyone connecting to the production deployment without authentication obtains write access. The compose file shipped in the repository is the one most operators will use.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (CORRECTED ADR-2070 note acknowledges the inversion, and calls it deliberate)

### F-02 — Deleted Pod memory leaves a searchable embedding in RuVector
- Topics: VC-22.6
- Evidence: `deleteAgentMemory` (agentMemory.ts:191) deletes only the Pod resource. "no reverse tombstone to RuVector - embedding row persists and stays searchable (docs/DATA-authority-erasure.md:88)." The material also notes a second read consumer was added (Cargo.toml:143-145), so the orphaned embedding now has two readers.
- Failure: A user who exercises their erasure right (GDPR or otherwise) has the vector representation of that data remain queryable by both MCP writes and the new direct read client. No delete-propagation hook exists.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE marker)

### F-03 — Full-sync rebuild silently destroys governed API-added classes
- Topics: VC-22.10
- Evidence: `rebuild_assert_graph` does `CLEAR GRAPH assert then INSERT DATA` (github_sync_service.rs:1026). The material notes "this CLEAR wipes the ENTIRE assert graph including runtime OWL classes/axioms added via the governed write door (github_sync_service.rs:952-954)" and "a class added via add_owl_class/add_axiom between full-syncs is NOT itself in GRAPH_PROVENANCE-protected history."
- Failure: An operator adds a class through the governed API; it works. Then `FORCE_FULL_SYNC=1` runs and the class is gone with no warning and no recovery other than re-adding it through the corpus markdown.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no (the fact is stated in the sequence diagram but the data-loss consequence is not marked as Debt/Drift/Open)

### F-04 — Enrichment decisions can commit in SQLite but fail to write to the knowledge graph, with no retry
- Topics: VC-22.8, VC-24.3
- Evidence: "no cross-store 2PC - the durable decision commits in SQLite first (writeback_triggered=true) - the Oxigraph write is best-effort, writeback_committed flips only on Ok (enrichment_proposals_handler.rs:576, :589)." No retry mechanism is shown for a `writeback_triggered=true, writeback_committed=false` state.
- Failure: A broker approves an enrichment; the decision is permanently recorded as approved in SQLite; the Oxigraph INSERT fails (transient network, lock contention); the approved fact never enters the knowledge graph and nothing ever retries it.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no (the mechanism is described but the permanent-inconsistency consequence is not flagged)

### F-05 — First-contact bridge decisions bypass the rationale gate
- Topics: VC-24.3
- Evidence: "OPEN: a case with no row yet carries no recorded tier, so a first-contact bridge decision that forwards no effective_tier is still ungated (handlers/enrichment_proposals_handler.rs:502-508)." The tier used by `gate_tier` is read from the case row at :494, which does not exist yet.
- Failure: An agentbox bridge POSTs a decision for a case the store has never seen, with `outcome: approve` and no rationale. The gate sees no recorded tier, does not refuse, and the unattributed or attributed approval proceeds.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (OPEN)

### F-06 — Wire node-id overflow aliases one node's data onto another
- Topics: VC-32.6, VC-23.7
- Evidence: "release does NOT truncate silently. remap_wire_id masks the id into 26 bits and an error! log names the class and both ids (:194-204, ADR-2024). The masked id still aliases another node, so an over-range id is loud, not harmless" (IDENTIFIER-taxonomy.md:132-142).
- Failure: When the system exceeds 67,108,863 nodes (the 26-bit mask), node 67108864 and node 0 share the same wire id. Position updates, analytics scores and drag operations for one node overwrite the other. The error log fires but the data corruption happens regardless.
- Confidence: medium
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (INVARIANT marker acknowledges the aliasing, but understates it as "loud, not harmless")

### F-07 — ElevationActor cases are lost between publish and persist on restart
- Topics: VC-24.5
- Evidence: "STILL OPEN on this path: the ElevationActor poll shown here persists set_status but has no boot reconciliation, so a case lost between publish and persist is not reloaded on restart. ADR-2101 adds that to DecisionElevationActor only" (elevation_actor.rs:1644).
- Failure: A concept-elevation case reaches its terminal state (merged/abandoned); the kind-31404 receipt publishes to the forum; the process crashes before `repo.set_status` lands; on restart the case is gone from memory with no reload, and the enrichment proposal stays in `approved` forever with a forum receipt that says otherwise.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (STILL OPEN)

### F-08 — Feature-flag update is wholesale replace, silently resetting omitted flags
- Topics: VC-18.2
- Evidence: "WHOLESALE REPLACE - no field merge, a partial POST silently resets omitted flags to the body's values" (feature_flags_handlers.rs:28-29). Also "FEATURE_FLAGS remains process-global, unpersisted and shared across all users."
- Failure: A power user POSTs `{"sssp_integration": true}` to enable SSSP display. The server receives only that field; `*flags = request.into_inner()` replaces the entire struct, resetting `ontology_validation` to its default. Ontology validation is silently disabled for every user until someone notices.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (marked in the diagram and as OPEN ADR-2059)

### F-09 — Web client drops the oldest binary frame on queue overflow, losing position chunks
- Topics: VC-32.12, VC-32.3
- Evidence: "DEBT. the web queue is bounded at 256 frames and drops the OLDEST on overflow (binaryFrameDispatcher.ts:28,72-76), so 'clients drop none' in ADR-2141 decision 6 is not literally true. The dropped chunk is unacknowledged and the server rebuilds it after the timeout."
- Failure: During heavy physics activity with a slow client (background browser tab), the 256-frame queue fills; the oldest unacknowledged position chunks are dropped; the server has to rebuild them after each 500ms timeout, degrading the stream for all clients sharing the coordinator's pump cycle.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DEBT)

### F-10 — DAG ranker silently stops ranking edges if subClassOf provenance is absent
- Topics: VC-16.8
- Evidence: "Open: the fix moves a dependency onto provenance. A producer that writes a hierarchical subclass edge without rdfs:subClassOf in owl_property_iri, or a store path that drops vc:owlProperty (src/adapters/oxigraph_graph_repository.rs:551), would silently stop ranking. The ADR names this as its review trigger; nothing at boot detects it."
- Failure: A future ingest change or an Oxigraph upgrade drops or fails to persist `vc:owlProperty`. Every class hierarchy in the visualisation collapses to a flat layout with no error, no warning and no boot-time check.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Open)

### F-11 — GPU SSSP always sets parent to -1, so no path can ever be reconstructed
- Topics: VC-15.6, VC-15.9
- Evidence: "publish distances to node_sssp map, parent=-1 always (no predecessor array) :309-329" (shortest_path_actor.rs). In contrast, the CPU path in VC-15.7 "reconstruct[s] predecessor chain into path_map :825-841."
- Failure: The `/analytics/pathfinding/path` endpoint dispatches `algorithm: Sssp` to the GPU actor, which returns distances but no paths. Any consumer that needs "how do I get from A to B" rather than "how far is B from A" gets nothing, while the parallel CPU SSSP endpoint that does return paths sits on a separate route that nobody has wired to this dispatch.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no (the parent=-1 fact is stated in the diagram but the functional consequence is not marked)

### F-12 — Connected components analysis hard-fails when the GPU is unavailable
- Topics: VC-15.8
- Evidence: "RESOLVED ADR-2054 - the CPU fallback... always silently returned every node as its own singleton component. Both the fallback fn and UpdateComponentEdges/cached_edges are REMOVED (connected_components_actor.rs:212-224) - a GPU Err now propagates as a hard error instead of a fabricated result."
- Failure: The GPU becomes unavailable (driver update, OOM, CUDA error). Every call to `/analytics/pathfinding/connected-components` returns 500. There is no degradation path and no way to get this analysis without the GPU.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no (presented as a RESOLUTION; the operational consequence of no fallback is not flagged)

### F-13 — Three independent ACSP relay connections share one bridge secret under three identities
- Topics: VC-24.11
- Evidence: "this is the SAME AcspClient type ElevationActor and DecisionElevationActor each construct independently at their own startup (elevation_actor.rs:1056, decision_elevation_actor.rs:521) - three separate relay connections under three different panel identities can exist concurrently, all using the same bridge secret."
- Failure: If any one of the three connections' keys leaks (through logs, a debug dump, or a relay compromise), all three governance channels are compromised. Additionally, three panel identities publishing with the same key creates attribution ambiguity on the relay.
- Confidence: medium
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (noted in the sequence diagram, no specific marker but clearly flagged as a concern)

### F-14 — Client settings changes are silently lost after 3 retry attempts
- Topics: VC-30.4
- Evidence: `settingsRetryManager` retries 3 times with exponential backoff (settingsRetryManager.ts:23-25), then "dispatches window CustomEvent settings-retry-failed on maxRetries exhausted settingsRetryManager.ts:123-125." No consumer of this CustomEvent is shown anywhere in the material.
- Failure: A user changes critical physics parameters; the server is briefly down or returns 5xx; after 3 retries over ~15 seconds the change is dropped from the retry queue; the `settings-retry-failed` event fires into the void; the user sees their change in the UI (which is local state) but it never persists to the server. On reload the old settings return.
- Confidence: medium
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no (the mechanism is described but the loss scenario is not marked)

### F-15 — Whelk reasoning has no timeout or axiom-count cap
- Topics: VC-20.3
- Evidence: "DOC-DRIFT - module doc claims complete EL reasoning (whelk_inference_engine.rs:5) but infer() lines 412-467 has no tokio timeout or axiom-count cap."
- Failure: A pathological ontology (many classes, deeply nested axioms) causes Whelk to run unboundedly on the post-sync reasoning path. The sync task blocks, no subsequent syncs run, the post-Sync materialisation and class-index refresh never fire, and the server appears healthy while the ontology pipeline is silently stalled.
- Confidence: medium
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DOC-DRIFT)

---

## Not judgeable from this material

1. **Actual production configuration**: Whether the shipped `docker-compose.unified.yml` is what runs in production, or whether a hardened overlay exists. F-01's severity depends entirely on this.

2. **Concurrency safety of the write-master stores**: Whether concurrent access to `enrichment.sqlite3` from `ElevationActor`, `DecisionElevationActor` and the HTTP handlers is serialized correctly (WAL mode alone does not prevent all race conditions at the application level).

3. **GPU error rates and recovery**: The material describes GPU paths failing as error branches but gives no data on how frequently this occurs or whether the GPU context recovers automatically after a transient error.

4. **The `settings-retry-failed` event consumer**: Whether a listener exists somewhere in the client that the material does not cover (a toast, a banner, a store update), or whether it is truly unconsumed.

5. **Backup and restore drill results**: Whether `scripts/backup-sqlite.sh` has been run against the actual production databases and whether the Oxigraph snapshot gap (PROPOSED ADR-2103) has been addressed in practice.

## visionclaw#2 / premortem

# Post-mortem — confidential-content exposure, failed erasure, and unauthorized mutation access

**Scope and method.** This post-mortem is reconstructed entirely from the estate diagrams at the pinned commits (`visionclaw 8692b12b…`, `agentbox 6500582d…`). Every claim below carries the citation the material itself gives; where I reason past what the diagrams state, the finding says so. The incident composite: a client's private corpus content surfaced in the governed graph, a deletion request for agent memory was demonstrably not honored, and accounts that were never granted roles held mutation access. The three root causes below each stand alone.

---

### R-1 — Shipped compose inverts the fail-closed RBAC posture: anonymous public reads and unassigned users become Editors
- Topics: VC-22.9, VC-18.1, VC-20.7
- Chain of events:
  1. Deployment uses the shipped `docker-compose.unified.yml`. It sets `RBAC_PUBLIC_READS` on (`docker-compose.unified.yml:132`) and `RBAC_ALLOW_OWNERLESS` with a `:-1` default (`docker-compose.unified.yml:133`), inverting a code base that is fail-closed by design.
  2. Boot proceeds owner-less (`src/main.rs:797`, `:802`), so no Owner assignment exists and only `POWER_USER_PUBKEYS` would map to Admin.
  3. Anonymous requests to safe methods on gated scopes resolve to `None` required level and pass through (`src/middleware/rbac_gate.rs:172-175`, passthrough at `:264-269`) — anonymous clients read `/api/graph/data`, all public analytics telemetry (VC-18.1 GET table), and all ontology GETs (VC-20.7).
  4. Any authenticated nostr pubkey with no explicit role assignment falls to `default_role`, which is **Editor** unless `RBAC_DEFAULT_ROLE=viewer` (`src/services/role_store.rs:366`, reached from `:359`) — mutation-class routes gated below PowerUser accept these users.
- Evidence: the CORRECTED rect in VC-22.9 states verbatim that "the shipped compose inverts both (`docker-compose.unified.yml:132, :133, with the :-1 default), so an unassigned pubkey resolves to Editor (`role_store.rs:359`)"; code-side fail-closure at `rbac_gate.rs:135-141` (`.unwrap_or(false)`) and `main.rs:809-818` (refuses owner-less boot without the flag); ADR-2027 records the open posture as a deliberate demo default; `GET /api/admin/rbac/whoami` is readable by any signed-in user (`rbac_gate.rs:160-166`).
- Warning signs visible today: the VC-22.9 diagram itself, raised by estate ADR-2087 and corrected under ADR-2070 — the exact inversion was documented before any incident, and left in place as ADR-2027's demo default.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high

### R-2 — Ingest gate divergence publishes author-private content: `working/` is ingested by default and a valid `owl-class` overrides an explicit `public: false`
- Topics: VC-21.1, VC-21.2, VC-21.3, VC-21.12, VC-22.1
- Chain of events:
  1. The default corpus source is local (`corpus_source_kind`, `corpus_source/mod.rs:193`), and `LocalDirectorySource` default base paths are `knowledge,working` (`src/services/corpus_source/local.rs:21`).
  2. An author follows the governing V4 doc, which promises `working/` is never ingested (`docs/VAULT-corpus-format.md:244`), and therefore treats frontmatter flags there as moot — a `working/` page carrying `public: true` (or an `owl-class`) passes `page_is_kg_included` (`src/services/github_sync_service.rs:2114`).
  3. Separately, on `knowledge/`, the gate is the disjunction `self.public || self.owl_class.is_some()` (`crates/visionclaw-domain/src/vault/mod.rs:162-163`), so a page with an explicit `public: false` plus a valid class marker is included anyway — the VC-21.12 divergence note records this as policy.
  4. The working page is kept as its own node unless a knowledge page owns the same id (`github_sync_service.rs:2156`); a plain page node is created with `metadata public=true` (VC-21.3, `KnowledgeGraphParser::create_page_node`).
  5. The pubkey visibility filter drops only nodes whose metadata is private (`visibility_filter.rs:93`, `PUBKEY_VISIBILITY_FILTER` default on at `:26`); a `public=true` node is served to every reader of `/api/graph/data` — anonymously under R-1.
- Evidence: both Drift blocks in VC-21.2 — docs V4 say "no `owl-class` bypass and no OR" (`docs/VAULT-corpus-format.md:236-238`) versus the OR gate at `vault/mod.rs:162-163`, and `:244` versus `local.rs:21` + `github_sync_service.rs:2156`; VC-21.12's divergence note ("an explicit public false plus a class marker remains included by policy… a valid owl-class overrides public false").
- Warning signs visible today: two explicit Drift paragraphs in VC-21.2 and one DIVERGENCE note in VC-21.12, all unresolved; authors relying on the V4 doc have no code-side guarantee matching it. Note the exposure of the `public:false + owl-class` variant additionally depends on the ontology-node branch's public metadata, which the material does not state — that sub-step is inferred.
- Confidence: high (mechanics fully cited; the `working/` + `public:true` exposure path is directly implied, the explicit-false variant partly inferred)
- Severity: high

### R-3 — Erasure is not propagated: `deleteAgentMemory` leaves the RuVector embedding row searchable, and the server now reads it directly
- Topics: VC-22.6, VC-22.1
- Chain of events:
  1. Client requests deletion of agent memory; operator deletes via the Pod UI — `deleteAgentMemory` issues only a Pod LDP `DELETE` on the `.jsonld` resource (`client/src/services/solidPod/agentMemory.ts:191-198`, `ldpClient.ts:203-212`).
  2. There is no reverse tombstone: the path never calls `mcp__claude-flow__memory_delete` or any RuVector endpoint (`docs/DATA-authority-erasure.md:88`); the embedding row in the external Postgres persists and stays searchable.
  3. The drift note records that the server now also holds a direct read-only RuVector client (tokio-postgres from `RUVECTOR_PG_CONNINFO`, crate `visionclaw-memory-cloud`, `Cargo.toml:143-145`), so the surviving row has a second, server-side consumer.
  4. Client audit (or the operator's own semantic search) retrieves the supposedly deleted content; no durable erasure record exists to prove or retry partial erasure (ADR-2102 is proposed only, `docs/DATA-authority-erasure.md` "one durable erasure record, five store acknowledgements" is not implemented).
- Evidence: the DIVERGENCE rect in VC-22.6 ("embedding row persists and stays searchable", "no delete-propagation hook exists in this path"); the read-side drift note (`Cargo.toml:143-145`); ADR-2102/ADR-2103 both remain proposed.
- Warning signs visible today: the tombstone gap is a standing, documented divergence in the data-authority matrix; no deletion path anywhere in VC-22 touches RuVector.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high

---

### F-01 — `FORCE_FULL_SYNC` wipes the entire assert graph, including classes added through the governed write door, and provenance does not protect them
- Topics: VC-22.10
- Evidence: "this CLEAR wipes the ENTIRE assert graph including runtime OWL classes/axioms added via the governed write door (`github_sync_service.rs:952-954`)"; the note over DIRECT/AG adds such a class "is NOT itself in GRAPH_PROVENANCE-protected history for the assert graph — only re-derivation from the corpus… restores it after the next rebuild."
- Failure: an operator runs a routine full sync; every ontology class/axiom committed since the last sync through `add_owl_class`/`add_axiom` is silently destroyed with no recovery path other than re-authoring.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: no (plain note, not a Tension/Debt/Drift/Open marker)
- Severity: high

### F-02 — Oxigraph has no point-in-time backup; `GRAPH_PROVENANCE` is not re-derivable
- Topics: VC-22.7, VC-22.11, VC-22.1
- Evidence: `REQUIRED_DBS` covers only the SQLite set (`scripts/backup-sqlite.sh:75-76`); the VC-22.11 note states "Oxigraph still has NO point-in-time backup — that bullet stands"; ADR-2103 (snapshot, RPO/RTO) is proposed only; VC-22.7 records `GRAPH_PROVENANCE` as "restore-only because it cannot be re-derived".
- Failure: Oxigraph store corruption or host loss destroys the append-only provenance graph and the signed PROV-O attribution permanently; the knowledge graph can be rebuilt from the corpus, the provenance cannot.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (residual open bullet in the VC-22.11 drift note; PROPOSED ADR-2103 in VC-22.7)
- Severity: high

### F-03 — A throttled chunk is acknowledged without being applied, and the server then treats those nodes as current forever
- Topics: VC-30.10, VC-32.8, VC-32.12
- Evidence: the TENSION note in VC-30.10 — `handleBinaryFrame` returns without applying when under 16 ms have elapsed (`wsClient.ts:26-27`), `updateNodePositions` then resolves and the chunk is acknowledged (`binaryProtocol.ts:393`, `:407`), "while ADR-2141 decision 5 acknowledges a chunk only after it has been applied"; server-side `acknowledge(seq)` makes the chunk's nodes held state that is "never moving it backwards" (`src/actors/position_stream.rs:225`).
- Failure: under a frame burst, a chunk carrying the only update for a set of nodes is dropped client-side yet acknowledged; the server never re-sends those nodes until they move beyond `streamMoveThreshold` or their analytics change — permanent silent divergence (wrong positions/analytics on screen, believed current).
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension)
- Severity: high

### F-04 — The ADR-2110 rationale gate is bypassable for first-contact cases with no stored tier, and unknown cases are silently stubbed into existence
- Topics: VC-24.3, VC-24.2
- Evidence: the OPEN note — "a case with no row yet carries no recorded tier, so a first-contact bridge decision that forwards no effective_tier is still ungated" (`handlers/enrichment_proposals_handler.rs:502-508`); the stub fallback creates a `pending` row for a case the store has never seen (`:537`); attribution (`approvedBy` did/hex) is parsed from the request body, not verified against the signer.
- Failure: a compromised or misconfigured bridge POSTs `approve` with an attributed DID on a fabricated case id; no rationale is required (no recorded tier), a stub row is created, and `append_derived_summary` lands triples in `GRAPH_ONTOLOGY_SUMMARY` (`:576`) attributed to a DID the caller chose.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Open)
- Severity: high

### F-05 — Insecure-defaults builds remove authentication on server-to-server edges entirely
- Topics: VC-28.5, VC-27.13, VC-27.7
- Evidence: image-gen agent path — "dev or dev-auth builds bypass to always-true" for `X-Agent-Key` (`src/handlers/image_gen_handler.rs:60-63`); agent-events ingest accepts unauthenticated frames when `ALLOW_INSECURE_DEFAULTS` is set in "debug/dev-auth builds only" (`src/agent_events/ingest.rs:276-290`).
- Failure: a dev-auth feature build reaches production (image tag mistake, emergency deploy); `/image-gen/agent-submit` and `/wss/agent-events` accept anyone, and combined with F-04's ungated tier the decide path is one leaked route away from unauthenticated KG writes.
- Confidence: medium (bypass behavior stated; the production deployment of such a build is inferred)
- Marked by authors: no
- Severity: high

### F-06 — NIP-98 `expected_url` is reconstructed from `X-Forwarded-*` request headers
- Topics: VC-26.4, VC-32.1
- Evidence: "reconstruct expected_url from X-Forwarded-Proto/Host/URI (`nip98.rs:196-218`)" (VC-26.4); VC-32.1's ER-25 note only guarantees the WebSocket and REST paths use the *same* reconstruction function, not that the headers are trusted.
- Failure: if the actix edge reads client-supplied `X-Forwarded-Host` (i.e., is not strictly behind a header-sanitizing proxy), an attacker mints a kind-27235 token whose `u` tag names their own host, sets the matching forwarded header, and the URL-match gate passes — full NIP-98 bypass on `/solid/*` and REST.
- Confidence: low (inferred — the material does not state proxy topology or header sanitization)
- Marked by authors: no
- Severity: high

### F-07 — The NIP-98 replay cache is a process-local `Mutex<HashMap>`
- Topics: VC-26.4
- Evidence: `REPLAY_CACHE` at `src/utils/nip98.rs:215` is a single-process in-memory map with a 2×-window TTL (`:178`); tokens are valid for `TOKEN_MAX_AGE_SECONDS` 60 (`:169`, `:362`).
- Failure: a restart, or more than one replica behind a load balancer, clears/partitions the claimed-event set; a captured token is replayable within its 60-second window and counts as a fresh authentication.
- Confidence: medium (inferred for the restart/replica scenario; process-locality is stated)
- Marked by authors: no
- Severity: medium

### F-08 — Feature-flag update is a wholesale replace of process-global, unpersisted state
- Topics: VC-18.2, VC-18.9
- Evidence: `*flags = request.into_inner()` with "no field merge, a partial POST silently resets omitted flags" (`feature_flags_handlers.rs:28-29`); `ontology_validation` is "the ONLY flag read as a real GATE" (`api_handler/ontology/mod.rs:332`); OPEN ADR-2059 records the store as "process-global, unpersisted and shared across all users".
- Failure: an operator (or script) POSTs only the flag they mean to flip; `ontology_validation` silently reverts to the body's default, the real validation gate opens, and invalid ontology mutations are accepted until the next restart re-randomizes nothing — the change is also invisible after reboot since nothing persists.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Open — ADR-2059)
- Severity: medium

### F-09 — Live configuration ships private proposal text to a third-party cloud endpoint
- Topics: VC-24.13, VC-28.9
- Evidence: Compose sets `VISIONCLAW_JEV=1` (`docker-compose.unified.yml:21`) and `ALLOW_CLOUD=1` (`:22`), routing "the private proposal text via the proxy to the selected backend, typesafe (`agentbox.toml:892`)" with `BACKEND_LOCAL=0` (`docker-compose.unified.yml:7-11`) to `api.typesafe.ai` (`docs/reference/configuration.md:417-427`, `crates/visionclaw-jev/src/config.rs:303-305`); the agentbox proxy holds the vendor key (`agentbox.toml:893-895`).
- Failure: confidential client decision material is transmitted to an external vendor as a side effect of the broker reading its inbox; a client with data-residency or confidentiality terms discovers their proposal content in a third-party LLM pipeline. The material presents this as a decided RESOLVED state, not a risk.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: no (recorded as a RESOLVED operator decision, ADR-2148)
- Severity: high

### F-10 — On-demand OpenAI STT edge posts user voice audio to `api.openai.com`
- Topics: VC-28.7, VC-28.9
- Evidence: the DRIFT note — `SetSTTProvider(OpenAI)` is live and `ProcessAudioChunk` "POSTs the audio to `https://api.openai.com/v1/audio/transcriptions` when `settings.openai` holds an API key" (`src/services/speech_service.rs:689-699`, default stays Whisper at `:75`).
- Failure: a runtime provider switch (the message is live) sends user speech — potentially identifying content — to a third party with no per-user consent gate on that path.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift)
- Severity: medium

### F-11 — Ingest provenance trusts asserted pubkeys; no signature is verified
- Topics: VC-27.13, VC-22.5
- Evidence: "SECURITY: ProvenanceStatus::Attributed means a well-formed pubkey was asserted, NOT that a signature was verified — the wire carries no sig field (`agent_events/provenance.rs:29-38`)"; KPI rows persist the declared intent verbatim as an invariant (`migrations/sqlite/0006…:1`).
- Failure: any party that can reach the authenticated ingest socket (or a compromised agentbox) fabricates attribution and declared intent in durable KPI/provenance records; Trust Variance, HITL Precision and the trace read path then report forged human judgement as real.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: no (flagged inline as "SECURITY:" but not under the four drift markers)
- Severity: medium

### F-12 — Two node-IRI mint paths: knowledge-graph nodes are minted as opaque `urn:ngm` inline, not the owner-scoped typed grammar
- Topics: VC-23.2, VC-23.3, VC-23.8
- Evidence: "oxigraph_graph_repository.rs:88,97 define and call local node_iri/edge_iri format! helpers minting urn:ngm:node / urn:ngm:edge inline instead of the typed uri::ngm::node_iri / edge_iri (`src/uri/mod.rs:355,370`)" — recorded as a still-open divergence; `urn:ngm` is accepted only by `parse_dual` (`src/uri/mod.rs:634-641`).
- Failure: knowledge-graph identities carry no owner-scoping or content address, so any custody/erasure/ownership logic keyed on `urn:visionclaw:kg:PUBKEY:…` silently misses these nodes; strict-parse surfaces reject them.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Divergence, still open)
- Severity: medium

### F-13 — ElevationActor has no boot reconciliation, and a resumed approval can open a duplicate PR
- Topics: VC-24.4, VC-24.5, VC-24.6
- Evidence: "STILL OPEN on this path: the ElevationActor poll… has no boot reconciliation, so a case lost between publish and persist is not reloaded on restart" (VC-24.5); ADR-2101's recorded follow-on 1 — "a resumed approval can open a duplicate PR" (VC-24.6).
- Failure: a crash between the 31404 publish and `set_status` leaves a terminal case permanently mid-flight in memory; on resume, a second PR for the same decision page is opened, double-writing governed content to the repository.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Open / recorded follow-on in ADR-2101)
- Severity: medium

### F-14 — The broker inbox is capped at 500 newest-updated rows, so older pending cases become invisible and die at TTL
- Topics: VC-24.13, VC-24.1
- Evidence: DEBT — "total is proposals.len() after store::all, which is capped at ALL_LIMIT 500 rows newest-updated first, so total is not the table size once more than 500 proposals exist" (`handlers/enrichment_proposals_handler.rs:738-745`, `broker_inbox_handler.rs:194`); cases past `OPEN_CASE_TTL` of 14 days are closed with `elevation_expired` (`elevation_actor.rs:68, :1347`).
- Failure: under case volume, pending cases fall off the broker's view, no human is ever asked, and they are silently expired with a terminal durable status — governance decisions lost without any error surfaced.
- Confidence: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt)
- Severity: medium

### F-15 — The agent-event hub drops oldest under backpressure, silently starving hub consumers
- Topics: VC-27.13, VC-25.3
- Evidence: `hub::publish` over a broadcast channel "drops oldest under backpressure" with `HUB_CAPACITY=256` (`src/agent_events/hub.rs:22`, `:32-34`); the KPI tap subscribes to this hub (`src/services/kpi_compute.rs:630`, `:650`).
- Failure: an agentbox burst overflows the hub; the KPI tap misses trajectories with no error anywhere, so `augmentation_ratio`, `trust_variance` and HITL tiles are computed from incomplete data and reported with full confidence.
- Confidence: high (mechanism stated; metric impact follows from the documented tap)
- Marked by authors: no
- Severity: medium

## visionclaw#3 / critical

### F-01 — Single-use payment authentication is process-local: restart or a second replica allows replay, and a full cache rejects all auth
- Topics: VC-55.1 (also VC-33.2, which shows the same cache at `src/utils/nip98.rs` backs every NIP-98 route)
- Evidence: The token is "claim[ed] … under mutex, bounded process-local cache" (`src/utils/nip98.rs:227`, `:295`) and "replay or capacity" both route to REJECT (`src/handlers/pay_handler.rs:418`). The topic states plainly: "One signed request cannot charge twice **within this process** … the cache does not span restarts or replicas", and closes with "Separate storage commits, permissive compatibility URL matching and fresh-token operation retries remain open." The fee is a "durable ledger debit" (`pay_handler.rs:303`) committed separately from the operation effect.
- Failure: A restart between debit and effect, or two replicas behind a load balancer, lets a captured signed request be replayed — double debit or repeated swap/pool operation. Separately, flooding distinct valid auths fills the bounded cache and every subsequent payment auth is rejected (denial of service). "Permissive compatibility URL matching" may additionally let a token signed for one URL verify on another.
- Confidence: high for payments (properties stated outright); medium, inferred, that the same restart/replica replay window applies to all NIP-98-protected routes (settings writes, broker decides) via the shared `src/utils/nip98.rs`.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes — the retry/URL/storage items are listed as "open" in the closing note; the restarts/replicas limit is asserted in prose rather than tagged.

### F-02 — Codescape cannot be secured at all: enabling auth refuses startup, presence signatures are unverified, and the container holds ten repositories
- Topics: VC-39.7, VC-43.1, VC-39.2
- Evidence: "the main port runs OpenAccess, reported honestly as auth open" (`codescape-server/src/auth.rs:120-124`, `routes/mod.rs:71`); the admission flow shows `require_auth? → true → Refuse startup` (`auth.rs:138`) — there is no configured-secure mode, the binary will not start. The presence shim "accepts the Schnorr signature unverified, the claimed DID is only a label" (`xr_presence.rs:20-23`, `:65-67`). The egress allowlist and no-`/vault` are "REJECTED as invariants … open until P2 ships an egress test" (ADR-2143:82, :144). The compose peer "maps the estate scene and ten member repositories read-only" into the service (`docker-compose.unified.yml:538-619`), and `/codescape/` is LAN-published in dev (`nginx.dev.conf:63-65`).
- Failure: Anyone who can reach the service reads the structure of ten repositories, speaks voice commands into it, and impersonates any DID in presence — and because auth cannot be enabled, the only mitigation is network isolation, which the register says is not an enforced boundary.
- Confidence: high (all stated); the current prod nginx path falls through to the SPA, so exposure today is the dev/LAN decision — but productionising Codescape as-is has no auth option.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Open R1/R2; Tension R3; "deliberately permits open dev-LAN pending P6").

### F-03 — The default dev environment boots with a full, peer-agnostic auth bypass armed
- Topics: VC-36.15
- Evidence: ADR-2108 flipped the compose default to `VISIONCLAW_DEV_MODE: "${VISIONCLAW_DEV_MODE:-1}"` (`docker-compose.unified.yml:116`): "launch.sh up dev boots an ARMED backend and the operator opts OUT". When active, the bypass is a "LAN-local FULL bypass, peer-agnostic: no NIP-98, no token, no peer check" (`src/utils/auth.rs:203-218`) granting `dev-mode-local-admin` to every request, with further bypass sites at `src/settings/auth_extractor.rs:25`, `src/handlers/memory_cloud_handler.rs:66`, `src/handlers/speech_socket_handler.rs:165`. The material's own caveat: "both services load env_file .env, so the var must never sit in a .env shared with prod — sharper now that the compose default supplies it unasked" (`docker-compose.unified.yml:113-115`).
- Failure: A dev or dev-auth build started with defaults on any network-reachable host hands full admin — graph writes, settings, drag/pin — to every peer with no credential; a `.env` shared between services silently propagates the arming. Release binaries refuse to boot with the var (exit 2, `project/src/main.rs:136`, `:161`), so the guard for everything short of release is operator discipline.
- Confidence: high (entirely stated).
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (ADR-2108 note and the diagram's own .env caveat).

### F-04 — The XR client's only credential is sent after upgrade, but release servers authenticate at upgrade — the immersive client cannot pass release auth
- Topics: VC-48.1 (and VC-36.3)
- Evidence: Server side: "Server requires Origin in release" (`http_handler.rs:67,129,131,139`), "Verify signed subprotocol or legacy bearer at upgrade" (`:146,151,181,183`), "Unauthenticated release upgrade refused" (`:314,321,322`). Client side: "Only after upgrade: request topology then send authenticate text" (`transport.rs:99,115,118`). The topic concludes: "A secret used for the post-upgrade text message cannot satisfy earlier release Origin and authentication checks", and marks the Tension "ADR-2161's Origin prerequisite also needs upgrade-time authentication alignment (`transport.rs:95,115`)". Related: the authenticate acknowledgement is never read, so even post-upgrade rejection is silent (VC-36.3, `transport.rs:117`).
- Failure: Point the XR client at a release-mode deployment and every `/wss` and `/ws/presence` connection is refused at upgrade — the immersive product is dead against production servers, creating pressure to weaken the upgrade gate (the actual auth boundary) to make it work.
- Confidence: high on the handshake mismatch (drawn from both sides); medium, inferred, that no intermediary proxy supplies upgrade-time credentials, since none appears in the material.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension, VC-48.1; Open on the unread ack in VC-36.3).

### F-05 — Shipped compose contradicts the fail-closed RBAC binary default: public reads on, any authenticated key is an Editor
- Topics: VC-33.6
- Evidence: "structural default RBAC_PUBLIC_READS=false `src/middleware/rbac_gate.rs:141` unwrap_or(false), but `docker-compose.unified.yml:132` sets RBAC_PUBLIC_READS=1 in the dev/unified compose service. An unassigned authenticated pubkey resolves to Editor via `UserRole::default_authenticated()` (`src/models/rbac.rs:68-70`, `src/services/role_store.rs:188,196-198`)". The authors mark the DIVERGENCE: "two different postures depending whether you read the binary default or the shipped compose env." The governing doc's citations to these env lines are also stale (BASELINE-architecture.md:273 vs compose:132/:133).
- Failure: Any environment built from the unified compose serves the whole graph to unauthenticated readers and grants write-level Editor to every authenticated Nostr key with no assignment, while an operator who read the Rust default believes the system is fail-closed.
- Confidence: high (stated, with line numbers on both sides).
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE plus DOC-DRIFT on the citations).

### F-06 — The Jev voice option's code promises local-only, but explicit endpoints return before the locality gate and the host compose grants cloud routing
- Topics: VC-42.2, VC-42.3, VC-39.7 (J1)
- Evidence: Resolution order: "Explicit endpoint configured? → Return resolved endpoint (`voice/jev.rs:192`)" comes before "Local assertion or cloud permission? (`:187,200`)". Drift: "The local-only promise in `voice/jev.rs:11` and the resolver contract at `:169` do not cover the explicit-endpoint return at `:190`." Compose sets `CODESCAPE_JEV=1` and grants the permission — "ALLOW_CLOUD=1 sends the utterance via the proxy to the selected backend (`agentbox.toml:892`)" (`docker-compose.unified.yml:504-508`). Threshold: "default threshold is 1.0 (`voice/jev.rs:182`)" with "Reject confidence below threshold" — so exactly 1.0 is accepted (`:409`); marked Tension: "Compose calls the enabled model advisory (`:507`) while the selection path can return an action at confidence one."
- Failure: Spoken utterances leave the host to a cloud model that can select a view command, while an auditor reading `jev.rs:11` is told processing is local-only; an operator who sets an explicit endpoint believing the locality gate still applies gets cloud routing with no permission check, and the uncalibrated inclusive-default threshold governs because compose leaves it empty.
- Confidence: high on gate order and the comment contradiction; medium, inferred, on exactly what transcript content is transmitted.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift and Tension).

### F-07 — An approved amendment can silently replace an "immutable" ontology resource: no after-apply identity check
- Topics: VC-41.4
- Evidence: The writer "checks current resource against proposal IRI (`write/apply.rs:229,230`)", then "Apply approved patch then parse new page (`:242,258`)", then persists — "DRIFT: fixture page now has replacement resource, no after-resource equality check (`:280,282`)". The topic states the corpus format's immutable-resource invariant "needs a before/after identity check, which the inspected amendment path lacks."
- Failure: A validly signed, human-approved amendment swaps the resource IRI of a supposedly immutable resource with no error anywhere; downstream consumers keep addressing the old IRI while the content now lives under a new one — corpus integrity broken silently through the legitimate governance path.
- Confidence: high (demonstrated by executing the real CLI writer on a real signed producer output).
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DRIFT).

### F-08 — Signed multi-page group proposals can never be applied: producer and consumer disagree on identity, and signing doesn't refuse
- Topics: VC-41.3
- Evidence: The producer's "Group subject is proposal IRI (`vault-core/src/proposal.rs:225,234`)" and "Postable group can be signed (`vault/src/main.rs:478,494`)"; the consumer "Require[s] note-page slug to answer to subject IRI (`ontology-apply.js:570,699`)" — "DRIFT: real grouped payload fails identity check before invoking writer (`:571,700`)", and "Further guards refuse grouped application (`:574,702`)". Open: "A supported all-or-nothing group apply/recovery contract, or explicit pre-signing refusal, is not established."
- Failure: The forum decides a multi-page change, the decision is signed and stored, and every attempt to execute it is refused before any write — approved governance output becomes permanently unexecutable, and nothing at signing time warns the proposer that they are creating an undeliverable decision.
- Confidence: high (both grouped fixtures fail pre-writer in the audit).
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DRIFT plus Open).

### F-09 — Review evidence inverts: the consequence count can include only version metadata while excluding the changed logical expression
- Topics: VC-41.2 (and the VC-41 business note)
- Evidence: The assessed graphs "include content-derived versionIRI (`build/turtle.rs:495,513`)", so the certificate probe can find "only versionIRI differs — one added and one lost triple (`rdfs-certificate/src/lib.rs:642`)", and "Blank-node exclusion can coexist with a non-conservative verdict caused solely by version metadata." Meanwhile "Consequences and generation [are] outside case digest (`vault-core/src/proposal.rs:260,364`)", and the business note states the review-facing result directly: "Its consequence count can include version metadata while excluding the changed logical expression."
- Failure: A reviewer judging a signed proposal sees a heavy consequence list generated by a regenerated version IRI (a no-op) and/or misses the consequence of an actually changed logical expression — semantically breaking amendments read as metadata noise and trivial ones read as conservative, so the human gate approves on inverted evidence while the case number stays stable across changing contexts.
- Confidence: high (the audit ran real signed CLI dry-runs through the real adapter).
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no — asserted in the topic's business note without any Tension/Debt/Drift/Open marker.

### F-10 — Two release paths disagree about ownership: the orchestrator frees a node the GPU registry still holds
- Topics: VC-52.2 (with VC-52.7)
- Evidence: On drag end the socket sends a "node-only Released notification (`position_updates.rs:259-266`)" and the "Orchestrator removes id without owner check (`physics_orchestrator_actor.rs:1189-1195`)", while the GPU path sends holder+node ids and the "Registry ignores a stale owner's release (`hold_registry.rs:68-70`)". Marked TENSION: "GPU may still hold a node after orchestrator clears its held set (`physics_orchestrator_actor.rs:1194`)". The expiry backstop "does not reject closed holders" and only runs within compute handling (`force_compute_actor.rs:1900-1913`). VC-52.7 adds that the socket's `dragged_nodes` duplicates the registry's per-holder set (`types.rs:73` vs `hold_registry.rs:83`).
- Failure: Two users on one node: a stale holder's late release frees the orchestrator's held set (auto-pause/wake resumes physics under the other user's hand) while the GPU registry correctly keeps the pin — the two authorities diverge on the same node, producing anything from a node stuck anchored after release to physics yanking a node someone is holding.
- Confidence: medium — source-confirmed plus a registry/model probe, but the authors note it is "not a fresh actor integration run".
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension; a known ADR gap).

### F-11 — The drag-physics acceptance gate passes a negative control: a node left pinned and silent satisfies all five checks
- Topics: VC-52.4
- Evidence: The release check subtracts the "dragger position at release" from "the observer's latest position" (`drag-physics-acceptance.mjs:359-364`) and "Positive difference passes" (`:386-388`); "No motion after release returns release time (`:230`)" and "Zero settle milliseconds passes rest check (`:353-355,381-384`)". Marked TENSION: "neither check requires a fresh post-release position observation (`:355,388`)". The executed negative control — node pinned at 150, zero post-release positions — passes all five checks, with the reported travel coming from different clients' earlier samples.
- Failure: A regression that pins nodes forever or stops position delivery entirely ships, because the acceptance script that gates drag behaviour cannot reject it; every drag/settle claim certified by this script is currently unverified.
- Confidence: high (negative control actually executed against a deliberately wrong peer).
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension).

### F-12 — The voice/external estate cannot be rebuilt or audited from the repository: dangling runtime dependencies and a dirty untracked checkout
- Topics: VC-35.12, VC-35.6
- Evidence: "Kokoros, Whisper-WebUI and xinference are UNTRACKED symlinks … All three dangle in this container. git ls-files returns nothing for any of them", yet "xinference … has live compose consumers (`docker-compose.unified.yml:160`, `:405`) … a runtime endpoint dependency." "Whisper-WebUI is a BROKEN SYMLINK … so the STT container source is not checked out here. Only the client-side URL contract … is verifiable" (VC-35.6). And "voice-stack/unmute is a NESTED checkout that the VisionClaw repo does not track … its working tree is dirty: read the declared revision, never the files on disk."
- Failure: STT and the LLM endpoint depend on artefacts absent from the repo: no rebuild, no pin, no audit. A host migration or container rebuild silently changes what transcribes speech or answers commands with no diff to review, and during a voice incident there is no source to inspect or roll back to.
- Confidence: high (stated, with the symlinks inspected).
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DIVERGENCE in VC-35.6; DEBT/UNPIN notes in VC-35.12).

### F-13 — Concurrent-backend code is live in the scene while its decision record is inactive, says the code does not exist, and headset acceptance is unmet
- Topics: VC-44.3 (and VC-44.1)
- Evidence: "Drift: ADR-2159's verification says no BackendConnection exists; current construction is wired into the scene (`graph_scene.gd:1282`). Its inactive status is not overturned." The transport evidence is "Rust loopback preflight" where the "Test thread substitutes for Godot pump, no engine or render-store load measurement (`two_clients.rs:7,10,38`)", and "ADR-2159 still requires E8 on the headset before activation."
- Failure: Production ships two-connection behaviour (id routing, ack sequences, per-volume packing) whose decision record is inactive and contradicts the code, and whose only evidence is loopback with a simulated main thread — a scene-level failure under real frame load would be invisible to every existing gate, and an engineer reading the ADR would conclude the feature isn't there.
- Confidence: high on the contradiction; medium, inferred, on the size of the unmeasured risk.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift).

### F-14 — Pin state is dead on both clients after ADR-2172 F3: sets with readers and UI but no writer, plus comments claiming the writer exists
- Topics: VC-53.6 (and VC-52.7)
- Evidence: "no path adds to either set (`backend_connection.gd:49,50,51`)" — "Unpin All, the HUD count and connection teardown still iterate sets that nothing fills"; "DRIFT: comment still says drag-end adds (`graph_scene.gd:951,952`)" while `_end_grab` records nothing (`:3452`). Desktop: "`pinnedNodeIds` still read (`graph.worker.ts:31`) … no writer, proxy pin calls are deprecated no-ops (`graphWorkerProxy.ts:410,416`)". Server-side explicit pins still exist (`hold_registry.rs:117-121`); it is the clients that can no longer create them (ADR-2172 F5 follow-up pending).
- Failure: A user "pins" a node in the headset (or desktop) and believes it anchored; nothing reaches the server, the pin silently releases on refetch or reconnect, the HUD reports zero pins forever, and maintainers are misled by comments asserting the writer exists.
- Confidence: high (stated for both clients).
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt and Drift; F5 recorded as follow-up).

### F-15 — Code→concept links are spoofable from string literals and carry no repository provenance
- Topics: VC-45.1, VC-45.2 (and VC-45.3)
- Evidence: The scanner is line-oriented — "Comment-looking line inside a Rust raw string also enters this branch (`annotations.rs:125,159`)" and the probe "observes the example become a bound annotation and two target links"; marked Tension: "The contract describes a comment annotation, while the recogniser accepts comment-shaped string content." The join then emits records with "Repository empty and revision None (`link_index.rs:281,284`)" even though the snapshot is labelled with repository and revision, because "the stream omits these provenance values and the join deliberately supplies empty defaults."
- Failure: Any dependency or generated file embeds annotation-shaped strings — deliberately or accidentally — and the room presents forged code→knowledge edges as authored evidence; because every record also carries empty repository/revision, a displayed link cannot be traced to any revision or refuted, so the poisoning is unauditable from the product itself.
- Confidence: high on the mechanics (executed against the real scanner and join module); medium, inferred, on adversarial exploitation in practice.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes for the scanner (Tension); the empty provenance is stated in the business note without a marker.

## Not judgeable from this material

1. **Which compose manifest production actually runs, and the `.env` files each service loads.** F-03, F-05 and F-06 all hinge on whether `docker-compose.unified.yml` (dev

## visionclaw#3 / premortem

# Post-mortem: production incident twelve months out

Written from the estate's own diagrammed record. Every claim below is anchored to a citation the material itself gives; where I reason past the record I say "inferred". Nothing here is asserted from unstated ordering or concurrency — where a diagram does not state an order, I do not treat it as one.

---

### R-1 — Payment replay across restart/replica, and non-idempotent fresh-signature retries, in the NIP-98 payment routes
- Topics: VC-55, VC-33.2, VC-33.3
- Chain of events: (1) The payment routes go live ("source verification, awaiting deployment"). (2) A client signs a NIP-98 token and a resource fee is debited ("resource fee: durable ledger debit", `src/handlers/pay_handler.rs:303`). (3) The server process restarts — deploy, crash, OOM — or a second replica behind a balancer serves the next request. (4) The single-use replay cache is process-local, so the spent event id is unknown to the new process/replica; the same `Authorization: Nostr <b64>` header is replayed. (5) If the replay lands inside the token freshness window (length unstated anywhere in the material), verification passes, the claim succeeds, and the ledger is debited a second time. Independently: the response is lost after a successful charge; "successful authentication spends it even if the operation fails" and "a freshly signed retry can still repeat an operation", so the client's legitimate retry double-charges with no idempotency key anywhere in the cited path.
- Evidence: "claim signed id under mutex, bounded process-local cache" `src/utils/nip98.rs:227`, `:295`; "the cache does not span restarts or replicas"; "A freshly signed retry can still repeat an operation"; "this is source verification, awaiting deployment" (VC-55, all sections). The same ADR-2002 single-use property is what every socket trusts (VC-33.2 note; VC-35.4).
- Warning signs visible today: VC-55's own business note states both limits verbatim; the routes were verified only in-source, never under a restart or multi-replica deployment.
- Confidence: high that both defects exist as described; the incident sequence is inferred (requires deployment plus a restart/replica or a lost response).
- Severity: high

### R-2 — Fail-open authentication posture ships with the compose file: public reads, unauthenticated-key-is-Editor, and an armed dev-bypass default that travels through `.env`
- Topics: VC-33.6, VC-36.15, VC-39.2
- Chain of events: (1) The unified compose — which "serves the whole estate as one scene" — is used to stand up the estate at a client site. (2) `docker-compose.unified.yml:132` sets `RBAC_PUBLIC_READS=1`, so the knowledge graph is readable without any credential (the Rust default is fail-closed; `src/middleware/rbac_gate.rs:141` `unwrap_or(false)`). (3) Any holder of any genuine Nostr key authenticates and resolves to Editor via `UserRole::default_authenticated()` (`src/models/rbac.rs:68-70`, `src/services/role_store.rs:188,196-198`) — graph-write access. (4) In parallel, `VISIONCLAW_DEV_MODE: "${VISIONCLAW_DEV_MODE:-1}"` (`docker-compose.unified.yml:116`) supplies the bypass var unasked; both services load `env_file .env`, and if the var reaches a dev-auth-feature build, `dev_full_bypass_active()` grants "every request... as DEV_MODE_PUBKEY... peer-agnostic: no NIP-98, no token, no peer check" (`src/utils/auth.rs:96`, `:203-218`). (5) Client data is read by strangers and/or written by an unknown Editor.
- Evidence: "compose ships public reads open and unassigned-pubkey-is-Editor by default, while the Rust struct-level default is fail-closed — two different postures" (VC-33.6 DIVERGENCE note); "the var must never sit in a .env shared with prod — sharper now that the compose default supplies it unasked" (`docker-compose.unified.yml:113-115`, VC-36.15); release builds refuse to boot with the var (`project/src/main.rs:109,136,161`) but dev/dev-auth builds do not.
- Warning signs visible today: the governing docs cite compose line numbers that have drifted repeatedly and "were never re-anchored" (VC-33.6 DOC-DRIFT), so a reviewer reading the baseline cannot see the current posture; ADR-2108 deliberately flipped the dev default to armed.
- Confidence: high that the divergence and armed defaults exist (stated); that a client deployment inherits them is inferred.
- Severity: high

### R-3 — An approved ontology amendment silently replaces an "immutable" resource, corrupting corpus identity
- Topics: VC-41.4, VC-41.1, VC-41.2
- Chain of events: (1) A contributor amends an existing page whose proposed version names a replacement resource (`vault/src/propose.rs:468`). (2) Assessment runs before/after with "no continuity comparison" (`vault/src/propose.rs:557,558,568`); the proposal subject stays the original resource IRI (`propose.rs:663,665`). (3) The reviewer approves what is presented as an amendment to a known resource. (4) The writer checks the *current* resource against the proposal IRI — which passes, because the on-disk page still carries its original identity pre-apply (`write/apply.rs:229,230`). (5) The patch is applied, the new page parsed, introduced errors checked, and persisted (`apply.rs:242,258,282,395`) — with "no after-resource equality check". (6) The persisted page now answers to a different resource; the corpus format's immutable-resource invariant is broken, and every persisted URN/DID/ACL entry keyed on the old identity (VC-33.9 taxonomy, lines 178-179) points at a swapped entity.
- Evidence: "DRIFT: fixture page now has replacement resource / no after-resource equality check / write/apply.rs:280,282"; the audit executed the real signed producer output through the real CLI writer and observed the swap (VC-41.4).
- Warning signs visible today: VC-41.3 already shows producer and consumer disagreeing on proposal identity; the audit's own caveat that it "does not execute live approval or attestation" means the approval gate has never been tested against this writer behaviour.
- Confidence: high that the missing check is real (it was reproduced on fixtures); that a resource-changing amendment occurs in ordinary use, and that approval then completes, is inferred.
- Severity: high

### F-01 — Codescape runs OpenAccess with a LAN-published path while holding read-only mounts of ten member repositories
- Topics: VC-39.7, VC-39.2, VC-43.1
- Evidence: "OPEN until P6: the main port runs OpenAccess, reported honestly as auth open, while port 3001 /codescape/ is LAN-published in dev by owner decision" — `codescape-server/src/auth.rs:120-124`, `src/routes/mod.rs:71`, `nginx.dev.conf:63-65`; the container carries "read-only views of the estate scene and ten member repositories" `docker-compose.unified.yml:538-619`.
- Failure: anywhere the 3001 path or port 4100 is reachable past the operator's machine, an unauthenticated peer browses the code graph of ten client/member repositories and follows positions, events and voice.
- Confidence: high (posture stated; exposure beyond the owner's machine inferred from "LAN-published").
- Marked by authors: yes (Open)
- Severity: high

### F-02 — Jev resolves an explicit remote endpoint before the locality gate, and the shipped compose grants cloud permission
- Topics: VC-42.2, VC-42.3, VC-39.7 (J1), VC-39.2
- Evidence: "An explicit remote endpoint can resolve while every local/cloud permission flag is false" — `voice/jev.rs:190-192`; "the local-only promise in voice/jev.rs:11... do not cover the explicit-endpoint return"; compose grants cloud routing (`docker-compose.unified.yml:505`) and "ALLOW_CLOUD=1 sends the utterance via the proxy" (`agentbox.toml:892`); the acceptance threshold is inclusive of 1.0 (`voice/jev.rs:182,409`).
- Failure: spoken transcripts from a shared code session leave the LAN by configuration, without the documented local-only contract; a client's overheard discussion is exfiltrated to a remote backend.
- Confidence: high on the resolution order and compose state (stated); the exfiltration harm is inferred.
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift, Tension)
- Severity: high

### F-03 — The passkey-derived Nostr private key appears to live in sessionStorage, contradicting the "in-memory only" claim
- Topics: VC-33.5, VC-33.9, VC-33.4
- Evidence: logout removes `nostr_privkey, nostr_passkey_pubkey, nostr_prf` from sessionStorage (`nostrAuthService.ts:460-464`, VC-33.5) — cleanup implies storage; but the identity table declares `LocalPrivateKeyMaterial` "storage in-memory only, never sessionStorage" (`nostrAuthService.ts:14,200`, VC-33.9).
- Failure: if the key sits in sessionStorage during a passkey session, one XSS reads `nostr_privkey` and holds the user's Nostr identity — per-request NIP-98 signing, presence, pod access — with no server-side revocation path stated anywhere.
- Confidence: medium — the contradiction is on the record; which claim holds at the incident commit is unverified (exposure inferred).
- Marked by authors: no
- Severity: high

- Evidence: relay "checks held room and avatar only" `xr_presence.rs:668`, poses carry removed local ids to both recipients `:673,677`; `send_all` uses `try_send` for every message kind and "full queue loses announcement / only successful sends counted" `xr_presence.rs:521,524`; the client falls back to a decimal local id when the mapping is missing (`xr-client/rust/src/presence.rs:633,636`); baseline drift says presence carries no avatars (`BASELINE-codescape.md:109`).

## visiongraph / critical

### F-01 — The final credential scan cannot read gzip-only content, so secrets can deploy to the public site
- Topics: VG-03.6
- Evidence: The pipeline compresses page JSON, Markdown and ontology JSON and "raw filenames replaced by gzip paths" (`publish.yml:343`, `publish.yml:317`), then runs "credential self-test then grep-based final scan" with the stated gap "compressed-only content is not scanned as text" (`check-secrets.sh:150`, `publish.yml:375`), then deploys (`publish.yml:526`). The scan therefore runs after the substitution it cannot see through.
- Failure: A secret committed into a page or ontology artefact whose raw form is deleted at the gzip step passes the last security gate and is published to public gh-pages.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DEBT, VG-03.6 SCAN node)

### F-02 — VisionClaw's inclusion predicate (`public OR owl_class`) can ship non-public pages in the public pod release
- Topics: VG-05.1, VG-04.1, VG-04.3
- Evidence: VisionClaw includes a page in its graph when "public OR a valid owl_class value" (`vault/mod.rs:162`), while the pod release is built from VisionClaw's local `vault` crate (`ontology-publish.yml:164`) and packed into a rolling `ontology-latest` release (`ontology-publish.yml:177`, `pack-pod-resources.py:116`). Nothing in the material shows a public-only filter between that build and packing, and the ADR requiring these policy differences to be "intentional and tested" is "proposed, partial, inactive" (`ADR-VG-001-publication-policy-boundaries.md:36`).
- Failure: A knowledge page under review with `public` unset but an `owl_class` value is excluded from the website by ontomark's boolean-only predicate (`input/page.rs:269`) yet included in VisionClaw's build output, packed and released publicly — private material leaves the vault on a green pipeline.
- Confidence: medium — inferred; the predicate and build path are stated, the absence of a pack-time public filter is not shown.
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no (VG-05.2 marks the acceptance contract inactive, but not this consequence)

### F-03 — The manifest declares working content excluded; the workflow publishes it, and triggers omit the inputs it consumes
- Topics: VG-01.5, VG-03.1, VG-03.6
- Evidence: `vault.toml:143-152` says the publish section is knowledge-only with working excluded; defaults are knowledge/`public_only=true` (`vault.toml:74-80`); the active workflow passes `--vault all` and "stages public working content too" (`publish.yml:155-181`). Separately, "push filters omit working pages and root assets despite consuming them" (`publish.yml:24`, `publish.yml:158`, `publish.yml:195`).
- Failure: A curator sets `public: true` on a working draft; the next push touching any knowledge file publishes that draft to the public site with no working-specific review trigger. Inversely, a fix made only in `working/` never triggers a run, so the live site serves stale working prose indefinitely.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (TENSION, VG-01.5 ACTUAL node; Debt, VG-03.6 footer)

### F-04 — Pod freshness is keyed on the consumer's build SHA, so a new corpus with an unchanged consumer commit is skipped forever
- Topics: VG-04.3, VG-04.1
- Evidence: "Puller compares stored buildSha only" (`ontology_pull.rs:312`); "same build SHA skips new source SHA before downloading checksum manifest"; the reproduced tests "keep source A active when source B arrives with the same build SHA" (`ontology_pull.rs:312`, `pack-pod-resources.py:117`). The corpus-sync event does deliver `source_sha` (`publish.yml:536`), but it is recorded in the index, not compared.
- Failure: The corpus is corrected (e.g., a factual or privacy fix) but the VisionClaw repo hasn't changed; `GITHUB_SHA` is identical, the puller skips before even fetching the checksum manifest, and the server serves the previous generation indefinitely with no error or staleness signal.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (DEBT, VG-04.3 SKIP node)

### F-05 — The public site and the pod are built by two different compilers under one shared source identity
- Topics: VG-04.1, VG-04.3, VG-05.1
- Evidence: "the workflow still builds VisionClaw's `vault`, while the site uses the released `ontomark`" (`ontology-publish.yml:164` vs `publish.yml:48`); the site build is all-vault public (`publish.yml:175`) while the pod build is knowledge-only; the only identity crossing the boundary is the event's source SHA (`publish.yml:536`) — "the event carries the source commit, not the assembled site digest" (VG-03.1).
- Failure: The same source commit yields different page sets, predicates and semantics on the site versus the server; a correctness or privacy fix shipped in ontomark never reaches the pod path, and nothing detects the divergence because both are labelled with the same source SHA.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no (stated as fact in VG-04.1 without a marker)

### F-06 — No manifest describes the final deployed site, and a receipt hash mismatch has already been observed at that boundary
- Topics: VG-05.3, VG-03.6
- Evidence: The compiler emits its generation markers (`generation.rs:90`, `build/mod.rs:977`, `build/mod.rs:1008`), then the workflow overlays static files (`publish.yml:261`), compresses (`publish.yml:358`) and adds the SPA and notices (`publish.yml:441`); "these checks do not regenerate a final-site manifest" (VG-03.6 DEPLOY node), and "the earlier public-receipt audit found one static-context hash mismatch at that boundary" (VG-05.3, citing `build/mod.rs:1015`, `publish.yml:261`).
- Failure: Deployed bytes have already diverged once from a recorded receipt without halting; any after-the-fact verification against the compiler marker validates the wrong artefact, so silent corruption or tampering between compiler emission and deploy is undetectable.
- Confidence: high (facts); the mismatch's cause is unestablished in the material
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (OPEN, VG-05.3); the observed mismatch itself is unmarked

### F-07 — Nothing in the chain acknowledges activation; a dispatch success masks total downstream failure
- Topics: VG-04.1, VG-03.1, VG-01.4
- Evidence: "request delivery is not downstream completion" (`publish.yml:536`); "a successful dispatch is not an activation acknowledgement" (`ontology_pull.rs:40`, `ontology-publish.yml:360`); the pull job's wiring in source "does not prove a running deployment's configuration" (`src/main.rs:917`); consumer activation is explicitly uncertified (`PUBLICATION-contract.md:20-26`).
- Failure: The pod release fails verification, or the server's pull job is disabled/misconfigured; the site publisher reports green and nobody learns the server is serving the previous generation until users report it.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (OPEN, VG-01.4) for the acceptance gap; the dispatch-is-not-activation fact itself carries no marker

### F-08 — Explorer caches have no generation identity, so retractions and corrections never reach open sessions
- Topics: VG-06.6
- Evidence: The search index is "cached in module" on first success and page JSON/Markdown responses get module caches (`pageService.ts:61`, `pageService.ts:113`); "caches have no generation identity — long-lived session can retain old prose" (VG-06.6).
- Failure: After a retraction or correction republishes the corpus, any reader with the tab open keeps resolving and displaying the removed prose indefinitely; there is no client-side version comparison to invalidate it.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DEBT, VG-06.6 G node)

### F-09 — An index outage is reported to users as a content fact ("concept exists" stub)
- Topics: VG-06.6
- Evidence: "unavailable or unmatched index returns concept-exists stub without a page lookup" (`pageService.ts:276`, with the module-level index cache at `pageService.ts:61`, `pageService.ts:113`).
- Failure: A transient CDN or network failure fetching the search index makes the UI assert a concept exists (or render stub content) instead of surfacing an error; users and support conclude pages were deleted or content is authoritative during what is actually an outage.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DEBT, VG-06.6 F node)

### F-10 — Apply/promote execute whatever `vault` binary is first on PATH, with no digest binding staged output to review
- Topics: VG-07.4
- Evidence: "CLI dependency resolved through PATH" and "no staged digest or review receipt binding" (VG-07.4 footer; `main.rs:153`, `main.rs:500`, `main.rs:522`); for promote, "successful exit is the promotion result — no post-write read or JSON outcome check" (`main.rs:543`).
- Failure: On a machine with a different or older `vault` on PATH, the guarded expectations (`--expect docs=1`) are interpreted by whatever binary was found, so writes land with different semantics; and because nothing binds staged headers to the reviewed catalogue, staged content cannot be proven to be what was reviewed.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DEBT, VG-07.4 footer)

### F-11 — The licence-admission register exists only on paper; the importer computes a digest and never compares it
- Topics: VG-07.1, VG-07.3
- Evidence: "inventory computes a digest but accepts no expected digest or admission register" (`main.rs:311`, `main.rs:370`), while ADR-VG-005 defines a reviewed register of version, digest and notice (`ADR-VG-005-licensed-source-admission.md:23`); staged headers copy source values from the catalogue (`main.rs:473`) regardless.
- Failure: An operator runs `stage` against an unreviewed or wrong-version RDF snapshot; no machine check objects, and the generated headers wear provenance metadata asserting reviewed sources — unlicensed or superseded third-party content enters the vault with false attribution.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no (stated as a plain limitation in VG-07.1's prose and LIMIT node, no marker)
