### F-13 — A failed stage run leaves a partial header set, refuses rerun, and offers no rollback
- Topics: VG-07.3, VG-07.4
- Evidence: "later error leaves earlier files — no directory rollback" (`main.rs:466-473`) while stage "refuse[s] an existing output directory" (`main.rs:102`, `main.rs:388`); apply then trusts on-disk `.md` counts (`main.rs:153`).
- Failure: Stage aborts at catalogue entry N after writing N−1 headers; rerun is blocked, so an operator must manually delete the partial tree — and can either mistakenly run apply against the partial set or delete the wrong directory.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no (VG-07.3 FAIL node carries no marker)

### F-14 — The operator-facing documentation describes a different system than the one that runs
- Topics: VG-01.5, VG-01.1, VG-01.3, VG-06.3, VG-05.4
- Evidence: README says v0.2.0, rules say v0.5.0, the workflow pins ontomark v0.7.0 by SHA-256 (`README.md:66`, `CLAUDE.md:23`, `publish.yml:48-51`); README says `knowledge/index.md` is committed while the manifest calls it a build artefact (`README.md:18`, `vault.toml:30-31`); README describes typed `true` only while `ExplicitPublish` also accepts the string `"true"` (`README.md:143-145`, `explicit.ts:23-25`); CAPABILITIES claims focus does not simulate while the host simulates domain and focus (`CAPABILITIES.md:50`, `GraphCanvas.tsx:44`); CLAUDE.md still claims React Three Fiber and historical FPS (`CLAUDE.md:8`); the publication contract references the retired `vault` compiler (`PUBLICATION-contract.md:3`).
- Failure: During an incident, on-call follows README/CLAUDE.md and acts on the wrong compiler version, wrong renderer stack and — most dangerously — the belief that working content cannot publish (F-03), converting the manifest/workflow override into an unreviewed publication.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (DRIFT and TENSION markers across VG-01.1, VG-01.5, VG-06.3)

### F-15 — Two diverging explorer trees exist and the material cannot say which revision the public site serves
- Topics: VG-06.1, VG-06.4, VG-06.5
- Evidence: The configured host is `publishing-tools/WasmVOWL/modern` with vowl-wasm 0.2.2 (`publishing-tools/WasmVOWL/modern/package.json:24`) deploying to knowledgeGraph gh-pages (`publish.yml:526`), while a retained `knowledgeGraph/explorer` pins vowl-wasm 0.1.1 with React Three Fiber (`knowledgeGraph/explorer/modern/package.json:20`); the two copies carry different mesh tables (nine vs seven repos, `mesh.ts:15`/`mesh.ts:22` vs sibling `mesh.ts:38`) and different domain counts (nine vs six, `scope.ts:75` vs sibling `scope.ts:73`); "It does not identify the revision currently served by the public site" (VG-06.1).
- Failure: During a live-site incident an engineer patches `knowledgeGraph/explorer` — the tree inside the deployment repo — and the fix never ships; the retained copy's older dependency set is also one mistaken build away from deployment.
- Confidence: high (facts stated; the mispatch scenario is inferred)
- Severity: low
- Marked by authors: no

## Not judgeable from this material

1. Whether anything between VisionClaw's `vault` build and `pack-pod-resources.py` enforces a public-only filter — decisive for whether F-02 is a real leak or a defence-in-depth gap.
2. Whether the Quartz staging tree can contain pages the compiler did not classify as public (e.g., via the static-context overlay or the notes route) — decisive for whether the string-`true` acceptance in `explicit.ts:23-25` is reachable or purely a latent mismatch.
3. The VisionClaw workflow's checkout semantics and concurrency: whether `ontology-publish.yml` checks out the event's `source_sha` or the branch head, and whether that workflow is serialized — decides whether site and pod can also diverge by racing events.
4. The grep patterns and path scope of `check-secrets.sh`, and which deployed artefacts are gzip-only in practice — decides F-01's exploitability (whether any real file class escapes the scan).
5. Live deployment state: which explorer revision the public site actually serves, and whether the production VisionClaw server runs the periodic pull at all (`src/main.rs:917` shows wiring, not deployment configuration).

## visiongraph / premortem

# Post-mortem: client-loss incident (data / custody failure)

Scope note: this document was reconstructed from the estate maps frozen at the cited commits (`visiongraph 556a015…`, `visionclaw 75b7241…`, `ontomark 0caae5d…`). Every claim below is checkable at the given path:line. Where I reason past what the maps state, I mark it inferred. No incident timeline beyond these defects is asserted.

---

## Root causes

### R-1 — Pod serves a stale corpus because freshness is keyed to the consumer's build SHA, not the source SHA
- Topics: VG-04.1, VG-04.3
- Chain of events:
  1. Curators publish a corrected ontology from a new visionGraph source commit; the site deploys and the workflow sends `corpus-sync` carrying `source_repo` and `source_sha` (`publish.yml:536`).
  2. VisionClaw's workflow resolves the source checkout, builds via its local `vault` crate, packs Turtle/JSON-LD/context/index, and records both `sourceSha` and `buildSha`, where `buildSha` comes from VisionClaw's `GITHUB_SHA` (`ontology-publish.yml:150–177`; `pack-pod-resources.py:116`).
  3. No VisionClaw code changed, so the new release carries the same `buildSha` the pod already stored.
  4. On server boot or periodic pull, the loader compares the stored `buildSha` only; on a match it skips before downloading even the checksum manifest (`ontology_pull.rs:205`, `ontology_pull.rs:312`, `ontology_pull.rs:320`).
  5. The pod keeps serving generation A while the public site shows generation B; nothing downstream compares `sourceSha`, and dispatch success was never an activation acknowledgement (`ontology_pull.rs:40`; `ontology-publish.yml:360`).
  6. The client acts on superseded knowledge from the pod; the divergence surfaces only on cross-checking against the site.
- Evidence: VG-04.3 SKIP node — "DEBT: same build SHA skips new source SHA before downloading checksum manifest" (`ontology_pull.rs:312`); "the current-source tests keep source A active when source B arrives with the same build SHA" (`pack-pod-resources.py:117`, `ontology_pull.rs:312`); buildSha provenance (`pack-pod-resources.py:116`); "request delivery is not downstream completion" (`publish.yml:536`).
- Warning signs visible today: the mapped tests already reproduce the exact stale-source scenario; `buildSha` is a consumer-CI commit id, structurally unrelated to corpus freshness, yet it alone controls the skip.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high

### R-2 — Working-vault pages reach the public site although the manifest declares working excluded
- Topics: VG-01.5, VG-01.3, VG-07.3
- Chain of events:
  1. `vault.toml` declares the working role `publishable=false` (`vault.toml:41-50`) and the publish section knowledge-only with working excluded (`vault.toml:143-152`) — the documented custody boundary.
  2. The active workflow contradicts it: it runs `--vault all` and stages public working content (`publish.yml:155-181`).
  3. A curator, relying on the manifest, keeps client-sensitive material in `working/` with `public: true` set (copied frontmatter, or an importer-written header — `space-corpus/src/main.rs:477` writes `public` from the catalogue), believing role-level exclusion protects it.
  4. The page is staged in the all-vault public build (`publish.yml:175`), Quartz's explicit filter evaluates it (`explicit.ts:23-25`), and it deploys to the public `knowledgeGraph` gh-pages (`publish.yml:526`).
  5. Detection fails: the final credential scan does not read compressed-only content (`check-secrets.sh:150`) and no final-site manifest is regenerated (`publish.yml:526`).
- Evidence: VG-01.5 ACTUAL (TENSION) — manifest declarations vs `publish.yml:155-181`; VG-03.1 BUILD — "All-vault public build and content staging"; VG-07.3 PUBLIC — "public comes from catalogue, default false, not forced private" (`main.rs:477`) against a README describing private staging (`space-corpus/README.md:14`).
- Warning signs visible today: the manifest/workflow contradiction is flagged as TENSION in the map; the importer can already emit publication-eligible headers contrary to its own README.
- Confidence: high — the publication route (public working content staged and deployed) is fully stated; only the specific mis-flagged page is inferred.
- Severity: high

### R-3 — Deployed bytes are transformed after the compiler's manifest, with no final-site integrity receipt
- Topics: VG-03.6, VG-05.3
- Chain of events:
  1. The compiler emits its generation marker with content/ontology digests and artefact hashes (`generation.rs:90`; `build/mod.rs:977–1008`).
  2. The workflow then overlays static contexts into site-data (`publish.yml:261`), copies into www with a stage-local hash check (`publish.yml:317`), gzips page JSON, Markdown and ontology JSON (`publish.yml:343`), and adds SPA shell, routes and notices (`publish.yml:421–490`).
  3. Each receipt covers only its own stage; the layout check compares path presence and counts (`check-urls.sh:115`), the secret scan skips compressed-only text (`check-secrets.sh:150`), and the deploy step regenerates no final-site manifest (`publish.yml:526`).
  4. A byte-level alteration or injection at or after the overlay/compression boundary therefore ships undetected.
  5. Precedent already exists: the earlier public-receipt audit observed one static-context hash mismatch at exactly this boundary (`build/mod.rs:1015`; `publish.yml:261`).
  6. The client detects served bytes matching no recorded digest; the operator cannot prove what was deployed.
- Evidence: VG-05.3 GAP — "OPEN: final bytes and loaded consumers need their own generation binding"; VG-05.3 What-it-shows — the observed static-context hash mismatch; VG-03.6 — "the retained KG-P probes reproduce compression, count-substitution and final-size gaps at this same source revision".
- Warning signs visible today: the observed-but-unexplained hash mismatch; the OPEN acceptance gap; the reproduced probe gaps at the mapped revision.
- Confidence: medium — the detection gaps and the prior mismatch are stated facts; that this mechanism produced the incident is inferred.
- Severity: high

---

## Further latent defects

### F-01 — Downstream publish predicate accepts the string "true", wider than the documented typed flag
- Topics: VG-01.3, VG-05.1
- Evidence: ExplicitPublish accepts boolean true OR the exact string "true" (`explicit.ts:23-25`); the README describes typed true only (`visionGraph/README.md:143-145`); the compiler grants publication only on YAML boolean true (`input/page.rs:269`).
- Failure: any content reaching Quartz staging with `public: "true"` as a string publishes; the site's second line of defence against malformed visibility flags does not exist.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Tension)

### F-02 — Final credential scan cannot read compressed-only content
- Topics: VG-03.6
- Evidence: the pipeline gzips page JSON, Markdown and ontology JSON (`publish.yml:343`) and the final scan is grep-based — "DEBT: compressed-only content is not scanned as text" (`check-secrets.sh:150`).
- Failure: a secret present only in compressed artefacts is deployed to the public gh-pages site undetected.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt)

### F-03 — Notes completeness is a count comparison; count substitution is already reproduced
- Topics: VG-03.6
- Evidence: "Layout checks raw-or-gzip path presence; notes completeness is a count comparison" (`check-urls.sh:115`); the deploy step "does not regenerate a final-site manifest" (`publish.yml:526`); the probes "reproduce compression, count-substitution and final-size gaps at this same source revision".
- Failure: a notes page is missing or replaced by another file of equal count; checks pass; an incomplete or substituted notes set ships publicly.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: no (named as a reproduced probe, but carries no Tension/Debt/Drift/Open label)

### F-04 — Domain gates check tier file existence, not decodable bytes
- Topics: VG-01.7, VG-03.5
- Evidence: "DEBT: tier existence is checked, bytes are not decoded; empty and invalid tiers pass scoped probes" (`check-space-domains.mjs:25`; `check-supply-chain-domain.mjs:36`); "tier path existence is not binary validation" (`publish.yml:271` area, VG-03.5).
- Failure: a truncated or corrupt binary tier passes the gates and deploys; the explorer's decode fails in readers' browsers for that domain.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt)

### F-05 — Supply-chain gate skips identity checks when the catalogue is absent
- Topics: VG-01.7, VG-03.5
- Evidence: "DEBT: absent supply catalogue exits successfully; no requirement that all 15 category IDs exist" (`check-supply-chain-domain.mjs:39`, `:42-54`); the scripts are "narrower than their names and the earlier diagram's fifteen-category guarantee" (`check-supply-chain-domain.mjs:39`).
- Failure: a release omits the catalogue; the gate passes silently; an incomplete or mis-identified supply-chain domain publishes.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt)

### F-06 — Push filters omit working pages and root assets the publisher consumes
- Topics: VG-03.1
- Evidence: "Debt: push filters omit working pages and root assets despite consuming them; matching knowledge or workflow changes and manual runs can still publish those inputs" (`publish.yml:24`, `:158`, `:195`).
- Failure: a correction made only in `working/` or to root assets is not published until an unrelated trigger fires; meanwhile clients act on superseded public content, and the eventual manual run publishes an unreviewed batch at once.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt)

### F-07 — Source-validation warnings are nonfatal
- Topics: VG-03.5
- Evidence: "validate --vault all; nonzero exit refuses the job, warnings remain nonfatal" (`publish.yml:127-130`); the same job runs source validation and collision lint (`publish.yml:88` area).
- Failure: any warning-class finding — including, if collisions are reported as warnings, an identity collision — ships in a release because only a nonzero exit blocks.
- Confidence: high on the stated behaviour; which findings are warning-class is not stated.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-08 — Explorer caches have no generation identity
- Topics: VG-06.6
- Evidence: "DEBT: caches have no generation identity; long-lived session can retain old prose" (`pageService.ts:61`, `:113`); successful index and page responses are cached in-module (`pageService.ts:169`, `:229`).
- Failure: after a republish — including a retraction — open reader sessions keep serving previously fetched page JSON and Markdown indefinitely.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium (high if the retained page was retracted for privacy — inferred escalation)
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt)

### F-09 — Overview scope validation checks ceilings, not structural validity
- Topics: VG-06.5
- Evidence: "DEBT: count ceilings do not establish finite integer counts, array lengths or valid overview edge endpoints" (`scopeStore.ts:512`; `types/scope.ts:153`); "Passing domain presence gates does not establish a structurally valid graph".
- Failure: malformed overview data (non-finite count, out-of-range edge endpoint) passes `assertScope`; the renderer crashes or draws edges to nonexistent nodes.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt)

### F-10 — Importer stage honours catalogue public flags despite a private-staging README
- Topics: VG-07.3
- Evidence: "public comes from catalogue, default false, not forced private" (`space-corpus/src/main.rs:477`); "Drift: the README describes private staging, but the implementation consumes the catalogue visibility flag; no private-only invariant is enforced by `stage`" (`space-corpus/README.md:14`, `main.rs:477`).
- Failure: a catalogue entry wrongly marked `public: true` produces headers that are immediately publication-eligible; combined with the all-vault staging route (R-2), unreviewed source-derived pages reach the public site.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: high
- Marked by authors: yes (Drift)

### F-11 — Promotion trusts exit codes and a PATH-resolved executable, with no digest binding
- Topics: VG-07.4
- Evidence: "Successful exit is the promotion result; no post-write read or JSON outcome check" (`main.rs:528`); "DEBT: no staged digest or review receipt binding; CLI dependency resolved through PATH" (`main.rs:210`, VG-07.4 NOTE).
- Failure: a `vault` binary earlier on PATH — different version or altered — performs the writes that set `public=true`; or a semantically failed edit exits 0 and the batch proceeds unverified, leaving the reviewed-source-to-published-page chain unproven.
- Confidence: high on the stated facts; the altered-binary escalation is inferred.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: yes (Debt)

### F-12 — Site and pod corpora are built by different compilers from the same source
- Topics: VG-04.1
- Evidence: "the workflow still builds VisionClaw's `vault`, while the site uses the released `ontomark`" (`ontology-publish.yml:164`; `publish.yml:48`).
- Failure: the same source commit yields different corpus bytes or semantics on the two surfaces; a client reading both gets inconsistent answers, and no divergence check flags it.
- Confidence: high on the divergent compilers; client-visible inconsistency is inferred.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-13 — No activation acknowledgement exists for corpus-sync dispatch
- Topics: VG-03.1, VG-04.1
- Evidence: "request delivery is not downstream completion" (`publish.yml:536`); "a successful dispatch is not an activation acknowledgement" (`ontology_pull.rs:40`; `ontology-publish.yml:360`); pull is wired to server boot and periodic timing (`src/main.rs:917`; `ontology_pull.rs:295`, `:389`) and "this source map does not prove a running deployment's configuration".
- Failure: dispatch succeeds while a mis-configured or stopped deployment never pulls; staleness persists with no alert, because nothing reports the loaded generation back.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-14 — Audit and competency checks verify declared intent, not the emitted graph
- Topics: VG-07.5
- Evidence: audit counts catalogue-declared targets and "reused-page edge presence is not compared" (`main.rs:570–600`); competency traverses only is-a and "empty case lists also pass" (`main.rs:249–303`); the emitted OWL reasoner is not run (`main.rs:273`).
- Failure: a release passes audit and competency while actual page edges diverge from the catalogue or contain unsatisfiable classes; incorrect relations publish.
- Confidence: high
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no

### F-15 — Licence admission is recorded on paper but not enforced by the toolchain
- Topics: VG-07.1
- Evidence: "inventory computes a digest but accepts no expected digest or admission register" (`space-corpus/src/main.rs:311`); the admission decision lives in a reviewed register (`docs/adr/ADR-VG-005-licensed-source-admission.md:23`) with no machine binding at any later gate (VG-07.4 NOTE: no staged digest or review receipt binding).
- Failure: an unadmitted or unlicensed RDF snapshot flows through staging and promotion into the public corpus; the breach is discoverable only by manual audit, not by any pipeline check.
- Confidence: high on the absent checks; the unadmitted-input occurrence is inferred.
- Severity: medium
- Marked by authors: no
